---

## 1. Requirements summary and decision ledger

The user asked for a Go port of mitmproxy with the non-overlapping Python and Rust feature sets scoped by explicit choice, any third-party module allowed, and package choices decided by the user rather than inferred. The answers below are the contract for this plan.

### 1.1 Scope decisions (user answers, round A)

| Area | Decision | Deferred (follow-up, not in this plan's phases) |
|---|---|---|
| Protocols | HTTP/1.1 + TLS interception (baseline), **HTTP/2, WebSocket, HTTP/3 + QUIC, raw TCP, raw UDP, DNS layer, DTLS** (`reverse:dtls://` via `github.com/pion/dtls/v3`; added to scope by the user after the review loops) | — |
| Proxy modes | regular, reverse, upstream (baseline), **transparent, socks5, dns** | — |
| Rust-side (packet-level) | **WireGuard mode**, **local mode on macOS, Windows and Linux** (by reusing upstream's prebuilt redirectors), **TUN mode (Linux)** (Linux local and TUN added to scope by the user after the review loops) | `rs:src/certificates/` (macOS keychain, unused by Python) |
| Frontends and extras | mitmdump CLI (baseline), **mitmweb backend with the upstream React build embedded, console TUI, Rust contentviews (protobuf/gRPC/msgpack/hex) + syntax highlighting, process_info** | — |

### 1.2 Architecture and compatibility decisions (round B, plus the two review-loop answers)

| Topic | Decision |
|---|---|
| Core model | **Hybrid**: pure parsers are sans-I/O and reuse upstream test vectors; connection orchestration is goroutine-per-connection over `net.Conn`; hooks run synchronously on the owning stream goroutine inside a single **hook dispatch domain** (§3.1). |
| HTTP fidelity | **Full**: own HTTP/1 parser ported from `py:mitmproxy/net/http/http1/read.py` and `assemble.py` (raw header case/order, obs-fold); HTTP/2 as two independent h2 endpoints with own HEADERS/CONTINUATION assembly on `xnet:http2.Framer` + own `hpack` decoders/encoders (header order and pseudo-header order preserved; `normalize_outbound_headers` default **true** as upstream, `validate_inbound_headers` honoured); HTTP/3 as own frame relay over `quic.Stream` with `github.com/quic-go/qpack` (ordered `[]HeaderField`). std `net/http` is used only for mitmweb and the onboarding app. Fidelity scope: HTTP/1 bytes, h2/h3 header lists and order, WebSocket uncompressed fragment layout and control frames. Not in scope: h2 frame boundaries, HPACK/QPACK representation choices, and WebSocket RSV1 (permessage-deflate is terminated per side, as upstream does in `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/websocket.py:111-117`). |
| Compatibility target | **Full**: flow file format v21 write; read of v18–v21 by porting `convert_18_19`, `convert_19_20`, `convert_20_21` (`py:mitmproxy/io/compat.py:391-433`, user decision in the review loop); versions < 18 rejected with `<mitmproxy-go version> cannot read files with flow format version N.` and versions > 21 with `<mitmproxy-go version> cannot read files with flow format version N, please update mitmproxy.` (`py:mitmproxy/io/compat.py:514-527`; tuple versions such as `(0, 10)` render as Python does, `(0, 10)`); HAR import/export; `~/.mitmproxy` CA layout and certificate attributes; filter grammar (32 tokens); 117 option names, `config.yaml`/`config.yml` with **upstream's precedence reproduced exactly** (user decision): dedicated CLI flags that were actually passed > `config.yml` > `config.yaml` > `--set` > defaults (`py:mitmproxy/tools/main.py:85-91`, `py:mitmproxy/optmanager.py:564-566`; `process_options` skips flags whose value is `None`); 46 hook names; mode spec grammar; mitmweb HTTP + WebSocket contract. Three binaries mirror upstream: `cmd/mitmdump`, `cmd/mitmproxy`, `cmd/mitmweb`. |
| local mode | **Reuse upstream redirectors**: Go implements only the IPC peer (`rs:src/ipc/mitmproxy_ipc.proto`) and, on Windows and in Linux packet mode, terminates the delivered IP packets in the same gVisor netstack used for WireGuard and TUN mode (macOS stream mode delivers TCP streams and UDP datagrams, so it needs no netstack). No GPL bindings, no signing in this repository. |

### 1.3 Package decisions (round C and mid-turn instructions)

| Concern | Decision (user) | Verified facts and consequences |
|---|---|---|
| CLI | `github.com/spf13/cobra` v1.10.2 + `github.com/spf13/pflag` v1.0.10 | `--set` uses `StringArrayVar` (not `StringToString`, whose map/CSV/quote-stripping semantics differ from upstream) and a port of `OptManager.set` / `_parse_setval` (`py:mitmproxy/optmanager.py:310-347,364-372,398`): repeated `--set` values for `Sequence[str]` options accumulate, bare `--set flag` means true, `toggle` flips booleans. Shell completion generators built in. |
| TUI | `charm.land/bubbletea/v2` v2.0.10 + `charm.land/bubbles/v2` v2.2.1 + `charm.land/lipgloss/v2` v2.0.6; tests with `github.com/charmbracelet/x/exp/teatest/v2` v2.0.0-20261004011457-ad85c59fdf4e (declares a dependency on `charm.land/bubbletea/v2`) | The `github.com/charmbracelet/bubbletea/v2` import path fails `go get`. `tea.Program.Send` blocks on an unbuffered channel (`bubbletea/v2@v2.0.10/tea.go:606,1192-1196`), which drives §3.1 rule 7. |
| Scripting | Starlark via `go.starlark.net` v0.0.0-20260930 (pseudo-version, no semver tags; BSD-3) | `load` is a Starlark keyword, so the lifecycle hook is exposed as `on_load`; all other 45 hook names are legal identifiers. Globals are executed with `Program.Init` (not `ExecFile`, which freezes globals) so scripts can keep module-level state; Starlark bytecode of ordinary hooks runs **only** inside the dispatch domain because unfrozen values are not thread-safe. **`concurrent` is provided** (user decision) as a predeclared builtin function written `request = concurrent(_request)` at module top level, because Starlark has no decorator syntax (`syntax/scan.go` has no `@` token); the design is in §1.3.1. Blocking builtins (`ctx.sleep`, `ctx.http_get`) called from ordinary hooks park the Starlark thread and release the lock only at dispatch depth 1; at depth > 1 they run inline without releasing it. `Thread.SetMaxExecutionSteps` bound via the Go-only option `script_max_steps`. |
| WebSocket | `github.com/zchee/gows` (Apache-2.0, zero deps), exact pseudo-version of the reviewed frame-API commit from the **in-session parallel unit** (branch commit first, main commit after the user merges its pull request) | Baseline `4a8b735` has message-mode control handling, public header codec and WithBuffered (`gows:conn.go:204-212`), not a usable relay lifecycle. The nine-point frame/relay contract and approved surface in P3 supply raw controls, fragment/close ownership, bounded decoded compression, extension negotiation and Abort. WebSocket adapter waits for that API/pin; no interim relay, `replace` or other library. |
| DNS | `codeberg.org/miekg/dns` v0.6.118 (BSD-3, `go 1.27.0`, pre-1.0) | Root package builds on go1.27.1 with only `x/crypto`, `x/net`, `x/sys` as indirect deps (module graph pruning keeps certmagic/sqlx/geoip2/prometheus out unless `cmd/` packages are imported). `HTTPS`/`SVCB` RR types present (`types.go`, `rdata/rdata.go`). DoQ not implemented upstream. |
| Filter regex | std `regexp` (RE2) first; on compile failure fall back to `github.com/dlclark/regexp2` v1.12.0 with `MatchTimeout` 100 ms | Python `re` features (backreferences, lookaround) only reach regexp2. Python's `$` also matches before a final newline; that is reproduced by a `regexp/syntax` rewrite when `$` is in tail position and by regexp2 elsewhere. |
| Observability | Baseline (`log/slog`, `net/http/pprof`, `runtime/metrics`) **plus OpenTelemetry** (`go.opentelemetry.io/otel` v1.47.0, `otelhttp` v0.72.0), exporter opt-in | pprof is enabled by the Go-only option `pprof_addr` (empty = off); upstream's `proxy_debug` keeps its meaning "Enable debug logs in the proxy core." (`py:mitmproxy/addons/proxyserver.py:193-198`). |
| YAML | `go.yaml.in/yaml/v4` v4.0.0-rc.6 (2026-06-17; no stable v4 tag yet; `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` is archived) | RC status is tracked as a risk (§8). |
| JSON | `encoding/json/v2` + `encoding/json/jsontext` (GA in Go 1.27; `GOEXPERIMENT=nojsonv2` opt-out exists); struct tags use `omitzero` | Project rule. |

### 1.3.1 Starlark `concurrent` design

1. *Spelling and hook name.* go.starlark.net has no decorator syntax: its scanner has no `@` token (`go.starlark.net@v0.0.0-20260930220527-d7438c5a85ac` `syntax/scan.go`) and `doc/spec.md` has no decorator production. `concurrent` is therefore a predeclared builtin function that takes a function and returns a wrapper value, written at module top level as `def _request(flow): ...` followed by `request = concurrent(_request)`. `FileOptions.GlobalReassign` stays off (the zero value, `syntax/options.go:28`), so `def request(flow): ...` followed by `request = concurrent(request)` fails to resolve, as in standard Starlark. The hook name comes from the global binding, not from the function, because `Function.Name()` returns the `def` name, `_request` (`starlark/value.go:759`): after `Program.Init` the loader walks the module globals and checks every global whose value is a `concurrent` wrapper under its binding name (rule 6), and the dispatcher looks up the same binding name in the frozen instance (rule 2). **Documented deviation** (§9, `docs/compat.md`): the `@concurrent` line of a Python script becomes this assignment. The wrapper implements `starlark.Callable` (`starlark/value.go:182-186`) so that a direct Starlark call of it, such as `request(flow)` from another hook or from top-level code, fails with the port's own text `concurrent(<fn>) cannot be called directly; it runs only when invoked as a hook`, where `<fn>` is the wrapped function's `Function.Name()` (a direct call can happen during module init, before the binding walk of rule 6 has named the wrapper); without `Callable` the user would see the interpreter's `invalid call of non-function (concurrent)` (`starlark/eval.go:1201`). **Documented deviation** (§9, `docs/compat.md`): upstream's wrapper is an `async def` (`concurrent.py:18-30`), so a direct Python call returns a coroutine that is never awaited and the handler never runs; Starlark has no coroutine value to return.
2. *Second module instance.* A frozen snapshot of the live globals cannot be built: a function reads its module's globals slice directly (`go.starlark.net@v0.0.0-20260930220527-d7438c5a85ac` `starlark/interp.go:640-641`, `fn.module.globals[arg]`), `Module` has no setter (`starlark/value.go:729-734`), and `StringDict.Freeze` freezes values in place (`starlark/eval.go:177-181`). Each script that binds at least one `concurrent` wrapper to a global therefore gets a second module instance from a second `Program.Init` run (`starlark/eval.go:436-444` builds fresh globals on every call), frozen with `StringDict.Freeze` right after init. Concurrent handlers are taken from that frozen instance; a frozen module may be called from several threads at once (`doc/spec.md:1390-1393`). Top-level code runs twice per load (and per reload). Each instance has its own predeclared object graph: `Program.Init` takes the predeclared dictionary as an argument (`starlark/eval.go:436-444`) and a function reads predeclared names from its own module (`starlark/interp.go:649-651`), so the second `Init` is passed a fresh `ctx` and fresh objects behind it (`ctx.options`, `ctx.master`, `ctx.log`), built for that instance and switched to worker mode after init (rule 4). `StringDict.Freeze` calls `Freeze` on every global value (`starlark/eval.go:177-181`), which reaches a predeclared object when a global aliases it, as in `o = ctx.options`. These objects keep `Freeze` as a no-op, because they hold no Starlark state of their own: what they read and write is Go state shared by both instances (the options manager, the command manager, the log), and that shared state is internally synchronised (rule 4 says how a worker reaches it). A real freeze would make `o.x = 1` fail in a worker although the same write through `ctx.options` is meant to work. **Documented deviation** (§9, `docs/compat.md`): module-level mutable state is not shared between a `concurrent` handler and the main-thread hooks; the handler sees the globals as they were after init, and writes to them raise "cannot insert into frozen hash table" (`starlark/hashtable.go:79,334`).
3. *Workers.* Each invocation runs on its own goroutine with its own `starlark.Thread`, bounded by a semaphore of min(32, GOMAXPROCS + 4) per script, the size of CPython's default executor that upstream's `run_in_executor(None, ...)` uses (`concurrent.py:30`; CPython 3.14.6 `concurrent/futures/thread.py:181`).
4. *Release and reacquire.* The worker call is built on §3.1 rule 4 (`addon.Concurrent`): the flow's hook chain waits, the dispatch lock is released while the handler runs and reacquired with a fresh frame for the merge-back. The flow is **not** intercepted, because upstream only awaits the executor (`concurrent.py:18-30`) and an intercept would let mitmweb or the console resume a flow whose merge-back is still pending. Code reached from a worker never touches shared state outside the dispatch lock. The second instance's predeclared graph (rule 2) has two modes. It is in **direct** mode while that instance's top-level code runs during its `Program.Init`, which happens on the loading goroutine under the dispatch lock, so a top-level `ctx.log("init")` there is logged at once instead of waiting on `master.Do`, which would deadlock. It is switched to **worker** mode under the dispatch lock after `StringDict.Freeze` and before the instance is handed to the dispatcher, so no worker goroutine can start against it in direct mode (the goroutine start orders the switch before the worker's first access). The main instance's graph is always in direct mode, because its code runs only inside the dispatch domain. In worker mode, attribute access does not need to know which goroutine or which worker it runs on, which it could not learn anyway (`HasAttrs.Attr` and `HasSetField.SetField` receive no `*Thread`, `starlark/value.go:367,385`, and up to min(32, GOMAXPROCS + 4) workers share the instance, rule 3): a `ctx.options` read loads the latest options snapshot from an `atomic.Pointer`; a snapshot is never modified after it is published; it is published once when the script is loaded and then by a subscriber registered with `options.Manager.Subscribe` (`go:options/manager.go:181` on main), which runs where the change is made, inside the dispatch domain (§3.1 rule 5), after every change and again after a rollback (`go:options/manager.go:176-180`); list-valued options are handed to Starlark as frozen values or as per-read copies, so two workers never share a mutable list. `ctx.options` writes, `ctx.master.commands.call` and `ctx.log` run through `master.Do` (§3.1 rule 5; `go:master/master.go:90` on main). Blocking builtins (`ctx.sleep`, `ctx.http_get`) park only that goroutine and never touch the lock. A worker's read of an option that a `master.Do` callback changes while the handler runs therefore returns the old or the new value. A visible consequence: a worker's `ctx.log` line reaches `add_log` and `eventstore` when the dispatch lock is next free, not at the moment of the call. *Reload and remove*: hot reload and `remove` never wait for in-flight workers while they hold the dispatch lock, because a worker blocked in `master.Do` (for `ctx.log`, say) would then wait for that lock forever. They mark the script's frozen instance retired and fire `done` at once, as upstream's `remove` takes the addon out of the chain and invokes `done` without waiting for anything (`py:mitmproxy/addonmanager.py:206-220`); no new invocation starts on a retired instance, and in-flight workers finish against it, its semaphore and its predeclared graph. A finished worker's merge-back (rule 5) runs as usual while its flow's hook chain is still waiting for it, as upstream's chain keeps awaiting `run_in_executor` across a reload (`concurrent.py:30`); it is dropped only when that wait has already ended because the connection closed and the stream's context was cancelled.
5. *Copy and merge-back.* The handler receives a deep copy of the flow (a Go-side clone wrapped as a fresh Starlark value); a snapshot of the copy's state is kept. On return, under the lock, each state field the handler changed relative to the snapshot is written to the live flow, and fields it did not change keep the live value (upstream shares one object, so the last writer wins per field; a full overwrite would discard concurrent `master.Do` edits). The merged fields are every key of the flow state (`py:mitmproxy/flow.py:143-159` plus the per-type keys, e.g. `py:mitmproxy/http.py:1237-1239`) except `version`, `type`, `id`, `timestamp_created` and the two connections: `error`, `is_replay`, `marked`, `metadata`, `comment`, `backup`, the HTTP `request`/`response`, the `websocket` data (`messages`, `closed_by_client`, `close_code`, `close_reason`, `timestamp_end`, `py:mitmproxy/websocket.py:161-175`), the DNS `request`/`response`, and the `messages` lists of TCP and UDP flows (`py:mitmproxy/tcp.py:58-62`); `intercepted` is handled by the replayed actions below. Message lists merge by index: entries that existed at copy time are taken from the copy, entries the live flow gained while the handler ran (the other WebSocket relay direction keeps appending, §3.1) are kept. `flow.intercept()`, `flow.resume()` and `flow.kill()` (which sets `error`) called on the copy are recorded as actions and replayed on the live flow under the lock in call order. Connection objects (`client_conn`, `server_conn`) are goroutine-owned (§3.1 rule 6), so the copy carries a read-only snapshot of both taken at copy time; a write to them from the handler raises an error, and they are never merged back (**documented deviation**: upstream's handler shares the live connection objects).
6. *Eligible hooks.* A `concurrent` wrapper is accepted when its binding name (rule 1) is one of the 23 hooks whose argument is a flow (the HTTP, WebSocket, TCP, UDP and DNS flow hooks). Bound to `on_load` or `configure`, it fails the script load with upstream's text `Concurrent decorator not supported for '<name>' method.` (`concurrent.py:13-16`), where `<name>` is the binding name. The other 21 hooks take no flow, or something other than a single flow (`tls_clienthello`, `next_layer`, `server_connect`, `client_connected` and the rest of the connection, TLS, QUIC and SOCKS5 hooks, plus `update`, `add_log`, `running`, `done`): they always run inline on the main thread, and a wrapper bound to one of them fails the load with the same text (**documented deviation**: upstream accepts them, `concurrent.py:13`). A wrapper bound to a global that is not a hook name fails the load with the same text, as upstream's check does for any other function name (`concurrent.py:13`).
7. *`script.run`.* Go's `script.run` command is a synchronous invocation of the script module only, exactly as upstream's (`py:mitmproxy/addons/script.py:180-188`: `configure`, `running`, then `eventsequence.iterate` over each flow, every event through `invoke_addon_sync(mod, evt)`, which visits the module and its own nested `addons` through `_iter_hooks` and `traverse`, `py:mitmproxy/addonmanager.py:110-117,243-262,278`, so no hook outside the script module and the nested `addons` it defines is invoked). It initialises the module once with `Program.Init` and builds no frozen second instance, because its `concurrent` handlers are never run. The eligibility walk of rules 1 and 6 runs on that module init, as on a `-s` load, and a failure takes upstream's load-error path: upstream's check runs when the decorator is applied during module execution inside `load_script` (`py:mitmproxy/addons/script.py:23-57`), the exception is caught there (`:51,54`) and logged by `script_error_handler` as `error in script <path>` with the error (`:60-71`), `load_script` returns `None`, and `script.run` invokes no event (`:178-179`); the port logs the same line and invokes no event. Its invoker checks each handler before calling it, whatever the dispatch depth: a `concurrent` wrapper is rejected with upstream's error `Async handler <hook> (<script>) cannot be called from sync context` (`py:mitmproxy/addonmanager.py:279-282`), where the port prints the script's path as `<script>`; upstream prints the module's repr there (`{addon}` at `:281`, such as `<module '__mitmproxy_script__.x' from '/path/x.py'>`), which has no Starlark counterpart (**documented deviation**). The first error ends the whole run, the remaining events and flows included, because upstream wraps the loop in one `safecall` (`script.py:180`), which logs `Addon error: <message>` (`addonmanager.py:56-59`). No other path reaches a flow hook synchronously: upstream's other synchronous invocations fire `update` (`py:mitmproxy/addons/core.py:62-238`, `view.py:400,413`, `clientplayback.py:263,287`, `serverplayback.py:155,171`, `comment.py:19`) or `configure`, `load`, `running` and `done` (`py:mitmproxy/addonmanager.py:139,146,189,220`, `script.py:124-131,229`), none of which the port lets a `concurrent` wrapper serve (rule 6), and the port adds no synchronous flow-hook invocation. `addon.Concurrent` keeps its own depth rule for Go addons (§3.1 rule 4).

### 1.4 Defaults set by the plan (no real contest; override at approval if wanted)

| Concern | Default | Why |
|---|---|---|
| HTTP/3 + QUIC | `github.com/quic-go/quic-go` v0.63.0 for the QUIC transport (`quic.Transport`, `quic.Conn`, `quic.Stream`, 0-RTT, datagrams, qlog) + `github.com/quic-go/qpack` v0.6.0 for HTTP/3 header blocks; the HTTP/3 frame parser is ported from `qgo:http3/frames.go` (MIT). **Not** `qgo:http3.RawServerConn`/`RawClientConn`: both decode into `*http.Request`/`*http.Response` (`qgo:http3/server_conn.go:149`, `qgo:http3/stream.go:325,356`), the client lowercases names (`qgo:http3/request_writer.go:236`) and the server rejects non-lowercase names (`qgo:http3/headers.go:159-163`), which loses header order and case. | `golang.org/x/net/quic` declares itself not production-ready (no 0-RTT, untuned, outside the Go security policy); `x/net/http3` is a test shim. |
| HTTP/2 framing | `golang.org/x/net/http2` + `/hpack` v0.59.0 (`Framer.ReadFrame`, `WriteRawFrame`, `hpack.Decoder`/`Encoder`); `ReadMetaHeaders` is **not** used because `readMetaFrame` validates and rejects uppercase names and late pseudo-headers (`xnet:http2/frame.go:1707-1830`), which contradicts `validate_inbound_headers=false`. | Go 1.27 moved HTTP/2 into `net/http/internal/http2` (not importable); x/net keeps the public `Framer`. |
| WireGuard + userspace stack | `golang.zx2c4.com/wireguard` v0.0.0-20260522 + `gvisor.dev/gvisor@go` (`v0.0.0-20261004063249-f57b8fc79db4`; the `go` branch builds on go1.27.1 for darwin/arm64 and linux/amd64, `@latest`/master does not) | Only pure-Go any-destination TCP/IP stack with `tcp.NewForwarder`/`udp.NewForwarder`; tun2socks/Tailscale prior art. +~3 MB binary. |
| Windows named pipes | **Withdrawn in revision 11 (ratified, §3.4 item 1).** `github.com/Microsoft/go-winio` v0.6.2 was planned for message mode, `FirstPipeInstance` and `RejectRemoteClients`; the landed `go:internal/local/windows_native_windows.go:126` sets the same flags through `golang.org/x/sys/windows` (already pinned) as a one-instance message pipe connected once, which reproduces upstream's `max_instances(1)` (`rs:src/packet_sources/windows.rs:43-49`); `windows_pipe_windows.go` owns the overlapped I/O | No module added; `x/sys/windows` exposes the flags directly. |
| DTLS | `github.com/pion/dtls/v3` v3.1.10 (MIT, `go 1.24.0`; builds on go1.27.1): `dtls.Server(net.PacketConn, net.Addr, *dtls.Config)` / `dtls.Client(net.PacketConn, net.Addr, *dtls.Config)` over the shared packet-preserving virtual transport (no net.Conn overload); `hookdata.TLS` keeps TLS Config and adds typed DTLSConfig selected by IsDTLS, built under dispatch by tlsconfig's DTLS owner; `Config.GetCertificate(*ClientHelloInfo)` (pion calls it when `Config.Certificates` is empty or the client sent SNI, `github.com/pion/dtls/v3@v3.1.10` `certificate.go:129-135`; the port leaves `Certificates` empty, so every handshake reaches `CertStore`, and without SNI the leaf's names come from the addresses as in P1 deliverable 6), `ServerName`, `SupportedProtocols` (ALPN), `VerifyConnection`, `InsecureSkipVerify`, `ClientAuth`, `KeyLogWriter` | std has no DTLS; pion is the only maintained pure-Go implementation. |
| Upstream redirector wheels | `mitmproxy_macos` 0.12.11 (`py3-none-any`, 2,508,780 B, sha256 `63349d9b…bae859`), `mitmproxy_windows` 0.12.11 (462,152 B, sha256 `59addc88…1a2d7`), `mitmproxy_linux` 0.12.11 (manylinux2014 x86_64 1,569,014 B sha256 `0e5670a6…6611c4`; aarch64 1,468,498 B sha256 `3de4f96c…3e9da`), all uploaded 2026-07-20 | The macOS wheel size shows its `.app.tar` is not the empty placeholder; the P6 pre-task still extracts and lists it and records the full digests in `go:internal/local/artifacts.go`. |
| Certificates | std `crypto/x509`; `software.sslmate.com/src/go-pkcs12` v0.7.3 (`Passwordless` encoder) for both `.p12` files: `Encode` for the key-bearing `mitmproxy-ca.p12`, `EncodeTrustStoreEntries` for the cert-only `mitmproxy-ca-cert.p12` (user decision) | Upstream writes passwordless `.p12` files; go-pkcs12's differ in structure (no MAC, a Java trust-store attribute on the cert-only bag, no `friendlyName` in the key file), listed in `docs/compat.md`, and Python's `pkcs12.load_pkcs12` reads both. |
| Compression | `github.com/klauspost/compress` v1.20.1 for zstd **and** gzip/flate/zlib (drop-in API, measured 2–3× faster decode than std in the library's own benchmarks; a proxy decodes every body for `~b` filters and content views), `github.com/andybalholm/brotli` v1.2.6 | std has no brotli/zstd; the project rule prefers a faster third-party package where performance matters. |
| protobuf views | `google.golang.org/protobuf` v1.36.12 (`encoding/protowire`, `dynamicpb`) + `github.com/bufbuild/protocompile` v0.14.1 for `protobuf_definitions` `.proto` loading; `protoc-gen-go` v1.36.12 for the IPC schema (generated code checked in) | Schema-less decode + runtime `.proto` parsing, as `rs:mitmproxy-contentviews/src/protobuf/` does. |
| msgpack view | `github.com/shamaton/msgpack/v3` v3.2.3 | `vmihailenco/msgpack/v5` has had no commit since 2023-10. |
| Highlighting | `github.com/alecthomas/chroma/v2` v2.27.0 | Pure Go; chroma token types mapped onto upstream's seven tags `Text, Name, String, Number, Boolean, Comment, Error` (`rs:mitmproxy-highlight/src/lib.rs:77-85`; risk §8). |
| Process info | `github.com/shirou/gopsutil/v4` v4.26.9; icons via `github.com/ebitengine/purego` v0.11.1 objc (macOS) and `syscall` (Windows) | Upstream has no Linux icons either. |
| Original destination | `golang.org/x/sys/unix` v0.48.0 (`SO_ORIGINAL_DST`; `IP6T_SO_ORIGINAL_DST` defined locally); macOS/FreeBSD `DIOCNATLOOK` ioctl on `/dev/pf` with hand-defined `pfioc_natlook`, falling back to `sudo -n pfctl -s state` parsing (`py:mitmproxy/platform/pf.py`) | No maintained Go pf package; SDK lacks `pfvar.h`. |
| Browser e2e | `github.com/chromedp/chromedp` v0.19.1 on ubuntu-26.04 | Reproducible in CI. |
| Clipboard (export addon) | `golang.design/x/clipboard` v0.11.0 behind build tag `clipboard` | CGO-free on desktop; optional. |
| Licence gate | `github.com/google/go-licenses/v2` v2.0.1 in CI with a denylist (GPL, LGPL, MPL, AGPL) | R10. |
| GitHub Actions | `actions/checkout@v7`, `actions/setup-go@v7` (latest majors on 2026-10-05: v7.0.1, v7.0.0) | Project rule: latest major only. |
| LRU/FIFO caches | hand-written (`CertStore` is a 100-entry FIFO upstream, self-synchronised with its own mutex because the onboarding handlers read it inside `addon.Concurrent` and the P3 DTLS layer's `GetCertificate` callback reads it, both outside the dispatch domain; the TLS configuration itself is built under the lock by `tlsconfig`, P1 deliverable 6) | Avoids MPL-2.0 `golang-lru`. |
| Script reload | 1 s mtime poll (`py:mitmproxy/addons/script.py` `ReloadInterval`) | Matches upstream, no fsnotify platform quirks. |
| Testing | `github.com/google/go-cmp` v0.7.0; `go.uber.org/goleak` v1.3.0; teatest v2 (above) | Project rule forbids testify. |
| Auth helpers | `golang.org/x/crypto` v0.57.0 (bcrypt, argon2, cryptobyte), `crypto/sha1` (htpasswd `{SHA}`; the htpasswd reader is ported, accepting exactly upstream's bcrypt and `{SHA}` entries, user decision), `github.com/go-ldap/ldap/v3` v3.4.14 (tested against an OpenLDAP service container in CI) | `py:mitmproxy/addons/proxyauth.py`, `py:mitmproxy/utils/htpasswd.py`, `py:mitmproxy/tools/web/webaddons.py`. |
| Public suffix, HTML | `golang.org/x/net/publicsuffix`, `golang.org/x/net/html` | `py:mitmproxy/tools/console/common.py`, `_view_xml_html.py`. |
| Explicitly avoided | `github.com/imgk/divert-go` (GPL-3.0), `github.com/AdguardTeam/gomitmproxy` (GPL-3.0), `github.com/sagernet/sing-tun` (GPL-3.0), `github.com/hashicorp/golang-lru/v2` (MPL-2.0), `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` (archived), `github.com/google/martian/v3` (archived) | Licence or maintenance. |

**Known capability losses accepted with these defaults** (the user acknowledges them at approval): std `crypto/tls` has no finite-field DHE, SSLv3, RC4, 3DES or export cipher suites and no `@SECLEVEL=0` equivalent, so `ssl_insecure` interception of legacy or IoT endpoints that only speak those suites fails where upstream (pyOpenSSL) succeeds; `mitmproxy-dhparam.pem` is written only for layout compatibility and never used. Go's `crypto/tls` runs ECH processing before `GetConfigForClient`; without the origin's ECH key the proxy sees only the outer `public_name` SNI, so clients attempting ECH to the proxy's leaf certificate fail unless the `strip_ech` option (default true, implemented by `strip_dns_https_records` in P4) has cleared the `ech` parameter of the HTTPS records they resolved through the proxy.

---

## 2. RALPLAN-DR summary

### Principles

1. **Pr1 Bytes in, same bytes out.** Anything the proxy does not deliberately change is forwarded unchanged at the fidelity scope defined in §1.2: HTTP/1 header case and order, HTTP/2 and HTTP/3 header lists and pseudo-header order, WebSocket uncompressed fragment layout, control frames, close codes. Normalisation only happens through an explicit option.
2. **Pr2 Upstream compatibility is a tested contract.** Flow files, CA layout, filter language, option names and precedence, hook names, mode specs and the mitmweb API are verified against upstream fixtures copied into `testdata/` and against the pinned Python mitmproxy in differential tests.
3. **Pr3 Hybrid, not dogmatic.** Pure parsers are sans-I/O with vector tests; anything that terminates TLS over TCP or QUIC uses the native `net.Conn`/`quic.Conn` Go APIs; protocol state machines are goroutine-owned.
4. **Pr4 Pure Go by default; native pieces are reused, not rebuilt.** Signed or kernel-adjacent components (macOS Network Extension, WinDivert driver) come from upstream release artifacts pinned by version and SHA-256.
5. **Pr5 From P1 on, every phase leaves a runnable mitmdump.** P0 is the only phase without a binary and is kept to the data model and registries that P1 cannot start without; afterwards nothing merges without the phase's acceptance tests.

### Decision drivers (top 3)

1. **D1 Asset compatibility**: users keep their `~/.mitmproxy` CA, `.mitm` dumps (v18 and newer), filter expressions, `config.yaml` and the mitmweb UI.
2. **D2 Maintainability on Go 1.27+**: std first; a third-party package when it is meaningfully better or std has no equivalent (the project's own rule); the five version-sensitive integrations (netstack, h2 framing, h3 framing, DNS codec, YAML codec) sit behind one adapter package each so drift is local.
3. **D3 Delivery-risk control**: ~48k lines of Python plus the Rust surface is ported in phases with upstream test vectors as the regression net, and the hardest native pieces (local mode) are isolated and feature-flagged.

### Viable options

| Option | Summary | Pros | Cons |
|---|---|---|---|
| **O1 (chosen)** Hybrid core, full wire fidelity, full compatibility | Own HTTP/1 parser, own h2/h3 endpoints on x/net Framer and quic-go streams, native TLS/QUIC I/O, upstream formats and APIs | Satisfies D1 fully; parsers reuse upstream vectors; h2/h3/WS fidelity equal to upstream; Go-native I/O | Largest code volume; two implementation styles to keep consistent; HTTP/2 and HTTP/3 state machines (SETTINGS, windows, stream states, GOAWAY, QPACK) are written here rather than borrowed; a lock discipline the type system cannot enforce (mitigated by the epoch-checked token, §3.1) |
| O2 std `net/http`-based proxy (goproxy-style), data-level compatibility only | Use `http.Server`/`Transport` with `Protocols`, keep flow files and CA compatible, redesign options/API | Fastest to ship; least code | Loses header case/order, non-token header names rejected, `Expect: 100-continue` handled by std, h2 re-encoded (map order); mitmweb React build unusable without a new API; violates Pr1 |
| O3 Faithful sans-I/O port | `Layer` state machines yielding `Command`s via `iter.Seq`, a server loop driving I/O | Upstream playbook tests port almost 1:1; deterministic tests | `crypto/tls` over TCP and quic-go are I/O-driven (no memory-BIO mode), so the TLS and QUIC layers need `net.Pipe`-style adapters and goroutines anyway (`xnet:http2.Framer` and `crypto/tls.QUICConn` would work sans-I/O); highest code volume; unusual for Go contributors |
| O4 Fork `github.com/lqqyt2423/go-mitmproxy` (MIT, v1.9.3, mitmproxy-compatible CA) | Start from an existing Go MITM proxy and add compatibility | Working HTTP/1, h2 (std) and WebSocket (gorilla) on day one | Built on std `net/http` and `x/net/http2` Server/Transport, so it has the O2 fidelity losses by construction; no flow format, filter, options, hooks or mitmweb compatibility; divergent architecture would be replaced piece by piece |

**Invalidation rationale**: O2 and O4 contradict driver D1 (mitmweb API, byte fidelity) and principle Pr1; O3 is rejected because the two I/O-bound libraries force adapters at the TLS and QUIC boundaries, which removes the main benefit of sans-I/O while keeping its cost. O1 keeps O3's benefit where it is cheap (pure parsers) and O2's benefit where it matters (native I/O). An actor-goroutine dispatch model was considered as a variant of O1 and rejected because the user fixed hooks on the stream goroutine; §3.1 rules 3 and 7 recover most of the actor model's safety (epoch-checked token, non-blocking frontend edges).

---

## 3. Target architecture

### 3.1 Concurrency and layering model

```
listener (TCP / UDP 4-tuple demux / QUIC / netstack forwarder / local-mode IPC)
  └─ proxy.ConnectionHandler            one goroutine per client connection; owns client Conn, server Conns, watchdog, pool
       ├─ layer.Next sniff              recording conn buffers until the sniff decision (py:mitmproxy/addons/next_layer.py)
       ├─ modes.* top layer             regular/reverse/upstream/transparent/socks5/dns (py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/modes.py)
       ├─ tlslayer.ClientTLS / ServerTLS crypto/tls over net.Conn; full ClientHello recorded (may span records) and replayed
       ├─ httplayer.Layer               HTTP/1 (own parser) | HTTP/2 (own endpoint per side on Framer+hpack) | HTTP/3 (own frames on quic.Stream + qpack)
       │    └─ httplayer.Stream         one goroutine per request/response pair; hooks dispatched under the dispatch lock
       ├─ websocket.Layer               after 101; one HTTPFlow owner, two socket readers + cloned inject channel
       ├─ tcplayer / udplayer           raw relay with message capture, inject channel, `ignore` fast path (TCP layer lands in P1)
       └─ dnslayer                      request/response matching by ID; resolver addon answers when no upstream
```

**Hook dispatch domain** (mirrors upstream's single event loop where it matters):

1. `addon.Manager.Hook(ctx, hook)` takes one process-wide dispatch mutex, runs the addon chain on the **calling stream goroutine**, fires the `update` hook with the flow after every flow hook (`py:mitmproxy/addonmanager.py:232-241`), then releases the lock. All addon-shared state (`view` store, `serverplayback` flow map, `clientplayback` queue, `stickycookie` jar, `eventstore`, `save` writer, `intercept`, every Starlark module) is therefore accessed single-threaded, exactly as upstream guarantees.
2. The intercept wait (`flow.WaitForResume`) runs **after** the lock is released, and **each connection disarms its own** watchdog (a counter) from before it waits for the lock until its flow's intercept wait ends (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_servers.py:69-75`, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/server.py:79-89`); the process-wide dispatch callbacks are not used for this, because they fire for every connection's hooks. The proxy fires hooks only through the connection's hook runner (P1 deliverable 1), which does both.
3. **Re-entrancy token with epoch.** Go mutexes are not re-entrant, so every hook and command API takes a `context.Context` carrying a pointer to the current *dispatch frame*; the frame is valid only while `frame.epoch == manager.epoch`. Commands that fire `update` or `trigger` re-enter through a valid frame instead of re-locking. A goroutine spawned inside a hook inherits a frame that becomes stale as soon as the lock is released, so any later use is detected (test builds panic on a stale frame; release builds return an error).
4. `addon.Concurrent(ctx, fn)` is allowed only at dispatch depth 1 and not inside `load`, `configure`, the `done` of `remove`/`clear` or a command (upstream raises "cannot be called from sync context" for an async handler dispatched through `invoke_addon_sync` or `trigger`, `py:mitmproxy/addonmanager.py:276-283`); it bumps the epoch, releases the lock around `fn` while that flow's hook chain waits, and opens a fresh frame when it reacquires. Addons that block on I/O (`dns_resolver`, LDAP `proxyauth`) use it.
5. Goroutines outside hooks (mitmweb handlers, the console loop, the script reloader, OTel) mutate flows only through `master.Do(ctx, func(ctx context.Context) error) error`, which takes the dispatch lock and hands the callback a valid frame so that `options.Set` (fires `configure`) or `flow.Resume` (fires `update`) inside the callback re-enter instead of deadlocking.
6. Protocol state machines (h1 stream, h2/h3 connection, WebSocket, TCP/UDP relay, netstack) are goroutine-owned and lock-free. A layer writes a flow field that handlers can see only inside a dispatch hold: in the `prepare` step of `addon.Manager.HookFunc`, which runs it and then the hook in one hold at depth 1 so handlers keep `Concurrent`, or through `master.Do`. One goroutine per flow owns those writes; a relay's reader goroutines hand data to it.
7. **Code running under the dispatch lock never blocks on a consumer outside it.** Exempt by design, as upstream blocks its loop the same way: the `dumper`, `termlog` and `save` writes to stdout, stderr and files. Hook-to-frontend notifications go through non-blocking paths: each mitmweb `/updates` client has a bounded queue (1,024 messages) and is closed with 1011 on overflow; the console keeps a coalescing dirty flag that a `tea.Cmd` goroutine drains with `tea.Program.Send` (never called under the lock, because `Send` blocks on an unbuffered channel); frontends call `master.Do` only from `tea.Cmd` goroutines or HTTP handler goroutines, never from inside the bubbletea `Update` function.
8. **Lock order**: dispatch lock → per-flow mutex (which guards intercept state and the backup only), never the reverse; `CertStore` and the option registry's read path have their own mutexes because they are read outside dispatch (onboarding handlers inside `addon.Concurrent`, pion's `GetCertificate` for DTLS in P3, option reads from any goroutine).

**Other model rules**

- **Server connection pool** is **per client connection**, as upstream's `HttpLayer` holds it (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/__init__.py:921-946`), keyed on (address, tls, via, transport_protocol) plus SNI as a documented stricter deviation; establishment is single-flight so concurrent stream goroutines wait for a connection that is still being opened; when the client speaks h2 and the first established upstream connection is not h2, the remaining waiters re-dial with `reuse=false` instead of joining (`:1178-1188`); `connection_strategy` eager/lazy honoured.
- **Half-close**: a `closeWriter` interface (`CloseWrite() error`) implemented by the recording conn, gonet conns and the TCP relay; for `*tls.Conn` the proxy sends `close_notify` via `tls.Conn.CloseWrite` (only after the handshake) **and then** `CloseWrite` on the underlying TCP conn so a FIN follows, because upstream half-closes TLS with a bare FIN (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/tls.py:460-466` leaves TLS shutdown to the transport close); every relay test asserts both directions close cleanly with goleak.
- **Buffered handover**: TCP byte transitions use the recording conn's replay: CONNECT → TLS, TLS → raw TCP on `ignore_connection`, 101 → WebSocket (`gows:WithBuffered`), h2c preface, SOCKS5 → next layer. UDP/DTLS transitions use the internal packet recorder/replay and preserve each datagram boundary, never the TCP byte recorder.
- **Timeouts**: `tcp_timeout` default 600 s, UDP 20 s (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/server.py:47`); the watchdog is a per-connection idle timer disarmed per rule 2.
- **UDP flows**: a distinctly named **internal packet transport** (not the public serializable `flow.UDPFlow`) provides virtual `net.PacketConn` per fixed 4-tuple, bounded packet queues, cancellation/deadlines, packet-preserving recorder/replay and Close/expiry isolation; no-op `SetReadBuffer`/`SetWriteBuffer` is shared by the UDP socket demux, netstack UDP forwarder and macOS per-flow IPC. UDP watchdog expiry is 20 s from the last datagram, with hook disarm/rearm and injected-clock tests owned in P3. QUIC runs one `quic.Transport` on that virtual conn per flow, which is how datagrams buffered during ClientHello sniffing (a Go 1.27 client's X25519MLKEM768 hello spans two Initials) are replayed in order to quic-go or to the raw passthrough; connection migration is unsupported, as upstream; proven by the P4 spike.
- **TLS ordering**: the client-side layer records bytes until the whole ClientHello handshake message has arrived (it may span several records, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/tls.py:32-71` `handshake_record_contents`/`get_client_hello`), fires `tls_clienthello`, then either replays the buffer into a raw TCP relay (`ignore_connection`, `tcp.TCPLayer(ignore=True)` upstream at `:600`) or starts server TLS first when `ClientHelloData.establish_server_tls_first` is set (`py:mitmproxy/tls.py:127`; `tlsconfig` sets it to `server.tls and connection_strategy == "eager"`, `py:mitmproxy/addons/tlsconfig.py:204-208`); a failed server handshake is logged and client TLS continues (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/tls.py:606-617`). **ALPN** (`py:mitmproxy/addons/tlsconfig.py:92-112,254-262,293-302`): for a secure web proxy (layer stack exactly `[HttpProxy, TLS]`, i.e. a client that speaks TLS to the proxy itself) the preset client-side protocol is forced to `http/1.1` (`:257-260`), because CONNECT over HTTP/2 is not supported (upstream's comment at `:254-256`); otherwise the preset is `client_conn.alpn` when it is already set on the client connection (by an addon in `tls_clienthello` or by a prior layer, `:262`); a preset, forced or not, is returned when the client offers it, else no protocol (`:97-101`), so a client that offers only `h2` to a secure web proxy gets no ALPN and Go leaves `NextProtos` empty instead of `["http/1.1"]`; otherwise the server's negotiated protocol is returned when offered; if the server refused ALPN (`server_alpn == b""`) the client gets no protocol either; otherwise the first client offer that is in `HTTP_ALPNS` (h2 only when `http2` is on) is chosen, else none. The server-side offer, when no addon has set one, mirrors the client's offers, dropping `h2` when `http2` is off (`:293-303`); a client that sent no ALPN extension, or a client without TLS, gets an empty upstream offer, so no ALPN extension is sent to the server (`:304-310`). `tlsconfig` sets `NextProtos` on the `*tls.Config` it builds in `tls_start_client` to exactly that single protocol or leaves it empty, because Go otherwise aborts with `no_application_protocol` where OpenSSL continues without ALPN. Main 60157dd still forces `http2=false` semantics on both sides. P3 HTTP integration removes that hard-coded gate **atomically with both negotiated-protocol endpoint factories and working multiplexed dispatch**, after the h2 engine lands; metadata crosses via dispatch snapshots, and the secure explicit-proxy exception above is retained.
- **Message injection** (`MessageInjected`) uses a per-layer channel selected alongside the relay reads; the proxy finds the owning connection by client id (P1 deliverable 1).
- **Flow fields** are written only under the dispatch lock (rule 6); the per-flow mutex guards intercept state and the backup only, as built in `go:flow`.

### 3.2 Package layout (`go:`)

Public packages (what a Go addon author or embedder imports):

```
flow/        Flow, HTTPFlow, TCPFlow, UDPFlow, DNSFlow, Error, flow state codec (py:flow.py, http.py, tcp.py, udp.py, dns.py)
flow/state/  state.Map, Decoder and value helpers: the serialised-state vocabulary every model's GetState/SetState uses (py:mitmproxy/coretypes/serializable.py)
omap/        insertion-ordered string-keyed Map with text and bytes key kinds; state.Map, the tnetstring codec and the options/command registries are built on it
connection/  Client, Server, TransportProtocol (py:connection.py)
httpmsg/     Headers (raw field tuples), Request, Response, Message, URL/cookies/multipart/status (py:http.py:49-179, py:mitmproxy/net/http/*)
websocket/   WebSocketData, WebSocketMessage, Fragmentizer (py:websocket.py)
tcp/ udp/    TCPFlow/UDPFlow message types (py:tcp.py, udp.py)
dns/         Message/Question/RR views over miekg v2 (py:dns.py, py:mitmproxy/net/dns/*) — state shape owned here (P0), codec glue in P4
tlsparse/    ClientHello parsing (TLS record, DTLS in P3, QUIC Initial in P4); the hook values live in addon/hookdata (py:tls.py, py:mitmproxy/net/tls.py, py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/quic/_client_hello_parser.py)
certs/       CA, leaf, CertStore, PKCS#12, dhparam constant (py:certs.py)
options/     Manager, typed registry, yaml v4 codec, --set parser (py:optmanager.py, options.py)
command/     Manager, lexer, type system (py:command.py, command_lexer.py, types.py)
addon/       Addon hook interfaces (46), Manager, Loader, dispatch domain, eventsequence, Concurrent (py:addonmanager.py, hooks.py, eventsequence.py)
addon/addontest/      recording addon for hook-dispatch tests
addon/eventsequence/  Iterate: the hook sequence of a finished flow (py:eventsequence.py)
addon/hookdata/       values passed to the connection, TLS and QUIC hooks (py:mitmproxy/proxy/server_hooks.py, py:tls.py, py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/quic/_hooks.py)
addons/<name>/  one package per upstream addon (py:mitmproxy/addons/*)
filter/      grammar, 32 tokens, RE2/regexp2 (py:flowfilter.py)
filter/regex/ pattern compilation: RE2 first, regexp2 for what RE2 lacks, Python class and flag semantics (py:flowfilter.py)
flowio/      tnetstring, Reader/Writer, compat (18→21), HAR, flowjson (flow_to_json port) (py:mitmproxy/io/*, py:mitmproxy/tools/web/app.py flow_to_json)
flowio/tnetstring/ mitmproxy's tnetstring dialect: Dumps, Load, Pop; dictionaries are omap.Map[any], the state.Map type (py:mitmproxy/io/tnetstring.py)
contentviews/ registry, metadata, views, highlight (py:mitmproxy/contentviews/*, rs:mitmproxy-contentviews, rs:mitmproxy-highlight)
script/      Starlark loader and bindings (py:mitmproxy/addons/script.py, py:mitmproxy/script/)
master/      Master, Do, lifecycle (py:master.py)
```

Internal packages (free to change):

```
cmd/mitmdump, cmd/mitmproxy, cmd/mitmweb        thin cobra mains (py:mitmproxy/tools/main.py, cmdline.py)
internal/proxy/layer/      layer contract: Layer, Context, Conn, Recorder, Hooks, ServerPool, Injected, Next, the registry that builds a hookdata.LayerStack (py:mitmproxy/proxy/layer.py, context.py)
internal/proxy/            Handler (ConnectionHandler), Connections, watchdog, recording conn, server pool (py:mitmproxy/proxy/server.py)
internal/proxy/proxytest/  in-process proxy and origins for integration tests
internal/proxy/layers/{modes,tlslayer,httplayer,tcplayer}   P1; websocket, udplayer (P3), dnslayer, quic (P4), socks5 (P5) follow; no tunnel package: TLS wraps the conn and an upstream CONNECT returns a conn
internal/proxy/modespec/   ProxyMode parsing (py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_specs.py)
internal/proxy/modeserver/ per-mode listeners (py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_servers.py)
internal/http1/            read.go, assemble.go (py:mitmproxy/net/http/http1/{read,assemble}.py)
internal/h2/               endpoint, HEADERS assembly, windows (py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/_http2.py, _http_h2.py)
internal/h3/               frame codec over quic.Stream, qpack glue (py:.../_http3.py, _http_h3.py)
internal/netutil/{check,encoding,freeport,localip,serverspec}   (py:mitmproxy/net/{check,encoding,free_port,local_ip,server_spec}.py)
internal/platform/         original_addr per OS (py:mitmproxy/platform/*)
internal/netstack/         gVisor glue (rs:src/network/*)
internal/wireguard/        wireguard-go device over netstack (rs:src/packet_sources/wireguard.rs); one configured peer on main (R46-16), multi-peer routing is a unit of the revision 11 round
internal/local/            redirector IPC, launch and artifact acquisition (rs:src/ipc, rs:src/packet_sources/{macos,windows,linux}.rs, rs:mitmproxy-rs/src/server/local_redirector.rs); Windows pipes through x/sys/windows, no go-winio (§3.4)
internal/tun/              Linux TUN device setup and netstack bridge (rs:src/packet_sources/tun.rs)
internal/processinfo/      active_executables, executable_icon (rs:src/processes/*)
internal/tools/cmdline/, internal/tools/dump/, internal/tools/console/, internal/tools/web/   (py:mitmproxy/tools/*)
internal/htpasswd/         htpasswd reader, bcrypt and {SHA} entries (py:mitmproxy/utils/htpasswd.py)
internal/magisk/           magisk module zip, subject_hash_old (py:mitmproxy/utils/magisk.py)
internal/pyrepr/           Python repr formatting shared by filter, tnetstring, options, encoding and dumper
internal/textwrap/         Python textwrap.wrap for option and command help
internal/human/            byte sizes, durations, timestamps, addresses for display; size parser (py:mitmproxy/utils/human.py)
internal/stateutil/        write-side state helpers of the models (Opt, OptBytes, Bytes, BytesList) and the Now/NewID timestamp and UUID formats (py:mitmproxy/coretypes/serializable.py, py:time.time, py:uuid.uuid4)
internal/strutil/          escaping, binary and XML sniffing, hex dumps, region protection (py:mitmproxy/utils/strutils.py)
internal/version/          release version (py:mitmproxy/version.py)
internal/testutil/         fixtures loader (py:mitmproxy/test/*)
internal/testutil/testflow/ TFlow, TReq, TResp, TClientConn, TServerConn and per-type flow builders (py:mitmproxy/test/tflow.py, tutils.py)
internal/difftest/         differential harness against the pinned Python
testdata/                  upstream fixtures with MIT notice (mitmproxy/ in upstream's test/mitmproxy/data layout, wg-test-client/)
```

Godoc comments end with a period; tests use `tests := map[string]struct{...}`; benchmarks use `b.Loop()`; hook dispatch asserts per-hook single-method interfaces at registration, never reflection on the hot path.

### 3.3 Cross-cutting conventions

- Formatting pipeline for every Go change: `gofmt -s -w .`, `gofumpt -w -extra .`, `modernize -fix -test ./...`, `goimports-rereviser -project-name=github.com/zchee/mitmproxy-go -use-cache -cache-fast-skip -rm-unused -set-alias -recursive .`.
- Logging: `log/slog` with a `termlog_verbosity`-driven level and upstream's `Log` command/`add_log` mapped to a handler that feeds `eventstore` (`py:mitmproxy/log.py`, `py:mitmproxy/addons/eventstore.py`).
- Errors: typed sentinel errors per package; `errors.Join` for multi-errors; hook errors are logged and the chain continues unless `AddonHalt`.
- CI (`go:.github/workflows/ci.yaml`, created in P0): `runs-on` only `ubuntu-26.04`, `xcode-27`, `windows-2025`; actions pinned at major (`actions/checkout@v7`, `actions/setup-go@v7`); block-style YAML lists; Go versions `stable` (blocking) and `tip` via `gotip` (non-blocking); jobs: build + vet + golangci-lint, unit (`-race`), integration (`-tags integration`), fuzz smoke (`-fuzztime=30s`), platform tests, licence gate (`go-licenses`), differential tests against Python (ubuntu only, `uv run --with "mitmproxy @ git+https://github.com/mitmproxy/mitmproxy@3368a0a"`), browser e2e (chromedp, ubuntu); the integration, platform and browser e2e jobs are added with the first test of their kind (integration from P1, platform from P5, browser e2e in P8). `go:.github/dependabot.yaml` groups Go module updates weekly. `go:docs/dependencies.md` records every pin, why it exists and the known-good upgrade path.

### 3.4 Ratified deviations from the P4+P6 round (revision 11)

The user ratified every deviation of the P4+P6 round at its closeout (2026-10-09 22:02 JST, closeout item 7; the ledger is `.omc/artifacts/p46-session/rulings-p46.md`, "USER CLOSEOUT DECISIONS batch 1"). This subsection records what each one changes in the architecture; the P4 and P6 sections of §5 stay as the record of what was planned, and where they conflict with this subsection, this subsection and the code on main prevail.

1. **No `go-winio`.** The Windows packet-mode pipe `\\.\pipe\mitmproxy-transparent-proxy-<pid>` is created through `golang.org/x/sys/windows` in `go:internal/local/windows_native_windows.go:126` (`CreateNamedPipe` with `PIPE_ACCESS_DUPLEX|FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED|FILE_FLAG_FIRST_PIPE_INSTANCE`, `PIPE_TYPE_MESSAGE|PIPE_READMODE_MESSAGE|PIPE_REJECT_REMOTE_CLIENTS`, one instance) and connected once; there is no separate listener that is closed after acceptance, the owned connected pipe handle is kept for the IPC, and `go:internal/local/windows_pipe_windows.go` owns the overlapped I/O on it until shutdown (cancellation, completion, join). The §4 row for `github.com/Microsoft/go-winio` is withdrawn; `go.mod` and `docs/dependencies.md` carry no row for it. Reason: the pinned `x/sys` already exposes the flags, and one fewer pre-1.0 module sits in the critical path.
2. **Synthetic IPC fixtures instead of captured frames.** P6 pre-task (b) planned captures of the pinned Python's IPC frames against the real redirectors. The landed rows use in-process and subprocess peers that speak the native protocol: a Unix-datagram subprocess peer with real IPv4/IPv6 TCP/UDP origins on Linux (`TestLocalLinuxPacketIPCTCPOrigin`, `TestLocalLinuxPacketIPCOrigin`) and a test-only `Redirector` on real message-mode named pipes on Windows (`TestLocalWindowsPacketIPCTCPNoElevation`, `TestLocalWindowsPacketIPCNoElevation`, R46-130). `docs/compat.md` under `internal/local` states that these fixtures prove the frontend and IPC path and are not evidence of driver activation or process attribution; native evidence is the packet-modes privileged job on Linux and the manual runbooks on macOS and Windows (§3.4 item 9, and the runbook unit of the revision 11 round).
3. **`modeserver.Config.ConfDir` and `Config.LocalRedirector` exported** (`go:internal/proxy/modeserver/modeserver.go:42-47`). The packet-source mode servers read the immutable configuration directory from the former, and the local frontends share one process-lifetime `local.Redirector` daemon through the latter; the proxyserver addon owns the daemon, and a frontend `Stop` disables interception without closing it. These two fields are the mode-server surface that P6 deliverable 5 left implicit.
4. **Private HTTP/3 origin acquisition; `layer.ServerPool` stays frozen.** HTTP/3 integration acquires upstream QUIC connections through a destination/SNI-keyed acquisition path private to `httplayer` (concurrent streams share a live session; eviction is idle-only and excludes borrowed or establishing entries, `go:internal/proxy/layers/httplayer/quicgo.go:303-317`; after a physical close, exchanges drain for `http3DrainTimeout`, `quicgo.go:33`) rather than by extending the pool contract frozen for the round. §3.1's per-client-connection pool rule applies to TCP origins; QUIC origins are not pooled through it, and the pool key has no QUIC transport value. A later unification is a shared-contract change under the §5 round rules, not an implied follow-up.
5. **`reverse_schemes_quic.go` and `reverse_schemes_http3.go`** fill the reverse-scheme table of `go:internal/proxy/layers/modes` as revision 10 planned; they are the only P4-owned files in that package besides the bootstrap, and `modes.go` was edited once.
6. **WireGuard acceptance through a subprocess DumpMaster harness (R46-117).** `cmd/mitmdump` loads no script addon before P10 (`-s` is unported), so the end-to-end row builds `dump.New` with the default addons plus an upper-casing TCP/UDP addon registered through `Manager.Add`, runs it as a subprocess with `--mode wireguard` against `wg-test-client` v0.1.6, and exercises the same proxyserver, `Handler` and flow path as the CLI without script loading. TUN rows run the built CLI. Recorded in `docs/compat.md` under `internal/wireguard`.
7. **One configured WireGuard peer (R46-16, user decision 2026-10-07 20:45 JST).** One wireguard-go device with the `{server_key, client_key}` peer of `wireguard.conf` and `AllowedIPs 0.0.0.0/0, ::/0`, so any source address the client uses is accepted; the Rust learned-source multi-peer routing and first-peer fallback (`rs:src/packet_sources/wireguard.rs`) were not ported (`go:internal/wireguard/server.go:33`). The user funded that port for the revision 11 round (closeout batch 2, item 5); until it lands, the `docs/compat.md` row the round owes (§5, "compat rows owed") records the deviation, and P6 deliverable 2's "peer routing by learned source IP with first-peer fallback" is read as that unit's scope.
8. **Linux local-mode readiness barrier (R46-142, R46-144).** Upstream's Linux redirector prints its IPC endpoint before it loads eBPF and applies the intercept rules, and never acknowledges application. The Go frontend therefore sends two byte-identical configuration datagrams (the configuration and the barrier), waits within a five-second budget for the Unix-datagram sender queue to drain to zero, and then reconnects the same socket to the advertised endpoint as a liveness check; it sends no third configuration datagram, and any send or reconnect failure or a closed peer reports not-ready so the start fails closed (`go:internal/local/linux.go:234-239,258-281`; the reconnect realisation of R46-144 is recorded in the ledger at `rulings-p46.md:1161`). The CI-34 proof (`TestLinuxLocalExecutable/success:_curl_process_intercepted` on the privileged job) depends on it. No ABI change was made; an explicit apply-ACK message is the revision 11 round's upstream proposal unit.
9. **Evidence boundaries (R46-134, R46-140, closeout item 6).** Windows native evidence for a landing is the exact-head Windows CI test job on the candidate, with a `GOOS=windows` test-binary compile as the local evidence. The Linux reference host for native local-mode interception is the CI `ubuntu-26.04` runner (packet-modes privileged job); the remote Debian 13 host (kernel 6.12.111) cannot load the pinned redirector's BTF (`aya::bpf: object BTF could not be loaded; BPF_BTF_LOAD Invalid argument`) and is recorded as a host note, not a product defect. macOS and Windows local mode get a manual runbook in the revision 11 round; neither is a CI row.
10. **CI toolchain selection (R46-135).** `actions/setup-go@v7` exports `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` itself, so a `go.mod` patch bump cannot be followed through an env override; every stable job in `ci.yaml`, `network.yaml` and `packet-modes.yaml` uses `go-version-file: go.mod` with `check-latest: true` (manifest first, go.dev fallback); the gotip job is unchanged. §3.3's "Go versions `stable`" is read accordingly.
11. **Lead deviations LD-1 to LD-5 (round mechanics).** LD-1: the lead re-stacked, gated with the lane's own gate script, pushed and landed the h3 SEC-2 commit while that lane was unavailable (identical patch-id). LD-2 and LD-3: the lead pushed lanes' already-gated commits from the main checkout with a per-command `x-access-token` authorization header when a stale netrc credential or the lane harness refused the lane's push. LD-4: the lead re-stacked packet-gates' commit onto exact main after the lane pane was lost (patch-id identical, lane full gate rerun under the lock). LD-5: the lead wrote the netstack lane's final handoff section from the ledger and re-verified the lane's evidence manifest. The rule these ratifications establish: a lead push or re-stack on a lane's behalf is allowed only with an identical patch-id, the lane's own gate script, exact-head CI on the new hash, and a ledger entry; a content change is never made by the lead.
12. **Test-only edit of a frozen package (NB-10) and plan vocabulary in a commit subject (NB-1).** `d68edb6` changed `go:internal/proxy/packettransport/transport_test.go` (admission versus delivery fixture) without a named grant; ratified, and the revision 11 round grants that file explicitly to its fixture-hardening unit. The plan-revision commit `cbfa242` names phases (`P3` to `P6`) in its subject; exempt by design because the plan's own vocabulary is the subject.
13. **Docs lint baseline (R46-123) and the `setup-uv` pin (R46-119).** Markdown gates compare raw markdownlint counts against the baseline classes (MD013/MD060 plus the legacy MD022/MD058/MD038) with no mass reformat and no lint-configuration edit; bare artifact URLs become Markdown links. `astral-sh/setup-uv` publishes no major tag, so its pin stays a full tag with a comment beside it; the other actions are at their latest majors (`checkout@v7`, `setup-go@v7`, `upload-artifact@v7`, `lint-action@v9`).

## 5. Phases

Each phase lists deliverables with upstream references, testable acceptance criteria and the verification commands. Phase order is also dependency order; P2 and P3 can run in parallel lanes after P1 (with the gated items noted), P7 can run alongside P5–P6.

### P0 — Foundation and data model

**Goal**: compile-able module with the data model, codecs and registries P1 cannot start without; CI green. No binary yet (Pr5 applies from P1). Certificates, the command lexer/type system and HAR were moved out of P0 to the phases that first consume them (P1, P1, P2); only the command *registration* core stays in P0 because `addon.Loader.AddCommand` needs it.

Deliverables
1. `go.mod` with the pins of §4, each module added with the first package that imports it; `go:hack/fmt.sh` running the formatting pipeline; `go:.golangci.yaml`; `go:.github/workflows/ci.yaml` (jobs and Go versions as §3.3, including the `tip` job, the licence gate and the differential job skeleton; the integration, platform and browser e2e jobs land with the first test of their kind); `go:.github/dependabot.yaml` (weekly, grouped); `go:docs/dependencies.md`.
2. `go:testdata/` import of upstream fixtures (under `testdata/mitmproxy/` in the layout of `py:test/mitmproxy/data`, and `testdata/wg-test-client/`) with `testdata/UPSTREAM_LICENSE` (MIT, Aldo Cortesi) and `testdata/README.md` recording the source commit: `py:test/mitmproxy/data/flows/*.mitm` (versions: `corrupted_gzip_body` 21; `event_stream`, `websocket` 20; `diff_data`, `error_log`, `incomplete_log`, `successful_log` 18) and matching `.har`, `py:test/mitmproxy/data/dumpfile-*.mitm` (`dumpfile-7` is v11, `dumpfile-7-websocket` and `dumpfile-019` are v7, `dumpfile-10` is v10, `dumpfile-19` is v20, `dumpfile-018` and older carry tuple versions) for the rejection and migration tests, `py:test/mitmproxy/data/har_files/*`, `corrupted_har/*`, `flows/compressed.zhar`, `confdir/*` (including `mitmproxy-magisk-module.zip`), `servercert/*`, `clientcert/*`, `dercert`, `mitmproxy.pem`, `testkey.pem`, `invalid-subject.pem`, `no_common_name.pem`, `htpasswd`, `test_config.yml`, `pf01`, `pf02`, `image_parser/*`, `image.*`, `py:test/wg-test-client/*` (binaries + `test.conf` + LICENSE).
3. `go:flow/`, `go:connection/`, `go:httpmsg/`, `go:websocket/`, `go:tcp/`, `go:udp/`, `go:dns/` **state models for every flow type** (needed by the hook test and by `websocket.mitm`): state dictionaries exactly as the `get_state` methods in `py:mitmproxy/flow.py`, `http.py`, `connection.py`, `websocket.py`, `tcp.py`, `udp.py`, `dns.py` and `coretypes/serializable.py`, cross-checked against the key sets present in the v21 fixture `corrupted_gzip_body.mitm` (note: the connection key `state` was removed in format 20 by `convert_19_20`, `py:mitmproxy/io/compat.py:420-424`, and unknown keys make Python's `set_state` raise "Unexpected fields", `py:mitmproxy/coretypes/serializable.py:115-118`); `metadata` as an insertion-ordered map; `Headers` as an ordered slice of raw `[2][]byte` with case-insensitive lookup (`py:mitmproxy/http.py:49-179`); `Request`/`Response` with `http_version`, `trailers`, timestamps; URL parsing (`py:mitmproxy/net/http/url.py`), cookies (`cookies.py`), multipart (`multipart.py`), status codes, `validate.py`; `WebSocketMessage` tuples `(opcode, from_client, content, timestamp, dropped, injected)`; TCP/UDP `(from_client, content, timestamp)`; DNS message state keys. The protocol *layers* for these types come later; only the data model is here.
4. `go:flowio/tnetstring`: tags `, ; # ^ ! ~ ] }`; writer emits `;` for str keys and values, `,` for bytes, and writes dict entries in **reverse insertion order** (upstream builds the output right-to-left with `_rdumpq`, `py:mitmproxy/io/tnetstring.py:70-148`; the keys in `corrupted_gzip_body.mitm` run from `websocket` to `version`); reader accepts bytes keys; floats formatted like Python `repr` (shortest round-trip digits; exponent form iff the decimal exponent is < -4 or >= 16; e.g. `1759600000.123456`, `1e+16`, `1e-05`), with a test vector table. `go:flowio/` `flowio.Reader` (BOM skip, `{` → HAR handled in P2, version check and the ported `convert_18_19`, `convert_19_20`, `convert_20_21` from `py:mitmproxy/io/compat.py:391-433`; versions < 18 and > 21 rejected with the §1.2 texts) and `flowio.Writer` (always v21).
5. `go:filter/`: tokenizer + an operator-precedence (shunting-yard) parser that mirrors pyparsing's iterative `infix_notation` (`py:mitmproxy/flowfilter.py:770-781`), verified by a golden file recorded from the pinned upstream for 418 expressions (`go:filter/testdata/parse_golden.json`), because upstream accepts and rejects inputs differently from a backtracking grammar (`a |`, `(a b)`, `(~q)` and a bare `!` are errors; `a&b` is one bare word), for `!`, `&`, `|`, parentheses, implicit AND, bare word = `~u`, quoted/unquoted regex args; all 32 tokens from `py:mitmproxy/flowfilter.py`; case-insensitive by default, `MITMPROXY_CASE_SENSITIVE_FILTERS=1`; MULTILINE for `~h*`/`~meta`/`~comment`, DOTALL for `~b*`; RE2→regexp2 fallback with `MatchTimeout` 100 ms; Python's `$`, which also matches before a final newline, reproduced by a `regexp/syntax` rewrite when `$` is in tail position and by regexp2 elsewhere; differential check of filter evaluation against the pinned Python over the fixture flows.
6. `go:options/`: typed option registry generic over `bool|int|string|[]string|*string|*int` with help/choices/defaults, `changed` subscribers with rollback on `OptionsError`, deferred options, `--set` parsing ported from `py:mitmproxy/optmanager.py:310-347,364-372,398` (sequence accumulation, bare flag = true, `toggle`), `config.yaml`/`config.yml` loading via yaml v4 with upstream precedence; `confdir` default `~/.mitmproxy`; the 29 core options from `py:mitmproxy/options.py`; checked-in lists `testdata/options-upstream.txt` (all 117 upstream names with type and default, extracted at the pinned commit) and `testdata/options-go-only.txt` (`pprof_addr`, `otel_exporter_endpoint`, `local_redirector_path`, `script_max_steps`).
7. `go:command/` registration core only: `Manager` with `Register(name, fn, opts...)` over a name→function table, where `fn`'s first parameter is a `context.Context` that carries the dispatch frame (a function without one is refused at registration), and `Call(ctx, name, args...)`, which runs every call through the `Runner` that `addon.NewManager` installs: under the dispatch lock as a synchronous call, re-entering through a valid frame in `ctx` (`addon.Manager.Call` and `master.Master.Call` are the same call), the command **type identities** (all 14 entries of the `py:mitmproxy/types.py:482-497` `CommandTypes` registry: `Arg`, `Bool`, `Choice`, `Cmd`, `CutSpec`, `Data`, `Flow`, `Flows`, `Int`, `Marker`, `Path`, `Str`, `StrSeq`, `Bytes`, as Go types with a `Name()`), and reflection-based signature checks at registration (`py:mitmproxy/command.py:86-95`); the lexer, argument *parsing/completion* and the `core` commands follow in P1. `go:addon/`: per-hook single-method interfaces for all 46 hooks with upstream names (incl. `error`, `requestheaders`, `responseheaders`), `Manager` (`register`, `load` with `Loader.AddOption/AddCommand`, nested `addons`, `traverse`, `_iter_hooks` semantics, `AddonHalt`), the dispatch domain (§3.1 rules 1–5 and 8: lock, epoch-checked frame token, `update` after flow hooks, `Concurrent` only where rule 4 allows it, `master.Do(ctx, fn)`), `eventsequence.Iterate`; `go:master/` with `Do`.
8. `go:internal/netutil/encoding`: identity/gzip/deflate/br/zstd decode/encode (`py:mitmproxy/net/encoding.py`); `server_spec`, `local_ip`, `free_port`, `check`.
9. `go:internal/testutil/testflow`: `TFlow`, `TReq`, `TResp`, `TClientConn`, `TServerConn` builders mirroring `py:mitmproxy/test/tflow.py`, `tutils.py`; `go:internal/difftest` runner (invokes the pinned Python via `uv`, skips when `uv` is absent).
10. Fuzz targets (hand-made seeds from the vectors; upstream's fuzz tests are hypothesis strategies without corpora): tnetstring, filter parser.

Acceptance criteria
- `go build ./... && go vet ./...` pass on darwin/arm64, linux/amd64, windows/amd64 (cross-compile) in CI; `go-licenses` reports no denylisted licence.
- tnetstring: every vector in `py:test/mitmproxy/io/test_tnetstring.py` ported passes; the float table (at least `0.0`, `1.5`, `1759600000.123456`, `1e16`, `1e-5`, `123456789012345680.0`) matches Python `repr` output recorded in the test; differential: 1,000 random floats formatted by Go equal `repr` from the pinned Python; a dict `{a: 1, b: 2}` serialises with `b` before `a`.
- Flow files: for each `testdata/mitmproxy/flows/*.mitm`, `Read → Write → Read` yields state equal (`go-cmp`) to the first read, and the written file is v21; for `corrupted_gzip_body.mitm` (v21) `Write(Read(file))` is byte-identical to what the pinned Python's own `FlowWriter(FlowReader(file))` produces (the differential job records that reference once; it removes the assumption that the checked-in file equals Python's re-serialisation); every file written by Go is read back by the pinned Python `FlowReader` without error (differential job); each pre-18 `dumpfile-*` returns exactly `<mitmproxy-go version> cannot read files with flow format version N.` (`N` rendered as Python renders it, e.g. `11`, `7`, `(0, 10)`); a hand-made v22 file returns the text with `, please update mitmproxy`; `dumpfile-19.mitm` (v20) migrates and round-trips.
- Filter: all cases from `py:test/mitmproxy/test_flowfilter.py` ported pass; parse error messages for the same malformed inputs are non-empty; a filter with a lookahead compiles through regexp2 and matches; differential: for each of the 32 tokens with one example argument, Go and the pinned Python select the same subset of the fixture flows. Python's `$`, which without MULTILINE also matches before a final newline (deliverable 5): `~b "done$"` matches a flow whose request body is `done\n` and does not match one whose body is `done\n\n`, as upstream's `re` does; `~u "done$"` matches an HTTP flow built with the `TReq` builder whose `path` is set directly to `/done\n` (not through URL parsing) and does not match `/done\n\n`; differential: one row per affected token, that is the ten regex tokens compiled without MULTILINE whose subject can end with a newline (`~b`, `~bq`, `~bs`, `~t`, `~tq`, `~ts`, `~m`, `~d`, `~u`, `~marker`; `_Rex` has no flags unless the token class sets them, `py:mitmproxy/flowfilter.py:219`), each with an argument ending in `$` against a flow whose subject ends with `\n`, and Go and the pinned Python agree on the match. `~src` and `~dst` are excluded because their subject is formatted as `host:port` and cannot end with a newline (`:536,550`); `~h`, `~hq`, `~hs`, `~meta` and `~comment` are excluded because they compile with MULTILINE (`:333,350,365,593,617`), where RE2's `(?m)$` already matches before every newline.
- Options: the 29 core option names, types and defaults equal `py:mitmproxy/options.py` (table test); precedence test through the `(*options.Manager).LoadAll(ctx, setSpecs, confdir, passedFlags)` entry point (the CLI itself arrives in P1; `passedFlags` is a `map[string]any`) with `--set listen_port=1`, `config.yaml: listen_port: 2`, `config.yml: listen_port: 3` and `passedFlags{"listen_port": 4}` resolves to 4, with an empty `passedFlags` to 3, without `config.yml` to 2, without `config.yaml` to 1; a flag absent from `passedFlags` does not override a config value; `--set ignore_hosts=a --set ignore_hosts=b` yields `[a b]`; `--set anticache` yields true; `--set anticache=toggle` flips.
- Hooks: a test addon with all 46 methods is invoked exactly once per `eventsequence.Iterate` event for an HTTP flow with WebSocket, a TCP flow, a UDP flow and a DNS flow, plus one `update` call after each flow hook; one dispatch test per remaining hook group (lifecycle `load/configure/running/done`, connection hooks, TLS hooks, QUIC hooks, `socks5_auth`, `next_layer`) shows each hook is invoked with its documented argument; a re-entrant command inside a hook does not deadlock (5 s timeout); `Concurrent` runs its body while another flow's hook proceeds; `Concurrent` at depth 2 returns an error; a goroutine that uses a stale frame after the lock was released panics in test builds; a `master.Do` callback that calls `options.Set` completes without deadlock.

Verification: `go test ./... -race -count=1`; CI matrix green; `fd -H -I -t d '^\.omc$'` shows only the root.

### P1 — HTTP/1 + TLS proxy core, raw TCP layer, regular/reverse/upstream, mitmdump

**Goal**: `mitmdump` proxies HTTP and HTTPS with hooks, like upstream for HTTP/1 traffic, and relays non-HTTP TCP.

**API as built that every deliverable codes against** (from `go doc` on main): hooks are fired with `(*addon.Manager).Hook(ctx, hook)`, which runs the chain and then `update` for a flow hook in the caller's hold of the dispatch lock; goroutines outside hooks change flows, addon state and options only through `(*master.Master).Do` (or `(*addon.Manager).Do`); a command's first parameter is a `context.Context`, and every `command.Manager.Call` runs through the addon manager's `command.Runner` under the dispatch lock as a synchronous call, so `addon.Concurrent` called with a command's context returns an error; `load` and `configure` are synchronous everywhere, and `addon.Concurrent` is allowed only at dispatch depth 1 outside `load`, `configure`, the `done` of `remove`/`clear` and commands; option changes go through `(*options.Manager).Set`/`Update` with a context, serialised by the caller through `master.Do`; the HTTP data model is `go:httpmsg` (`Request`, `Response`, `Message`, `Headers`), flows are `go:flow` (`HTTPFlow`, `TCPFlow`, `Base.Intercept`/`Resume`/`WaitForResume`/`Kill`), state maps are `go:flow/state` over `go:omap`; hook values for connection and TLS hooks are `go:addon/hookdata` (`Context`, `NextLayer`, `ClientHello`, `TLS`, `ServerConnection`). The per-flow mutex in `flow.Base` is unexported and guards intercept state and the backup only.

Deliverables

1. **Spine (serial first step; nothing in deliverable 5's layer part or deliverables 6–10, 12 and 17 starts coding against the proxy until its first commit is on main).** The contract lives in a new package `go:internal/proxy/layer`, so every layer package imports it without importing the handler; the machinery lives in `go:internal/proxy`. Contents:
   - **Public layer descriptor** in `go:addon/hookdata` (P0 package change; user decision): `hookdata.LayerKind` (one constant per layer type of this phase: `LayerRegular`, `LayerReverse`, `LayerUpstream`, `LayerClientTLS`, `LayerServerTLS`, `LayerHTTP`, `LayerTCP`; later phases add theirs), `hookdata.HTTPMode` (`HTTPModeRegular`, `HTTPModeTransparent`, `HTTPModeUpstream`, as `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/__init__.py` `HTTPMode`), `hookdata.LayerSpec{Kind, HTTPMode, Ignore}` and `hookdata.LayerStack []LayerSpec`, an ordered stack, outermost first. `hookdata.NextLayer.Layer` changes from `any` to `LayerStack`; nil means no decision yet, as upstream's `layer is None`. A stack and not a single descriptor, because upstream's `next_layer` returns pre-built stacks (server TLS over client TLS, `py:mitmproxy/addons/next_layer.py:150-153`; client TLS over HTTP for an explicit proxy, `:412-426`; the reverse-proxy stacks, `:346-410`), and re-asking after each single layer would decide differently: `stack_match` compares the whole layer stack (`:63-72`), so after client TLS a secure web proxy would no longer match the explicit-proxy branch (`:139`) and would get a transparent HTTP layer (`:170`), and `reverse:tls`/`reverse:tcp` carrying HTTP-looking plaintext would get an HTTP layer instead of a TCP relay (`:196`). Go addons outside the module choose layers with these values. `LayerRegular`, `LayerReverse` and `LayerUpstream` are only built as top layers by the Handler; `next_layer` handlers never return them, as upstream's never do.
   - `layer.Layer` (`Run(ctx context.Context, c *layer.Context) error` and `Kind() hookdata.LayerKind`) and a registry `layer.Register(kind, constructor)` / `layer.Build(c, stack hookdata.LayerStack)` that each layer package fills from `init` and that builds the whole stack, each layer getting the next one as its child, so `addons/nextlayer` imports no concrete layer. The innermost layer of a stack that needs a child (a TLS layer whose stack ends with it) runs the next-layer loop again, as upstream's default child `NextLayer` does.
   - `layer.Context`: the hook-visible `*hookdata.Context` (client, server, options, and `Layers`, which holds the `layer.Layer` values of the stack so `addons/tlsconfig` can test the secure-web-proxy stack `[regular, client TLS]` by `Kind`), the client `layer.Conn`, the hook runner, the server pool, the injection channel, the per-proxy `http_fidelity` counter of deliverable 4, and a logger prefixed with the client address as upstream's `log_prefix` (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_servers.py:67`).
   - `layer.Conn` (`net.Conn` plus `CloseWrite() error`; for TLS: `close_notify`, then FIN on the TCP conn, §3.1) and the **recording conn** (`layer.Recorder`: `Buffered`, `Peek`, `StopRecording`; after `StopRecording` reads replay the recorded bytes, then read the socket) used at every handover of §3.1.
   - **Hook runner** `layer.Hooks` with `Fire(ctx, hook addon.Hook) (*layer.Snapshot, error)` and `FireFunc(ctx, prepare func(context.Context) error, hook addon.Hook) (*layer.Snapshot, error)`.
