Both disarm **this connection's** watchdog (a counter, as `py:mitmproxy/proxy/server.py:79-89`) before waiting for the dispatch lock, so lock wait time counts as disarmed; dispatch; for a hook that carries a flow, call `flow.WaitForResume` with the stream's context **after** the lock is released (a disconnect cancels the wait); then re-arm (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_servers.py:69-75`). No layer calls `addon.Manager.Hook` directly. `Fire` and `FireFunc` return a snapshot of the handler-visible flow fields taken inside the hold (user decision), and what a layer sends after a hook (the TCP content as the handlers left it, the HTTP response) is read only from that snapshot, as the rules for every unit below state; this first commit freezes the snapshot's type and contents.
   - **Atomic mutate-then-fire entry point** in `go:addon` (P0 package change): `(*addon.Manager).HookFunc(ctx, prepare func(context.Context) error, hook Hook, finish func(context.Context)) error` runs `prepare`, then the hook chain and `update`, then `finish` in the **same hold** at the same depth as `Hook` (`finish` exists because the snapshot must be taken after `update`, whose handlers may change the outbound fields, and `Hook` is sealed; it receives the dispatcher's current synchronous frame, refreshed after any `addon.Concurrent`, and must not call `addon.Concurrent`), so a layer appends a TCP message or sets `flow.Response` and fires the hook atomically while handlers keep `addon.Concurrent`; a `prepare` error skips the hook. `prepare` runs with a synchronous frame (`inSync(ctx, "HookFunc prepare")`, `go:addon/dispatch.go`), so `addon.Concurrent` called from it returns an error and cannot release the lock between `prepare` and the hook; the hook's handlers get the ordinary depth-1 frame. `Manager.Do` followed by `Manager.Hook` inside it is not used for this, because it re-enters one level deeper (`go:addon/dispatch.go` `enter`: `depth: f.depth + 1`) and `Concurrent` refuses depth > 1.
   - `addon.Config.OnDispatchStart`/`OnDispatchEnd` and their `master.Config` copies stay as process-level callbacks for observability (lock hold-time metrics in P11); their godoc is corrected to say so (P0 package change). The watchdog never uses them, because they fire for every acquisition by any goroutine.
   - **Watchdog** (`go:internal/proxy`): per connection, `tcp_timeout` seconds (UDP 20 s, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/server.py:47`), activity registered on every read and write, disarm counter as above, injectable clock for unit tests.
   - **Next-layer loop** `layer.Next(ctx, c) (layer.Layer, error)` (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layer.py:248-331`): asks `next_layer` on every client or server read, never at start: upstream's mode servers replace the handler's initial ask-on-start `NextLayer` with the mode's top layer (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_servers.py:193-196`), and every later `NextLayer` is created without `ask_on_start` (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/modes.py:27,35,72`, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layer.py:257,278-289`). It asks until a handler has set a non-nil `hookdata.NextLayer.Layer`; a client close before a decision closes the connection (`layer.py:280-285`); the buffered events are replayed into the chosen layer through the recording conn. The chosen `LayerStack` is built whole through the registry (`layer.Build`).
   - **ConnectionHandler** `proxy.Handler`: accepts one client `net.Conn`, sets `connection.Client` state and timestamps, fires `client_connected`/`client_disconnected` (`addon.ClientConnectedHook`/`ClientDisconnectedHook`), builds the top layer from the `hookdata.LayerSpec` its mode server passes in (`LayerRegular` for `regular`, `LayerUpstream` for `upstream`, `LayerReverse` for `reverse`, as `make_top_layer`, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_servers.py:473-490`) through `layer.Build`, and runs it; the mode layers ask for their child with `layer.Next` (deliverable 8). It closes the client socket and every server connection it owns when the top layer returns. Layers read and write the conns they are given, may `CloseWrite` them, and never `Close` a conn they did not open.
   - **Server-connection pool** `layer.ServerPool`, per client connection, keyed as §3.1: `Open(ctx, srv *connection.Server, reuse bool) (layer.Conn, error)` fires `server_connect` (a handler may set `srv.Error`, as `proxyserver`'s self-connect guard does), dials outside the lock through an injectable dialer with `connect_addr` as source, fires `server_connected` or `server_connect_error`, and later `server_disconnected` (`hookdata.ServerConnection`); establishment is single-flight and its waiters block outside the lock; the h2 re-dial rule of §3.1 is implemented and unit-tested with a fake dialer even though h2 lands in P3.
   - **Injection path**: `layer.Injected{Flow flow.Flow; Message any}` (`*tcp.Message` in this phase; WebSocket and UDP messages in P3) delivered to the owning layer through the context's injection channel, which the layer selects on next to its reads; `proxy.Handler.Inject` finds the handler by client connection id through a `proxy.Connections` registry (upstream `inject_tcp`, `py:mitmproxy/addons/proxyserver.py`).
   - Ships with a fake two-layer stack test under `-race` and goleak, the conformance tests every layer package runs (`layertest`: half-close in both directions, replay at handover, no direct `Manager.Hook` call), and `go:docs/compat.md` rows for anything that differs from upstream.
2. **Mode specs, mode servers and the `proxyserver` addon.** `go:internal/proxy/modespec` ports `py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_specs.py` (every mode name including later-phase ones, the `@[host:]port` listen suffix `:103-136`, default ports, `transport_protocol`, `reverse:https`/`reverse:dns` = TCP **and** UDP `:234-239`); `go:internal/proxy/modeserver` runs the TCP instances for regular, reverse and upstream (dual-stack when the listen host is empty) passes each accepted connection's mode `LayerSpec` to `proxy.Handler`, and starts and stops instances when `mode` or `server` change, as upstream (`py:mitmproxy/addons/proxyserver.py:258`; a change of `listen_host` or `listen_port` alone restarts nothing), on its own goroutine and never under the dispatch lock (upstream's `configure` spawns a task, `py:mitmproxy/addons/proxyserver.py:299-300`; `Servers.update` `:55-99`); stopping closes the listeners without waiting for connection goroutines (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_servers.py:268-276`), which need the lock to fire `client_disconnected`; a listen failure on `EADDRINUSE` carries upstream's `Try specifying a different port` hint naming the mode spec with `@<port+2>` (`:260-266`). `go:addons/proxyserver` ports `configure` (`Invalid proxy mode specification: <m> (<e>)`, `Cannot spawn multiple servers on the same address: <addr>`, `:258-288`), `server_connect` with `connect_addr` and the self-connect guard (`Request destination unknown. Unable to figure out where this request should be forwarded to.`, `:376-396`), the `proxyserver.active_connections` command and `inject.tcp` (registered once `command.FlowType` has its binding, deliverable 11). A mode that parses but belongs to a later phase (`transparent`, `socks5`, `wireguard`, `local`, `tun`, `dns`, and the UDP/QUIC reverse schemes) is rejected in `configure` with `OptionsError` text `Proxy mode <spec> is not supported by mitmproxy-go yet.`, listed in `docs/compat.md` until its phase. `proxyserver` implements the server-setup interface that `master.Run` looks up by addon name (deliverable 12). The integration harness `go:internal/proxy/proxytest` maps `*.test` names to its in-process origins through the server pool's injectable dialer and starts the proxy with `ssl_verify_upstream_trusted_ca` set to the origins' CA, so the curl criteria resolve `example.test` and verify the origin's certificate without DNS or a system trust store.
3. `go:certs/`: `CreateCA` (RSA `key_size`, SHA-256, `CN=mitmproxy, O=mitmproxy`, validity `now-2d` to `+10y`, extensions BasicConstraints CA critical / EKU serverAuth / KeyUsage keyCertSign+cRLSign critical / SKI), `DummyCert` (reuses CA key, 199 days, SAN critical when subject empty, AKI = CA SKI, no SKI, optional CRL DP, CN only if < 64 chars), `Store` (keyed by CN/SANs/wildcard forms, FIFO cap 100 `py:mitmproxy/certs.py:451`, own mutex, `certs` option `[domain=]path` with `cert_passphrase`), `FromStore` loading an existing confdir or creating it as `create_store` does (`:563-615`): `mitmproxy-ca.pem` (PKCS#1 key + cert) and `mitmproxy-ca.p12` (key + cert) under a umask of `0o77` added to the process umask (`umask_secret`, `:547-560,576-596`); `mitmproxy-ca-cert.pem`, `mitmproxy-ca-cert.cer`, `mitmproxy-ca-cert.p12` and `mitmproxy-dhparam.pem` (the `DEFAULT_DHPARAM` constant, `:46-47`) under the process umask, as upstream (`:598-615`). Both PKCS#12 files are written with `software.sslmate.com/src/go-pkcs12`'s `Passwordless` encoder (user decision): `mitmproxy-ca.p12` with `Encode`, and the cert-only `mitmproxy-ca-cert.p12` (upstream `serialize_key_and_certificates(key=None, …)`, `:605-613`) with `EncodeTrustStoreEntries` and the friendly name `mitmproxy` (`EncodeTrustStore` would name the entry after the certificate's subject). They differ in structure from the files `cryptography` writes (measured with 50.0.2: an HMAC-SHA-256 MAC keyed from the empty password with 2048 iterations, and `friendlyName` `mitmproxy` on every bag): go-pkcs12's passwordless files carry no MAC, the key file's bags carry `localKeyID` but no `friendlyName`, and the cert-only bag carries the Java trust-store attribute (`2.16.840.1.113894.746875.1.1`). These differences are listed in `docs/compat.md`; what must hold is that Python reads both files. An in-memory CRL as `dummy_crl` (`:407`).
4. `go:internal/http1`: request/status line and header parsing with obs-fold join `\r\n ` (`py:mitmproxy/net/http/http1/read.py:166-248`), body readers (chunked, content-length, HTTP/1.0 until close; `Transfer-Encoding` vs `Content-Length` precedence as upstream), trailers, assembly from raw fields (`assemble.py`). The `http_fidelity` counter is per proxy instance, carried in `layer.Context` (so a test's count does not depend on other tests in the binary), exported to slog now and to OTel in P11. This deliverable lands at step 0, before `layer.Context` exists, so it exports the counter type itself, which its parse and assembly functions take as a parameter, and deliverable 1 adds a field of that type to `layer.Context`. It counts each place where the proxy itself emits head bytes that differ from the bytes it read for a message no addon changed: an obs-fold line joined into one value, a request or status line or header block re-assembled from parsed fields in a different byte form (case, whitespace around `:`, line endings), and a `Content-Length` or `Transfer-Encoding` header the proxy rewrote itself. A change made by an addon is not a normalisation and is not counted.
5. `go:tlsparse` + `go:internal/proxy/layers/tlslayer` (TLS mechanics only; the configuration belongs to deliverable 6): ClientHello parser ported from `py:mitmproxy/net/tls.py`, `py:mitmproxy/tls.py` and `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/tls.py:32-71` (`StartsLikeTLSRecord`, `HandshakeRecordContents`, `GetClientHello`, `ParseClientHello`; SNI, ALPN, cipher suites, extensions in order) over the recorded multi-record handshake; `hookdata.ClientHello` gains `ClientHello *tlsparse.ClientHello` (P0 package change; upstream `ClientHelloData.client_hello`, `py:mitmproxy/tls.py:113-133`). The client and server TLS layers fire `tls_clienthello`, `tls_start_client`/`tls_start_server` and use `hookdata.TLS.Config` exactly as the handlers left it; a nil `Config` logs `No TLS context was provided, failing connection.` at error level and closes that connection (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/tls.py:276-281`); server-TLS-first per §3.1 with non-fatal failure (`:606-617`); `ignore_connection` replays the recorded ClientHello into a raw TCP layer (`:600`); after a handshake the layer fills the connection fields (`TLSVersion`, `Cipher`, `ALPN`, `CertificateList`, `SNI`, `ALPNOffers`, `TimestampTLSSetup`) and fires `tls_established_*` or `tls_failed_*`; half-close per §3.1.
6. `go:addons/tlsconfig` (`py:mitmproxy/addons/tlsconfig.py`), which builds every `*tls.Config` under the dispatch lock in the TLS start hooks, as upstream builds the context in `tls_start_client` (`:210-262`, certificate at `:220`) and returns early when a user addon already set one (`:212-213`): certificate selection from `certs.Store` (SANs from the upstream certificate's CN/SANs/O when `upstream_cert` is on, the client SNI and the server address), `add_upstream_certs_to_client_chain`, ALPN per §3.1 (forced `http/1.1` for the secure web proxy, `:254-262`; `NextProtos` set to exactly one protocol or left empty), `tls_clienthello` setting `EstablishServerTLSFirst` (`:204-208`), `tls_version_*`, `tls_ecdh_curve_*` and `request_client_cert` mapped onto `crypto/tls` with a documented table of unmappable OpenSSL names (§1.4 losses), client certificates from a file or `<dir>/<sni>.pem`, `ssl_insecure` and `ssl_verify_upstream_trusted_*`, `SSLKEYLOGFILE` master-secret logging, and CRL serving from its `request` hook at `/mitmproxy-<ca serial>.crl` with `application/pkix-crl` and the CRL distribution point copied from the upstream certificate (`:582-645`). `ciphers_client`/`ciphers_server` accept only colon-separated exact OpenSSL suite names (user decision): each name maps to a `crypto/tls` suite, and anything else (an alias, `!`, `+`, `-`, `@STRENGTH`, an unknown or unsupported name) fails `configure` with an `OptionsError` naming the entry; IANA and OpenSSL suite names are mapped both ways, and the connection's `cipher` and `cipher_list` fields record OpenSSL names, as upstream reports them; listed in `docs/compat.md`. Until the h2 layer lands (P3), ALPN is computed as if `http2` were false on both sides, listed in `docs/compat.md`.
7. `go:internal/proxy/layers/tcplayer` (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/tcp.py`; user decision on the name, with `udplayer` and `dnslayer` in later phases): raw relay with `TCPFlow` messages, `tcp_start/message/end/error` hooks, `ignore` fast path (no flow, no hooks, used by `ignore_connection`), injection, half-close via `CloseWrite`. **One owner goroutine per TCP flow** appends each message and fires `tcp_message` through `Hooks.FireFunc`, then sends the message's content as the handlers left it (`:116-118`); the two reader goroutines only hand bytes to the owner over an unbuffered channel (a reader does not read again until the owner has taken its bytes, which keeps upstream's backpressure, as its reader awaits the handling of each event), so the two directions are serialised through each hook, as upstream's `_server_event_lock` does (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/server.py:384-389`).
8. `go:internal/proxy/layers/modes` (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/modes.py`): `DestinationKnown` base (server connect before the first byte with `connection_strategy=eager`, `:45-60`), `ReverseProxy` for schemes `http`, `https`, `tls`, `tcp` (the UDP side of `reverse:https`/`reverse:dns` lands with P4; `udp` and `dtls` in P3; `http3`, `quic`, `dns` in P4), and `HttpProxy`/`HttpUpstreamProxy`, which only hand over to a next-layer ask, as `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/modes.py:22-36` do (for these modes `nextlayer` answers with the HTTP layer in regular or upstream mode, under client TLS when the client speaks TLS to the proxy, `py:mitmproxy/addons/next_layer.py:412-426`); CONNECT and upstream CONNECT belong to deliverable 10, as upstream keeps them in the HTTP layer.
9. `go:addons/nextlayer` (`py:mitmproxy/addons/next_layer.py`): `ignore_hosts`, `allow_hosts`, `tcp_hosts`, `udp_hosts` (UDP matching becomes active in P3), `rawtcp`, TLS/HTTP detection, the SNI-based host match (`:229-237`), returning `hookdata.LayerStack` values built as upstream builds its stacks: `ignore_connection` gives a TCP layer with `Ignore` unless `show_ignored_hosts` is on (`:127-132`); TLS seen gives server TLS over client TLS (`:150-153`); an explicit HTTP or upstream proxy gives client TLS (when the client speaks TLS to the proxy) over the HTTP layer in regular or upstream mode (`:412-426`); a reverse proxy gives the stack of its scheme (`:346-410`): `http` is client TLS when TLS is seen over HTTP, `https` is server TLS, client TLS when seen, then HTTP, `tcp` is client TLS when seen over TCP, `tls` is server TLS, client TLS when seen, then TCP (the UDP, QUIC and DNS schemes follow in P3 and P4); reverse modes use the transparent HTTP mode, as upstream does; the remaining decisions (`tcp_hosts`, ALPN, `rawtcp`, the HTTP default) give single layers (`:160-196`). Host patterns are compiled with `filter/regex.Compile(pattern, regex.IgnoreCase)` (upstream `re.compile(x, re.IGNORECASE)`, `:84-98`). A host pattern that needs the regexp2 fallback is accepted (user decision). A match that regexp2 abandons at its 100 ms limit is logged with the pattern and host and counts as a **match** for `ignore_hosts` and as **no match** for `allow_hosts`, so a slow pattern never causes interception that the user tried to prevent; it counts as no match for `tcp_hosts` and `udp_hosts`. This needs a match call that reports abandonment, which `regex.Matcher` does not have (it returns only a bool): `go:filter/regex` gains `MatchStringReport(m Matcher, s string) (matched, abandoned bool)`, where `abandoned` is always false for an RE2 matcher, a P0 package change owned by this deliverable. `regex.MatchTimeout` is a constant and `regex.SetLogger` installs one package-level `Logger`, so the deliverable also adds `MatchStringReportTimeout(m Matcher, s string, limit time.Duration) (matched, abandoned bool)`, which `MatchStringReport` calls with `MatchTimeout` and the criterion calls with a short limit; neither reports to the package `Logger`, so an abandoned host match is logged once, by `nextlayer`. Sniffing stops at 64 KiB of client data for the HTTP host search and at the declared ClientHello length capped at 64 KiB; beyond the cap the connection is handed to a raw TCP layer and the decision is logged (upstream has no cap; here every re-ask runs under the global dispatch lock); the cap is listed in `docs/compat.md`.
10. `go:internal/proxy/layers/httplayer` (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/__init__.py`, `_http1.py`, `_base.py`, `_events.py`, `_hooks.py`, `_upstream_proxy.py`): event vocabulary and endpoint interfaces that the stream state machine and the HTTP/1 endpoints meet at (so the P3 h2/h3 endpoints plug in without touching the stream machine); `HttpStream` with `requestheaders`/`request`/`responseheaders`/`response`/`error` hooks, the stream sets `flow.Response` and fires through `Hooks.FireFunc`; HTTP/1 client-facing and server-facing endpoints with keep-alive, pipelining, HTTP/1.0 read-until-close and the pipe handover after 101 or a CONNECT 200 (`_http1.py:152-170,465-472`); error responses as `make_error_response` (`Server: mitmproxy-go <version>` where upstream sends `mitmproxy <version>`, listed in `docs/compat.md`; `Connection: close`, `Content-Type: text/html`, body from `format_error`, `_http1.py:483-496`, `_base.py:43-60`) with the status mapping of `ErrorCode.http_status_code` (`_events.py:81-125`); streaming (`stream_large_bodies`, `store_streamed_bodies`, `body_size_limit` with 413; `check_body_size` sets the stream flag, `:568-650`); for a streamed response `responseheaders` fires before the first body byte is relayed and `response` fires once after the last byte (`:441-475,505-515`); request streaming with the same rules (`:290-347`); `keep_host_header`, `connection_strategy`, `Expect: 100-continue` (handled by the stream state machine, not automatically), 1xx passthrough, upgrade handoff points (P3), plain HTTP through an upstream proxy (absolute-form request with `Proxy-Authorization` from `upstream_auth`) and the refusal text `Upstream proxy <addr> refused HTTP CONNECT request: <code> <reason>` (`_upstream_proxy.py:111`), server connection pool use per §3.1. CONNECT handling (the child of a CONNECT tunnel, and of an upstream CONNECT, is decided by `layer.Next`, as upstream creates a `NextLayer` there, `__init__.py:799,823`) with the `http_connect`, `http_connected` and `http_connect_error` hooks and `http_connect_send_host_header`, and upstream CONNECT with the `http_connect_upstream` hook and `upstream_auth` on the CONNECT, live here, as upstream keeps them in the HTTP layer (`__init__.py`, `_upstream_proxy.py`). `httpmsg.Message` gains `Stream bool` and `StreamFunc func(chunk []byte) [][]byte` (P0 package change; user decision), the counterpart of upstream's `stream: Callable[[bytes], Iterable[bytes] | bytes] | bool`, which must be set in `requestheaders` or `responseheaders` (`py:mitmproxy/http.py:247-259`): a non-nil `StreamFunc` streams the body and maps each chunk to the chunks sent on, and it is called once more with an empty chunk at the end (`__init__.py:317-341,447-471`). The function runs **under the dispatch lock**, as upstream runs it on its event loop: the stream goroutine takes the lock once per chunk, so a streamed body with a function costs one lock acquisition per chunk and holds up other connections' hooks for the function's run time, while a body streamed with `Stream` alone takes the lock only for its hooks. The function receives no context, so it must not change options, call commands or fire hooks (each would wait for the lock it runs under); this is listed in `docs/compat.md`. Neither field is part of the flow state, as upstream does not save `stream`.
11. `go:command/` completion and `go:addons/core`: `command.FlowType` binds to `flow.Flow` and `command.FlowsType` to `[]flow.Flow` (P0 package change; `command` may import `flow`, which imports neither `command` nor `addon`); the 16 `core` commands (`py:mitmproxy/addons/core.py:23-276`) and `core.configure`'s checks, every command running under the dispatch lock as a synchronous call. The lexer (`py:mitmproxy/command_lexer.py`), the argument type system with parsing and completion (`py:mitmproxy/types.py`) and `Execute(ctx, cmdline)` land in this phase (user decision). Upstream resolves a flow-spec argument (`@all`, `@focus`, `~q`) through `view.flows.resolve`, the P2 `view` addon; until that addon is registered, parsing a `flow` or `flow[]` argument fails with an error that names the missing `view.flows.resolve` command and the argument. `Execute` parses the line and calls the command through `command.Manager.Call`, which, as built, runs every call through the addon manager's `Runner` under the dispatch lock, so a command line needs no other path.
12. **Startup, shutdown and flow loading** (`go:master` P0 package change, plus `go:addons/{errorcheck,readfile,keepserving}`): `master.Run` gains upstream's sequence (`py:mitmproxy/master.py:54-92`): `errorcheck` check, server setup on its own goroutine (never under the dispatch lock) raced against shutdown, `errorcheck`, the `running` hook, `errorcheck` and `finish`, wait for shutdown, `done` even when `running` failed; its first commit defines the interfaces `master.Run` looks up by addon name, as upstream looks up `errorcheck` and `proxyserver` (`:62-88`): an errorcheck interface (shut down when an error was logged, and finish) and a server-setup interface taking a context, which deliverable 2's `proxyserver` implements; `errorcheck` never calls `os.Exit`: it makes `Run` return an error that carries exit status 1, which `cmd/mitmdump` turns into the process's exit status, so tests observe it without the process exiting. `master.LoadFlow` ports `load_flow` including the reverse-mode retarget of HTTP flows (`:125-145`) and fires each `eventsequence.Iterate` event through `Manager.Hook`. `errorcheck` exits with status 1 and `Error logged during startup, exiting...` (`py:mitmproxy/addons/errorcheck.py:28-46`). `readfile` reads on its own goroutine started by `running` (`py:mitmproxy/addons/readfile.py:70-86`), calls `LoadFlow` per flow, supports `-` for stdin and `readfile_filter`, and registers `readfile.reading`. `keepserving` polls `readfile.reading`, `replay.client.count`, `replay.server.count` and `proxyserver.active_connections` (`py:mitmproxy/addons/keepserving.py:22-31`) and reads `client_replay`/`server_replay` only when the option is registered (`Options().Has`), treating `command.ErrUnknownCommand` as zero, because those names belong to P2 and `mitmdump -nr` must exit in P1.
13. Output and storage addons: `dumper` (`flow_detail` 0–4, `dumper_filter`, `dumper_default_contentview`, the `tcp_message`, `tcp_error` and `http_connect_error` output, `py:mitmproxy/addons/dumper.py:255-385`; colour only when the output is a terminal, as `vt_codes.ensure_supported` decides, `:52,89-92`; contentview output is uncoloured until P7), `termlog` (`termlog_verbosity`), `save` (`save_stream_file` with `+` append, strftime path and rotation when the formatted name changes with directories created, `py:mitmproxy/addons/save.py:88-103`; exit 1 on a write error, `:104-121`; `save_stream_filter`; the `save.file` command once `FlowsType` is bound, `:132-133`). Writes by `dumper`, `termlog` and `save` to stdout, stderr or a file block under the lock by design, as upstream's do on its loop (§3.1 rule 7 exemption).
14. Access and policy addons: `block` (`block_global`, `block_private`), `proxyauth` (basic, htpasswd and LDAP; `socks5_auth` wired in P5; the LDAP bind runs inside `addon.Concurrent`), `go:internal/htpasswd` ported from `py:mitmproxy/utils/htpasswd.py` onto `golang.org/x/crypto/bcrypt` and `crypto/sha1`, accepting exactly upstream's formats (`$2y$`/`$2b$`/`$2a$` bcrypt and `{SHA}`, refusing anything else at load, `:32-38`; user decision), `upstream_auth`, `browser`.
15. In-process apps: `go:addons/apphost` (user decision on the name; the counterpart of `py:mitmproxy/addons/asgiapp.py`, serving an `http.Handler` from the `request` hook inside `addon.Concurrent`), `onboarding` + `onboardingapp` (mitm.it page and certificate downloads; static assets copied byte-exact from `py:mitmproxy/addons/onboardingapp/static` with the MIT notice, templates translated to `html/template`; user decision), and `/cert/magisk` building `mitmproxy-magisk-module.zip` per `py:mitmproxy/utils/magisk.py` in `go:internal/magisk`, including the certificate file name from OpenSSL's `subject_hash_old` (MD5 of the DER subject, `:88-93`).
16. `go:contentviews/` registry + `raw`, `hex_dump` (16 bytes/line + ASCII) and `hex_stream` (lowercase hex, permissive reencode) with the `rs:mitmproxy-contentviews/src/hex_dump.rs` and `hex_stream.rs` semantics (owned by this phase only), `json`, `xml_html`, `urlencoded`, `query`, `multipart`, `image` (metadata only: hand ports of upstream's four parsers, PNG, GIF, JPEG and ICO, with identical metadata, `py:mitmproxy/contentviews/_view_image/`; user decision), `css` and `javascript` (ports of `py:mitmproxy/contentviews/_view_css.py`, `_view_javascript.py`, whose lookbehind patterns use `github.com/dlclark/regexp2` with the 100 ms match timeout; a timed-out match leaves the area unmarked and is logged; user decision), `auto` priority selection, `content_view_lines_cutoff`.
17. `go:cmd/mitmdump` with `go:internal/tools/cmdline` and `go:internal/tools/dump`: flags generated from the option registry with `make_parser` semantics (`--x`/`--no-x` for booleans, repeatable for sequences, short names and metavars from `py:mitmproxy/tools/cmdline.py`; an option of an addon not yet ported has no flag, as upstream skips an absent option, `py:mitmproxy/optmanager.py:412-418`), `--options`, `--commands`, `--set`, `-q` and `-v` mapped to `termlog_verbosity`/`flow_detail` (`py:mitmproxy/tools/main.py:27-34`), positional filter arguments setting `save_stream_filter`, `readfile_filter` and `dumper_filter` (`:156-164`), the cobra `completion` subcommand; options applied with `(*options.Manager).LoadAll` inside `master.Do` from `--set`, `config.yaml`/`config.yml` and the passed flags; SIGINT and SIGTERM call `Master.Shutdown`, SIGPIPE is ignored (`:122-138`); the DumpMaster addon set of this phase (`py:mitmproxy/tools/dump.py`, `py:mitmproxy/addons/__init__.py`). `--version` prints three lines, `Mitmproxy-go: <version>`, `Go: <runtime.Version()>` and `Platform: <GOOS/GOARCH>`, with no OpenSSL line (user decision; upstream prints `debug.dump_system_info()`, listed in `docs/compat.md`). `-s` arrives with P10 (the `scripts` option belongs to the script addon).
18. **Items carried over from P0.** (a) Decoded bodies are bounded on every `httpmsg.Message` `Content`/`ContentOrRaw`/`Text`/`TextOrRaw` path (today they decode without a limit and are reached from network data under the dispatch lock through `~b*` filters, contentviews at `flow_detail` ≥ 3 and the dumper) by the Go-only option `content_decode_limit` (type `str`, default `256m`, parsed with `internal/human.ParseSize`; user decision): decoding goes through `internal/netutil/encoding.DecodeLimit`, and a body whose decoded size would exceed the limit counts as undecodable, so `Content` and `Text` return an error and `ContentOrRaw`/`TextOrRaw` return the raw bytes, as for any other undecodable body. The `core` addon registers the option and its `configure` publishes the limit to `httpmsg` through a package-level atomic setting (`httpmsg.SetDecodeLimit`), whose default is the same 256 MiB, so code that runs without the addon is bounded too. The setting is process-global: two Masters in one process share it, the last `configure` winning (listed in `docs/compat.md`); `core`'s `done` restores the 256 MiB default; a test that sets it does not call `t.Parallel` and restores the previous value with `t.Cleanup`. The option is added to `testdata/options-go-only.txt` and listed in `docs/compat.md` (upstream has no bound). No streaming zstd decoder in this phase: `DecodeLimit` as built allocates up to about 48 MiB per zstd call at small limits, recorded in R8. (b) Host patterns as deliverable 9 states; flow filters keep the 100 ms regexp2 limit. (c) and (d) landed on main before this phase started: command help wraps to 70 columns through `go:internal/textwrap` (6747459), and the four Python-repr helpers are one `go:internal/pyrepr` (069c588), which the dumper uses. (e) The command lexer and `Execute` land in deliverable 11.
19. Fuzz targets: http1 read/assemble/chunked, TLS ClientHello parser (`GetClientHello`, `ParseClientHello`), command lexer (seeds from the P1 vectors and `py:test/mitmproxy/net/test_tls.py` data).

Acceptance criteria (each names its inputs, what is observed and the pass condition; integration tests run under `-tags integration` with real sockets on `127.0.0.1:0`; every wait has a deadline that fails with a goroutine dump instead of hanging, and no criterion asserts an upper time bound below 10 s on a real clock; each addon criterion also requires that addon's rows of the option table test)

- *Spine (1)*: with `net.Pipe` peers and an injected clock: a fake two-layer stack hands over with every recorded byte replayed exactly once, fed one byte per `Read`; a `prepare` passed to `HookFunc` and the hook it precedes run in one hold: with handlers that do not call `addon.Concurrent`, a concurrent `Master.Do` callback never observes the prepared state without the hook having run; separately, a handler of a hook fired through `HookFunc` can call `addon.Concurrent` (which releases the lock, so the first property is not asserted for it), and `addon.Concurrent` called inside `prepare` returns an error wrapping `addon.ErrSyncContext`; 50 concurrent `ServerPool.Open` calls for one key dial once; in regular mode the first `next_layer` call happens when the client's first bytes arrive, never before; `go test -race` with goleak passes; a grep in the integration script finds no `.Hook(` call under `internal/proxy/layers/`.
- *Watchdog (1), unit*: injected clock, `tcp_timeout=5`: a connection idle for 5 s of fake time expires; a connection whose hook runs for 30 s of fake time, or whose flow waits intercepted for 30 s, does not expire, and expires 5 s after the hook returns or the flow resumes.
- *Watchdog (1), integration, negative*: `tcp_timeout=1`; connection A is idle; connection B sends requests in a loop whose `request` hook sleeps 200 ms; connection C holds an intercepted flow. Observed: A is closed by the proxy (deadline 10 s); at the moment A's close is observed, B and C are still open; C completes after `flow.Resume` through `master.Do`. A design that disarms on any hook in the process fails the first condition, one that never disarms fails the second.
- *Intercept (1, 10)*: `tcp_timeout=1`, a flow intercepted for 4 s: still open after 4 s, completes after resume (a lower time bound only).
- *Mode specs and servers (2)*: every case of `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/test_mode_specs.py` (all specs, error texts); the TCP-mode cases of `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/test_mode_servers.py` and `py:test/mitmproxy/addons/test_proxyserver.py`, each upstream test function mapped to one Go case or listed as not applicable with a reason; two `regular@8080` modes give `Cannot spawn multiple servers on the same address: *:8080` as upstream formats it; a `mode` change at runtime through `master.Do` starts the new listener and stops the old one while a connection on the old listener completes; a request whose destination is the proxy's own listen address gets the error response of a failed server connection carrying the `Request destination unknown` text, and no connection to the proxy itself is opened; `--mode transparent` fails `configure` with the not-supported text.
- *Certificates (3)*: all cases of `py:test/mitmproxy/test_certs.py`; `certs.FromStore` loads `testdata/mitmproxy/confdir/` (upstream-generated) and a leaf it issues for `example.test` verifies against that CA with `crypto/x509`; differential (`difftest`): a confdir created by Go is loaded by the pinned Python (`certs.CertStore.from_store`) and a leaf Python issues from it verifies, both Go-written `.p12` files load with the pinned Python's `pkcs12.load_pkcs12(data, None)` and recover the same certificate and key: the key file gives `.key` equal to the CA key and `.cert.certificate` equal to the CA certificate (DER), and the cert-only file gives no key, `.cert` None and the CA certificate as the single entry of `additional_certs`, as Python's own cert-only file loads (verified with `cryptography` 42.0.0 and 50.0.2, both ends of the pinned range); bag structure, MAC and the key file's `friendlyName` are not compared; on unix, `mitmproxy-ca.pem` and `mitmproxy-ca.p12` have no group or other permission bits, and the four certificate-only files have the mode the process umask gives, as upstream; the `certs` option `[domain=]path` and `cert_passphrase` load the `testdata/mitmproxy/servercert/*` and `clientcert/*` fixtures.
- *HTTP/1 parser (4)*: every case of `py:test/mitmproxy/net/http/http1/test_read.py` and `test_assemble.py` (header case, order, obs-fold, chunked, content-length edge cases, invalid inputs); differential: a seeded generator (seed logged) produces 500 request heads covering obs-fold, whitespace around `:`, empty values, absolute/authority/asterisk targets and invalid versions, and for each head Go and the pinned Python `mitmproxy.net.http.http1.read` either both reject it or both return the same (method, target, version, raw header list); the S2 corners (duplicate `Content-Length` with different values, chunk extensions, `HTTP/1.0` with keep-alive, 204 with a body, obs-fold inside `Set-Cookie`) are fixed rows.
- *Byte fidelity (4, 10)*: a raw client sends a request with headers `X-Foo`, `x-bar`, `X-FOO` in that order, byte by byte, through the in-process DumpMaster set to a raw-socket origin that records what it receives; the origin's bytes equal the client's header block; same for the response direction; the proxy's `http_fidelity` counter is 0 after the exchange. A second row sends an obs-folded header and asserts a counter of 1 and the joined value upstream produces.
- *TLS mechanics (5)*: the ClientHello cases of `py:test/mitmproxy/net/test_tls.py`, `py:test/mitmproxy/test_tls.py` and `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/layers/test_tls.py` (each upstream test function mapped to a Go case or listed as not applicable with a reason); a hand-built ClientHello of more than 4 KiB split across at least three TLS records, and the same message split into records of at most 512 bytes, each fed one byte at a time to `GetClientHello`, yield the complete message, and `tls_clienthello` sees its SNI and ALPN list; differential: SNI, ALPN list, cipher list and the (type, raw body) extension list of these hellos equal what the pinned Python's `ClientHello` reports; a `tls_start_client` handler that leaves `Config` nil produces the `No TLS context was provided` log line and closes the client connection; `close_notify` followed by FIN is observed on the raw side of a half-closed TLS conn.
- *TLS end to end (5, 6, 7, 9)*: `curl -x http://127.0.0.1:<port> http://example.test/` and `curl --cacert <confdir>/mitmproxy-ca-cert.pem -x … https://example.test/` against in-process origins succeed with `request` and `response` hooks observed (skipped with its reason when `curl` is absent; the CI image has it); a `tls_clienthello` handler that sets `IgnoreConnection` makes the origin receive the client's original ClientHello bytes, also when the hello spans three records, and no flow is created and no `tcp_*` hook fires (upstream hands the connection to `tcp.TCPLayer(ignore=True)`, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/tls.py:600`); with `connection_strategy=eager` and an origin that refuses TLS, the client handshake completes and the log contains `Unable to establish TLS connection with server`.
- *tlsconfig (6)*: every case of `py:test/mitmproxy/addons/test_tlsconfig.py` (SAN set from upstream certificate, SNI and server address; client certificates; option mapping including the unmappable-names table; CRL path); `ciphers_client=ECDHE-RSA-AES128-GCM-SHA256:ECDHE-RSA-AES256-GCM-SHA384` restricts the client side to those two suites, `ciphers_client=HIGH:!aNULL` fails with an `OptionsError`, and after a handshake on `TLS_ECDHE_RSA_WITH_AES_128_GCM_SHA256` the client connection's `cipher` is `ECDHE-RSA-AES128-GCM-SHA256`; a table test covers every branch of the §3.1 ALPN rule; integration: a client offering `h2,http/1.1` through the regular proxy negotiates `http/1.1` and the origin receives an offer without `h2`; a client that speaks TLS to the proxy itself (`[regular, client TLS]`) negotiates `http/1.1`; a leaf generated for `https://example.test` has the SAN set of the origin certificate; the CRL URL returns `application/pkix-crl` with a DER body that parses as a CRL signed by the CA.
- *TCP layer (7)*: every case of `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/layers/test_tcp.py`; `reverse:tcp://<echo>` relays a non-HTTP stream with one `tcp_message` per direction per read; a `tcp_message` handler that rewrites `Messages[len-1].Content` changes the bytes the peer receives; with both directions sending at once under `-race`, every `tcp_message` handler sees as the last message the one that triggered it; `inject.tcp` delivers a message in each direction; both directions half-close cleanly (goleak).
- *Modes (8)*: the regular, reverse and upstream cases of `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/layers/test_modes.py`; `reverse:https://origin` forwards `Host: origin` by default and the client's `Host` with `keep_host_header=true`; with `connection_strategy=eager` the reverse server connection is open before the client sends a byte; upstream mode over CONNECT: the upstream proxy receives the exact line `CONNECT host:443 HTTP/1.1` and `Proxy-Authorization: Basic <base64(upstream_auth)>`, and `http_connect_upstream` fires; upstream mode over plain HTTP: the upstream proxy receives the absolute-form request line with `Proxy-Authorization`; an upstream proxy that answers CONNECT with 407 yields the `refused HTTP CONNECT request` text.
- *Layer stacks (1, 9, 10)*, integration: (i) a secure web proxy (the client speaks TLS to the proxy, ALPN `http/1.1`) gets the HTTP layer in regular mode under client TLS, so a CONNECT and an absolute-form request inside the TLS session are handled as by a regular proxy (`next_layer.py:139,412-426`), not as by a transparent one; (ii) `reverse:tls://` and `reverse:tcp://` with a client that speaks TLS and then sends plaintext that looks like HTTP relay the bytes through the TCP layer with `tcp_message` fired (`:346-410`), and no HTTP hook fires; (iii) differential: an addon that counts `next_layer` invocations per connection, run in Go and in the pinned Python over the same scripted connections (regular HTTP, regular HTTPS through CONNECT, secure web proxy, `reverse:https`, `reverse:tls`, `reverse:tcp`), records the same count for each; the scripted client writes one protocol message at a time and waits for the reply before the next, so read boundaries, on which the count depends, are the same in both runs.
- *nextlayer (9)*: every TCP, TLS and HTTP case of `py:test/mitmproxy/addons/test_next_layer.py` as `hookdata.LayerStack` comparisons (the UDP, QUIC and DNS cases are listed by name for P3/P4, not skipped); a client that sends 64 KiB without a line end is handed to the raw TCP layer and the log says so; with a backtracking pattern and a host built to exceed the regexp2 limit (the matcher is driven through `MatchStringReportTimeout` with a limit the test sets, so no wall-clock bound is asserted), the abandoned match passes the connection through untouched when the pattern is in `ignore_hosts`, also passes it through when it is in `allow_hosts`, relays nothing as raw TCP when it is in `tcp_hosts`, and logs one line naming the pattern each time.
- *HTTP layer (10)*: the scenarios of `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/test_http.py` and `test_http1.py` re-expressed over `net.Pipe`-backed peers (as P3 does for `test_http2.py`), each upstream test function mapped to one Go case or listed as not applicable with a reason (h2-only and HTTP/3 cases move to P3/P4 by name); an unreachable origin yields status, headers and body equal to upstream's `make_error_response(502, …)` for the same message, except the `Server` value, which is `mitmproxy-go <version>`; `body_size_limit=1k` with a 2 KiB request body yields upstream's 413 response.
- *Streaming (10)*, integration, built with `integration && !race`, not `t.Parallel`, with an in-process client and origin that never buffer (a discard reader and a fixed write buffer): a 100 MiB response with `stream_large_bodies=1m` is relayed; `responseheaders` fires before the first body byte reaches the client and `response` fires once after the last byte with `response.RawContent` nil (`store_streamed_bodies=false`); peak `/memory/classes/heap/objects:bytes` from `runtime/metrics`, sampled every 50 ms from a baseline taken after `runtime.GC()`, stays below 50 MiB; the same for a 100 MiB **upload** with `stream_large_bodies=1m`; a `responseheaders` handler that sets an upper-casing `StreamFunc` changes the bytes the client receives, and a `requestheaders` handler that sets one changes the bytes the origin receives, in both cases with the final empty-chunk call observed once; a unit test with a `StreamFunc` asserts that the function runs while the dispatch lock is held (a `master.Do` callback started during the call completes only after it returns).
- *Commands and core (11)*: every case of `py:test/mitmproxy/addons/test_core.py` and the flow-free cases of `py:test/mitmproxy/test_command.py`; `command.Manager.Register` accepts a function taking `[]flow.Flow` and `Call` passes the flows through; every case of `py:test/mitmproxy/test_command_lexer.py` and `py:test/mitmproxy/test_types.py` whose argument types exist in this phase (the flow-spec cases are listed by name for P2); `Execute(ctx, "flow.kill @all")` without the `view` addon returns the error naming `view.flows.resolve`; differential: a corpus of command lines lexed by the pinned Python `command_lexer` and by Go gives the same tokens.
- *Startup and loading (12)*: every case of `py:test/mitmproxy/addons/test_errorcheck.py`, `test_readfile.py`, `test_keepserving.py` and `py:test/mitmproxy/test_master.py`; an error logged during `configure` makes the process exit with status 1 and print `Error logged during startup, exiting...`; a listener that cannot bind exits 1 the same way; `running` returns before `readfile` finishes reading a 1,000-flow file (a `running` handler that records the time is observed while flows still arrive); a `request` handler on flow N calls `addon.Concurrent` and waits inside it until a `master.Do` callback started from the test goroutine has run, the callback observes exactly N flows loaded, and loading then continues to the last flow; in `reverse:https://other` mode a loaded HTTP flow's request host, port and scheme are rewritten as `load_flow` does.
- *Output and storage (13)*: every case of `py:test/mitmproxy/addons/test_dumper.py`, `test_termlog.py` and `test_save.py` (`+` append, rotation on a changed strftime name, write error exit); differential: for each `testdata/mitmproxy/flows/*.mitm` and `--flow-detail` 0 to 4, `mitmdump -nr <file> --flow-detail N` from Go and from the pinned Python, each with stdout and stderr to a pipe (so neither emits colour, `vt_codes.ensure_supported` returns false for a non-terminal), print identical text, and both exit 0, except `corrupted_gzip_body.mitm` at `--flow-detail` 3 and 4, where the pinned Python dumper itself fails with `UnicodeEncodeError` (a surrogate from the raw view's `surrogateescape` decoding reaches the Rust highlighter): that pair is an explicit expected-oracle-failure row of the test, naming the Python exception, and the Go side still exits 0 with the raw view's output; a row whose content Python renders with a view that is not ported in P1 is listed in the test with the view's name and the phase that ports it; `-w out.mitm` written by Go is read by the pinned Python `mitmdump -nr out.mitm` with exit 0 and the same request lines, and a file Python writes is read by Go `-r` and printed by `dumper`.
- *Access and policy (14)*: every case of `py:test/mitmproxy/addons/test_block.py`, `test_proxyauth.py`, `test_upstream_auth.py`, `test_browser.py` and `py:test/mitmproxy/utils/test_htpasswd.py`; `testdata/mitmproxy/htpasswd` loads, a line with an apr1 hash is refused at load as upstream refuses it; proxyauth answers 407 with `Proxy-Authenticate` in regular mode and 401 with `WWW-Authenticate` in reverse mode; LDAP (`integration` tag, ubuntu CI job with an OpenLDAP service container, skipped locally without Docker; user decision): a correct bind is accepted and a wrong password refused; `browser.start` is tested on the three CI operating systems with a recording executable that the test builds and places first on `PATH` under the browser names upstream searches (user decision): the executable records its arguments, and the test asserts the proxy and certificate flags upstream passes and that `done` terminates it.
- *In-process apps (15)*: every case of `py:test/mitmproxy/addons/test_asgiapp.py` (as `apphost`), `test_onboarding.py` and `py:test/mitmproxy/utils/test_magisk.py`; `/cert/pem`, `/cert/p12`, `/cert/cer` and `/cert/magisk` return the confdir files with upstream's content types; the magisk zip built from `testdata/mitmproxy/confdir/` equals `mitmproxy-magisk-module.zip` entry by entry (names and contents); differential: `subject_hash_old` of the fixture CA equals Python's; every static asset is byte-identical to upstream's file.
- *Contentviews (16)*: the cases of `py:test/mitmproxy/contentviews/test__view_{raw,json,xml_html,urlencoded,query,multipart,css,javascript}.py`, `_view_image/test_image_parser.py` and `_view_image/test_view.py` (with the `testdata/mitmproxy/image_parser/*` fixtures), `test__registry.py` and `test___init__.py`; the `#[test]` cases of `rs:mitmproxy-contentviews/src/hex_dump.rs` and `hex_stream.rs`; a css input built to make a regexp2 pattern exceed its limit returns the unmarked text and one log line.
- *mitmdump CLI (17)*: `mitmdump --version` prints exactly the three lines with the build's version, `runtime.Version()` and `GOOS/GOARCH`, and exits 0; every case of `py:test/mitmproxy/tools/test_main.py`, `test_cmdline.py` and `test_dump.py`; a table maps each flag of the generated parser to its option and default and equals the `mitmdump --help` option list of the pinned Python restricted to the options this phase registers; `-q` and `-v` set `termlog_verbosity` and `flow_detail` as upstream; a positional filter sets the three filter options; precedence through `LoadAll` as the P0 test, driven by the binary; `mitmdump completion zsh` (and bash, fish, powershell) prints a script that passes that shell's syntax check, and `mitmdump __complete --` (cobra's dynamic completion protocol, which the generated scripts call instead of embedding the flag list) returns every registered flag; SIGTERM ends a running `mitmdump` with `done` fired and exit status 0; **option table test**: every option registered by the DumpMaster set of this phase equals name, type and default in `testdata/options-upstream.txt`.
- *Carried-over items (18)*: with `content_decode_limit=1m`, a gzip body that decodes to 2 MiB makes `Content` return an error, `ContentOrRaw` return the raw bytes, `~bs` evaluate against the raw bytes and the dumper print the raw body, and the process's heap growth during the call, measured after `runtime.GC()` in a test that does not call `t.Parallel`, stays below 8 MiB plus the decoder's fixed buffers; with the default, the same body decodes; the option table test lists `content_decode_limit` with its type and default; the help wrapping and the single Python-repr helper are covered by the tests that landed with them on main.
- *Fuzz (19)*: each target runs its CI smoke (30 s) without a crash or a goroutine leak.

Verification: `go test ./... -race -count=1`; `go test -tags integration ./internal/proxy/... ./cmd/...`; differential job in CI; fuzz smoke; `fd -H -I -t d '^\.omc$'` shows only the root; manual: a `mitmdump` session log attached to the PR.

#### Execution: contracts and parallel units

Contracts (each frozen by its owner's **first** commit: types, interfaces and godoc, functions whose bodies are trivial, and the conformance tests later implementations must pass; a function with real logic lands only implemented and tested, never as a stub; a later change to a frozen contract is agreed with every consumer before it lands):

| Contract | Package and names | Owner | Consumers |
|---|---|---|---|
| Layer descriptor | `addon/hookdata`: `LayerKind`, `HTTPMode`, `LayerSpec`, `LayerStack`; `NextLayer.Layer` typed as `LayerStack` | deliverable 1 | 9, Go addons, every layer package (its `Kind`) |
| Layer model | `internal/proxy/layer`: `Layer`, `Constructor`, `Register`, `Build(ctx, c, stack)`, `Context` (with `Do`), `Conn`, `Recorder`, `Hooks` (`Fire`, `FireFunc`, both returning `*Snapshot`), `Snapshot`, `ServerPool` (`Open` with `OpenOptions{Reuse, Setup}`, `Upgrade`, `Lookup`), `Dialer`, `Injected`, `Next(ctx, c)`; `internal/proxy`: `Record`, `MaxRecordBytes`, `HookRunner` | deliverable 1 | 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 2 |
| Atomic hook entry | `addon`: `(*Manager).HookFunc(ctx, prepare, hook, finish)`, `HookFlow` | deliverable 1 | 1 (`Hooks.FireFunc`), through it 7 and 10 |
| Handler and registry | `internal/proxy`: `Handler` (taking the mode's top-layer `hookdata.LayerSpec` from its mode server), `Connections`, `(*Handler).Inject` | deliverable 1 | 2 |
| Mode specs | `internal/proxy/modespec`: `Mode`, `Parse`, the per-mode types | deliverable 2 | 8, 9, 12, 17 |
| ClientHello | `tlsparse`: `ClientHello`, `Extension`, `StartsLikeTLSRecord`, `HandshakeRecordContents`, `GetClientHello`, `ParseClientHello`; `hookdata.ClientHello.ClientHello` | deliverable 5 | 6, 9 |
| TLS configuration hand-off | `hookdata.TLS.Config` set by `tlsconfig` in `tls_start_client`/`tls_start_server`, used unchanged by the layer | deliverables 5 and 6 together | 5, 6 |
| Certificates | `certs`: `Cert`, `GeneralName`, `CreateCA`, `DummyCert`, `Entry`, `Store`, `FromStore`, `(*Store).GetCert`, `DefaultDHParam` | deliverable 3 | 6, 15 |
| HTTP/1 parser | `internal/http1`: `ReadRequestHead`, `ReadResponseHead`, `ReadHeaders`, `ExpectedBodySize`, `AssembleRequestHead`, `AssembleResponseHead`, the chunked and length body readers | deliverable 4 | 10 |
| HTTP events and endpoints | `internal/proxy/layers/httplayer`: the request/response event types and the client-facing and server-facing endpoint interfaces | deliverable 10 | 10 (HTTP/1 endpoints), P3 |
| Stream fields | `httpmsg.Message.Stream`, `httpmsg.Message.StreamFunc` | deliverable 10 | 13, P3, P10 |
| Flow command types | `command.FlowType` → `flow.Flow`, `command.FlowsType` → `[]flow.Flow` | deliverable 11 | 2 (`inject.tcp`), 13 (`save.file`) |
| Startup and loading | `master`: `Run` sequence, `LoadFlow` | deliverable 12 | 2, 12, 17 |
| Contentview API | `contentviews`: `View`, `Metadata`, `Registry`, `PrettifyMessage` | deliverable 16 | 13, P2, P7, P8 |
| Integration harness | `internal/proxy/proxytest`: `Start(t, opts...)` returning the in-process proxy (listen address, master, confdir, CA, hook recorder), raw, echo and TLS origins | deliverable 2 | every integration criterion |
| Option registration | each addon registers exactly upstream's name, type, default and help | each addon deliverable | 17 (option table test) |

P0 packages edited in this phase, one owner each (shared files are edited only by their owner, in small commits with tests, landed early): `addon/manager.go` and `addon/dispatch.go` (deliverable 1), `addon/hookdata/hookdata.go` (deliverable 1 for the layer descriptor first, then deliverable 5 for the ClientHello field), `httpmsg` message files (deliverable 10 for the stream fields first, then deliverable 18 for the decode bound), `filter/regex` (deliverable 9), `command` types (deliverable 11), `master/master.go` (deliverable 12), `options` (deliverable 17 for flag generation), `docs/compat.md` and `docs/dependencies.md` (append-only, every deliverable).

Landing order and what may run in parallel:

| Step | Lands on main | What may start after it |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | (nothing; P0 as built) | in parallel at once: 3 certs, 4 http1 parser, 5's `tlsparse` part, 11, 13 (`save.file` once 11's `FlowsType` binding is on main, contentview output once 16's registry is), 14, 16, 17's flag generation, 18 (a)'s option and `core` parts, 19's http1 and ClientHello targets, 2's `modespec` part |
| 1 | spine first commit: the layer model contract, `HookFunc`, the hook runner's interface and conformance tests | 7 tcplayer, 8 modes (once `modespec.Parse` is on main), 9 nextlayer (once `tlsparse.ClientHello` and `modespec.Parse` are on main), 10 httplayer's stream machine (against fake endpoints), 5's layer part, 12 (`LoadFlow` once `modespec.Parse` is on main), 18 (a)'s `httpmsg` edit once 10's stream-field commit is on main (the declared edit order for those files) |
| 2 | spine implementation: `Handler`, watchdog, recording conn, `ServerPool`, `Next`, injection | 2's `modeserver` and `proxyserver` parts and the `proxytest` harness |
| 3 | `tlsparse.ClientHello` with its vectors; `httplayer` event and endpoint types; `certs.FromStore` | 6 tlsconfig; 10's HTTP/1 endpoints (with step-0 deliverable 4 on main); 15 in-process apps |
| 4 | `proxytest` harness | the integration criteria, each written as soon as its pieces are on main |
| 5 | every addon package of deliverables 2, 6, 9, 11, 12, 13, 14, 15 | 17's DumpMaster assembly and option table test |
| 6 | everything above | the mitmdump differential and CLI criteria; phase verification |

Rules for every unit: never wait for connection goroutines while holding the dispatch lock (server stop, `done` and `Clear` do not join them); pool waiters block outside the lock; `WaitForResume` gets the stream's context; a layer writes a flow field that handlers can see only in the `prepare` of `Hooks.FireFunc` or inside `master.Do` (§3.1 rule 6); between hooks it keeps what it has read (a body, a TCP chunk) in its own variables and stores it into the flow in the next hook's `prepare`; `Hooks.Fire` and `Hooks.FireFunc` return a snapshot of the handler-visible flow fields taken inside the hold, and a layer reads what it sends after a hook only from that snapshot (user decision); every layer test runs under `-race` with goleak.


### P2 — Addon parity, HAR, flowjson

Deliverables: `go:flowio/har` import/export (`py:mitmproxy/io/har.py`, `py:mitmproxy/addons/savehar.py` incl. `.zhar` zlib level 9) and `go:flowio/flowjson` (port of `flow_to_json`, `py:mitmproxy/tools/web/app.py`, shared with P8; its DNS branch, which needs the HTTPS-record helpers of `py:mitmproxy/net/dns/https_records.py`, is gated on P4 deliverable 1 and stubbed with an explicit "unsupported flow type" error until then); `flowio.Reader` `{` → HAR branch; addons `anticache`, `anticomp`, `stickycookie`, `stickyauth`, `mapremote`, `maplocal`, `modifybody`, `modifyheaders` (shared `[/flow-filter]/regex/replacement` spec parser from `py:mitmproxy/utils/spec.py`), `blocklist`, `export` (curl/httpie/raw/raw_request/raw_response; clipboard behind the `clipboard` build tag), `cut`, `comment`, `command_history`, `disable_h2c`, `savehar` (`hardump`), `serverplayback` (12 options), `clientplayback` (`client_replay`, concurrency; h2 replays are gated on P3), `update_alt_svc` (`keep_alt_svc_header`, ReverseMode only, `py:mitmproxy/addons/update_alt_svc.py:24-35`; owned here, P4 only adds the HTTP/3 listener it points at), `view` (`view_filter`, `view_order`, 22 commands; needed by web/console) and `eventstore`, `intercept` (`intercept`, `intercept_active`), `server_side_events` (non-default addon, `py:mitmproxy/addons/server_side_events.py`). `strip_dns_https_records` lands wholly in P4 (it only has a `dns_response` hook).

Acceptance criteria: each addon package ports the corresponding `py:test/mitmproxy/addons/test_<name>.py` cases (map-table tests); HAR: for each `testdata/mitmproxy/har_files/<x>.har`, import then serialise each flow with `flowjson` with `id`, `timestamp_created` and both connection ids set to the fixed test values (`py:test/mitmproxy/io/test_har.py:12-31`) equals `<x>.json`; export of each `testdata/mitmproxy/flows/<x>.mitm` with the creator version fixed to `1.2.3` equals `<x>.har` with no other normalisation (`py:test/mitmproxy/addons/test_savehar.py:160-174`); a Go-exported HAR is imported by the pinned Python without error (differential); `serverplayback`: each fixture request replayed unchanged returns the recorded status, headers and body, a request that differs only in a `server_replay_ignore_params` key still matches, an unmatched request is killed when `server_replay_extra=kill` and forwarded when `forward`; `clientplayback` replays an HTTP/1 dump against an `httptest` origin and the origin sees identical requests; the `modify_headers`/`modify_body`/`map_remote`/`map_local` examples from `py:docs/src/content/overview/features.md` behave as documented, each example being one row of the table test; `view` ordering/filtering tests pass; each addon's option names, types and defaults equal upstream's (table test per addon).

### P3 — HTTP/2, WebSocket, raw UDP and DTLS

**Settled dependency handoff**: the in-session, parallel gows unit extends `github.com/zchee/gows` on `frame-api` from `4a8b735`, obtains review, pushes and opens a pull request for the user to merge. The WebSocket unit consumes the reviewed API commit and pins its exact pseudo-version (branch commit first, main commit after merge), updates the dependency manifest and `docs/dependencies.md`, and uses no `replace` directive. There is no interim relay and no alternative WebSocket library. The exported API is frozen by the gows unit's first report and approved by the lead before adapter implementation; the behavior contract below is binding without guessing method signatures. DTLS remains on `github.com/pion/dtls/v3 v3.1.10`, DTLS 1.2 only.

#### Execution: contracts and parallel units

This section is built against main `60157dd`: HTTP orchestration still constructs concrete HTTP/1 endpoints with sequential exchanges; ALPN has a hard-coded HTTP/2-disabled gate; replay rejects h2 in both clientplayback and httplayer. Handler dialing/listeners/recording are TCP/byte-stream-only, snapshots and injection support HTTP/TCP but not UDP/WebSocket, and `hookdata.TLS.Config` is `*tls.Config`. Existing protocol state models are not transports or runnable layers. Each missing integration has one owner below; standalone protocol tests do not close executable integration.

| Unit | Exclusive files / integration ownership | Tests and acceptance owned | Frozen output / waits | Landing order |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Shared protocol contracts | `addon/hookdata`, `internal/proxy/layer` receipt/endpoint/packet declarations, inject.go and Handler snapshot/injection paths; public websocket/udp helpers before their serial transfer | Typed config compatibility; UDP/WebSocket snapshot, clone, identity/direction, bounded injection and receipt cancellation tests | Packet transport, receipt and endpoint descriptors, layer kinds, snapshots; consumers agree before freeze | First, serial; implemented contracts/tests only, no stub logic |
| gows frame relay API | Separate gows repository: frame/connection/reader/writer/compression API and tests | The nine-point contract below and existing message-mode conformance | Exact reviewed API commit/pseudo-version; no proxy dependency | Starts in parallel; consumed before WebSocket adapter |
| h2 protocol engine | `internal/h2` and its dependency adapter file; no import of httplayer | Case-by-case mapping of `test_http2.py`, `_http_h2` buffering, HEADERS fuzzing, credit/padding/error/ping tests | Independent protocol API and original-byte receipts; frozen shared contracts only | Develops after contract freeze; lands before HTTP integration |
| HTTP multi-protocol integration | httplayer implementations, including its h2 adapter, endpoint factories, demux, pool/replay and 101 handoff glue; existing tlsconfig files for ALPN; clientplayback | Version interop, hooks/streaming/concurrency, positive/negative ALPN, h2c, saved h2 replay, head deadlines, bounded loaders, 64 MiB/stall/heap/SSE gates | Negotiated-protocol snapshots and WebSocket unit's frozen handoff interface; h2 engine first | ALPN promotion and working endpoint factories/dispatch land atomically; 101 glue written only by this owner |
| UDP transport and relay | Proxy modeserver/listener/dialer/record/watchdog packet implementations, modes layer, nextlayer UDP/DTLS branch and udplayer | `test_udp.py`, UDP mode/next-layer cases, demux/packet replay, injection, 20 s clock expiry | Per-tuple packet transport, bounded queues and UDP dialing/metadata; frozen contracts first | Packet transport before raw relay and DTLS |
| DTLS parsing/configuration/interception | `tlsparse`, tlslayer DTLS branch, new `addons/tlsconfig/dtls.go`, CertStore-to-pion adapter | `test_tls.py` DTLS vectors, tlsconfig options/SAN/CA/client-cert/version cases, real pion integration and DTLS head deadline | `DTLSConfig`, packet transport, synchronized metadata publication; no edits to existing tlsconfig files | Parser early; full layer after config/packet contracts |
| WebSocket relay | Public `websocket.Fragmentizer`, `internal/proxy/layers/websocket` | `test_websocket.py`, Fragmentizer, buffered 101, controls/compression, real Handler hooks/drop/inject/kill/intercept/races, frame fuzzing, Autobahn harness | gows pin, shared snapshots/injection; frozen 101 handoff interface supplied to HTTP owner | After gows and shared contracts; adapter before executable 101 glue |
| Presentation and executable gates | dumper, dump default registration and `TestAddonOrder`, proxytest helpers, dedicated Linux workflow/harness, filter/regex minor cleanups | h2/WS/UDP dumper cases, Name/default-set registration, executable smoke, complete Autobahn reports and privileged netem | Runnable mitmdump path, isolated gate recipes/artifacts; no skipped required acceptance | Helpers early; final gate after adapters |

#### File ownership and serial transfers

- **Shared protocol contracts is the first and final writer** of `addon/hookdata/**`, `internal/proxy/layer/**`, `internal/proxy/inject.go` and the snapshot/injection paths of `internal/proxy/handler.go`. Consumers implement injected-message delivery only inside their own layer packages against the frozen interface; they never edit these shared files or Handler paths.
- Receipt, endpoint-descriptor and packet-transport declarations live in the neutral `internal/proxy/layer` package, which the h2 engine may import, **never in httplayer**. Shared protocol contracts writes no httplayer file. Endpoint/event ownership declarations here mean this plan's contract text and its adoption by **HTTP multi-protocol integration, the sole writer of every `internal/proxy/layers/httplayer/**` file**, including the h2 adapter and 101 glue.
- Shared protocol contracts first writes clone/snapshot helpers and message identity in public `websocket/**` and `udp/**`. **At the contracts commit's landing on main**, ownership transfers: WebSocket relay becomes the sole writer of `websocket/**` (Fragmentizer in a new file) and UDP transport and relay becomes the sole writer of `udp/**`. No concurrent or later shared-contract edits to those packages.
- **UDP transport and relay** writes `internal/proxy/modeserver/**`, Handler dial/listen/record paths (not snapshot/injection), proxy nextlayer/record/watchdog packet implementations, `addons/nextlayer/**` UDP/DTLS selection, modes-layer packet integration and `internal/proxy/layers/udplayer/**`. **DTLS parsing/configuration/interception** writes `addons/tlsconfig/dtls.go`, `tlsparse/**` and the tlslayer DTLS branch. **HTTP multi-protocol integration** writes existing `addons/tlsconfig/*.go` (excluding the DTLS owner's new dtls.go) and `addons/clientplayback/**`; HTTP and DTLS freeze the helper boundary before either contribution lands. **h2 protocol engine** writes `internal/h2/**`. **WebSocket relay** writes `internal/proxy/layers/websocket/**`.
- **Presentation and executable gates** writes `addons/dumper/**`, `internal/tools/dump/**`, `internal/proxy/proxytest/**`, `.github/workflows/**` and the filter/regex minor cleanups. It is the **one writer of `docs/compat.md` in this phase**. h2, DTLS and WebSocket submit bounded-window, version-rule and compressed-single-frame/Fragmentizer row text through their notes and DONE reports; presentation and executable gates lands all rows in one commit. Providing row text is not permission for those consumers to edit the shared document.

Shared declarations are frozen serially, never edited concurrently with their consumers. All addon-shared state stays under dispatch; no network I/O, wait for connection owners, or unrelated lock is held under dispatch. Every new addon uses upstream's `Name()` and has a registration test alongside the entire default set.

**Shared receipts, snapshots and injection**: one connection Receive owner demultiplexes by stream ID to one flow owner per exchange. The routing identity includes the endpoint/connection identity as well as stream ID, because two upstream connections can each use stream 1. Freeze ordered HEADERS, informational responses, DATA, trailers, END_STREAM, stream reset and connection-error events; the flow owner drives HttpStream, which remains the sole owner of body-limit and streaming decisions. Reject PUSH_PROMISE (no invented pushed flows), acknowledge SETTINGS and respect peer concurrent-stream, frame, header-table and window limits. The h2 adapter lives in httplayer; independent internal/h2 types prevent a package cycle.

Endpoint Send retains the existing **blocking-to-consumption, borrowed-slice** contract: no mutable DATA slice retained after return, and success means all transformed output for that event was written to the peer socket or deliberately dropped, not accepted by a queue. Each flow owner may wait on its send; connection Receive continues independently, with bounded queues and cancellation waking sends. A stream receives an original-byte receipt charged once for the DATA flow-controlled length **including padding**, independent of transformed output length. Buffered body consumption completes after append to the flow body; streaming consumption completes only after **all** transformed output is written or deliberately dropped. Expansion, drop and delayed StreamFunc output work in both request and response directions without granting credit for queued output. Cancellation/RST/GOAWAY/error releases chunks and reservations once, invalidates outstanding receipts and wakes senders; it sends no stream WINDOW_UPDATE on a closed stream.

Extend `layer.Snapshot` with cloned UDP-last-message and HTTPFlow.WebSocket last-message/data fields, including `closed_by_client`, close code/reason and `timestamp_end`. Extend Handler Inject with cloned `*udp.Message` and `*websocket.Message` variants (HTTPFlow identity for WebSocket), identity/type/direction checks and bounded queues; freeze oversize/full/closed error semantics with the consumers. One flow owner receives both directions and injections, publishes/appends via FireFunc, then reads snapshots; socket readers never mutate live flows. This is also the path used by drop/kill/intercept tests, not a direct call to the layer bypassing Handler.

**gows frame/relay contract** (reuses its existing header codec, WithBuffered and extension machinery):
1. Distinct frame/relay mode mutually exclusive with ReadMessage/NextReader/NextWriter/Serve; message-mode defaults and conformance unchanged.
2. Freeze read/write signatures and ownership. Frame results expose opcode, FIN, relevant RSV bits, payload and wire-compressed-versus-decoded status. Payload is borrowed until the next ReadFrame or Abort; the adapter clones it. `Frame.Compressed` derives from the opening RSV1 and Conn carries it to continuations; false on controls or without negotiated compression, illegal RSV1 is a ProtocolError. Writes do not retain borrowed payload after documented completion.
3. Every Ping/Pong/Close is observable: no automatic Pong, swallowed Pong, close echo, hidden drain or unsolicited controls. Validate control FIN/length, opcode, role masking, close code and UTF-8 reason. A valid Close returns `(Frame, nil)` with parsed body available through existing CloseError/helper; malformed close returns ProtocolError. After close, data is a protocol error; transport EOF yields typed peer-closed status, distinct from protocol/transport failure.
4. Mask by endpoint role with fresh keys; serialize complete frame writes; explicit opcode/FIN and zero-length fragments, with controls between continuations. Uncompressed boundary preservation is not a buffer-size hint.
5. Decode inbound compression and encode hook-edited plaintext using the opposite side's negotiated parameters, with separate inbound/outbound dictionaries, window bits and takeover flags; correct resets/trailer removal/RSV1 placement, never compressed controls. **Approved DecodeFrame contract**: non-final compressed frames return `(nil, false, nil)`, FIN returns whole plaintext/complete=true; uncompressed returns payload/complete=FIN. Call it in order for every compressed data frame, never for interleaved controls. Decoded size is bounded by Conn's read limit; overflow returns ProtocolError close code 1009 and frees buffers. Buffered decode does not claim a DEFLATE fragment-to-fragment correspondence; the Fragmentizer deviation is in deliverable 2. WriteFrame compression is selected on the opening fragment; inconsistent continuation compression and opening data during fragmentation are typed usage errors, controls remain legal/uncompressed.
6. HTTP upgrade validates actual request/response extension parameters through existing gows machinery, using approved `ParseCompression(offer, response []string) (CompressionParams, bool, error)` with one value per repeated header line and bool=negotiated. Reject unsolicited/unsupported extensions, duplicate/out-of-range parameters and construct each side explicitly. Test different direction parameters and multi-message compressed/uncompressed mixtures. gows's Dialer extension-validation fix lands as a separate tested commit after its contract.
7. WithBuffered owns/copies pre-read bytes exactly once; test complete/partial frame prefixes after 101 in one read, including masked payload prefixes and compressed fragments.
8. `Abort()` abruptly closes transport with no control write, is idempotent and safe concurrently with blocked reads/writes, freeing buffers exactly once. Conn.Close retains message-mode semantics and returns ErrConnMode in frame mode. Deadline/Abort interrupts I/O and partial-write failures terminate predictably; data after close is rejected while correct relay-owned control completion is allowed. No goroutine leaks or hidden close drain.
9. Conformance: observable raw Ping/Pong with no replies; exact fragmented text/binary/empty uncompressed frames; role violations; absent/valid/invalid close bodies; continuation/control interleaving; compressed fragmented decode/re-encode with/without takeover and differing windows; buffered partial frames; canceled blocked I/O; unchanged existing message-mode tests. Publish exact API and measured results with the dependency commit.

**Carried work and ownership**: HTTP integration owns h1 head and h2 preface/HEADERS read deadlines and the replay loaders' flowio/har `MaxEntries` bound alongside removal of h2 replay gates; DTLS owns ClientHello/head deadlines. A head deadline is separate from the idle watchdog and must expire despite trickled incomplete bytes, with cancellation/connection cleanup tests. Freeze timeout configuration at the shared contract review rather than inventing an upstream option. Gates/cleanup owns the filter/regex anchor-free capture-table check, typed capacity errors and class builder concatenation. Windows privfile ACLs/check-use and the privileged filesystem CI job stay in the next phase, not this network gate.

Deliverables
1. `go:internal/h2` plus the httplayer-owned adapter/driver: **two independent h2 endpoints per proxied connection** (client-facing server endpoint, upstream-facing client endpoint), each with its own SETTINGS, connection and stream windows, stream state table and GOAWAY handling; HEADERS/CONTINUATION assembled by the relay and decoded with its own `hpack.Decoder` (not `ReadMetaHeaders`), one `hpack.Encoder` per direction; DATA re-framed to the peer's `MAX_FRAME_SIZE`. **Advertised SETTINGS**: `MAX_CONCURRENT_STREAMS` 100, `INITIAL_WINDOW_SIZE` 1 MiB, `MAX_FRAME_SIZE` 2^17 (upstream uses 2^31−1 windows and 2^17 frames, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/_http_h2.py:50-67`). **Bounded window policy** (the h2 engine supplies its deviation row to the `docs/compat.md` owner, presentation and executable gates; deviation from upstream's immediate acknowledgement, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/_http2.py:238-240`; confirmed by the user): the connection starts with a 128 MiB receive window (enlarged from the RFC initial 65,535 bytes by WINDOW_UPDATE), returned promptly for each valid DATA frame read off the socket, independently of stream consumption; connection credit does not authorize extra stream credit. Each receive endpoint/direction has its **own** 128 MiB stream-grant budget; these are not one combined full-duplex cap. Original-byte receipts, including DATA padding, follow the shared contract above. A stream's window is returned when the stream **consumes** the data, which means: for a streamed message (`stream_large_bodies`, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/__init__.py:631-649`), the original chunk's full transformed output has been written to the downstream peer's socket or deliberately dropped (both request and response StreamFunc paths); queue acceptance is never consumption, and bytes that have left the receive buffer but still sit in the opposite endpoint's send buffer (`BufferedH2Connection` semantics, below) are still charged to the same Σ counter; for a message buffered for its hooks, the bytes have been appended to the flow's body buffer, which is the flow's content and is bounded by `stream_large_bodies`/`body_size_limit` exactly as on HTTP/1 (`:568-650`), not by this budget (counting only the downstream write there would deadlock every buffered body larger than its stream window, because nothing is sent downstream before the `response` hook); a 128 MiB budget per receive endpoint/direction bounds the **sum of granted (unconsumed) stream windows**: 100 MiB is reserved for the 100 initial windows, and a stream may grow beyond 1 MiB (up to 16 MiB) only by drawing on the 28 MiB headroom, first come first served; **growth trigger**: when the stream's consumer has consumed half of the stream's current window since the window was last set, the window doubles, capped at 16 MiB, or grows by the remaining headroom when less than the doubling increment is left; growth is drawn from the headroom only; reset the consumed-since-set counter when the half-window threshold triggers a growth evaluation, including partial or denied growth, so one consumption receipt cannot repeatedly trigger growth when headroom reopens. Returned stream credit replaces consumed original input credit; it does not charge transformed output. Growth returns to headroom when the stream closes, and the reserved 1 MiB returns when the stream slot is freed; so the invariant is `Σ granted stream windows ≤ 128 MiB` at all times and a stalled or intercepted stream can never consume more than its granted window; for streamed messages, **original received DATA** is bounded by 128 MiB per receive direction (both receive and handoff queues stay charged), and a stalled stream stalls only itself while the shared TCP reader continues. This invariant does not bound arbitrary memory allocated by an addon or StreamFunc expansion; transformed output uses bounded send queues, and the unchanged 160 MiB heap scenario below uses identity streaming with fixed peer buffers; a buffered body is the flow's content and is bounded only by `body_size_limit`, which is unset by default, as upstream's is (`py:mitmproxy/addons/proxyserver.py:176-178`); the endpoint owner maintains Σ without cross-owner locks, asserts `Σ ≤ 128 MiB` on every grant/release transition, and publishes a synchronized immutable snapshot and maximum for tests; observations do not race-read its counter; **chunk hand-off**: each stream's receive buffer is a list of chunks allocated with `MAX_FRAME_SIZE` (2^17 bytes) of capacity and filled by appending, never a contiguous buffer grown by doubling; a full chunk moves to the opposite endpoint's send buffer by ownership, without copying; the partly filled tail chunk moves only when the send buffer is empty and the downstream peer can take data now (the client-facing stream and connection windows both have credit for at least one byte), and it then becomes the send buffer's head and drains as credit allows; otherwise the receiver keeps appending to the tail (an implementation may instead copy the tail's bytes into the send buffer's last chunk when that chunk is also the send buffer's head and has room, a copy of at most one chunk, after which the tail chunk is released); the hand-off is checked on two events, when DATA is appended to the tail and when the send buffer drains or the downstream stream or connection window reopens; an emptied chunk is released, not kept;
