| Presentation and gates | `addons/dumper/**` (DNS and HTTP/3 output), `internal/tools/dump/**` (default-addon registration order and `TestAddonOrder`), `.github/workflows/ci.yaml` and `network.yaml`, `hack/network-gate.sh` and `hack/network-gate_test.sh` (the two HTTP/3 measurements run sequentially beside the retained rows), `docs/compat.md` and `docs/dependencies.md` rows of this track | Registration and option-table tests, dumper cases, the fuzz-job discovery of the three new targets, the gate rows below | Row text from the other units through their notes and `DONE` reports | Helpers early; the gate rows after the integration unit |

#### File ownership and serial transfers

- **DNS codec and resolver** is the only writer of `dns/**`; the state shape on main is not changed, the codec is added beside it. It also owns the three addon/view surfaces that depend on the codec (`dnsresolver`, `stripdnshttpsrecords`, the `dns` contentview) and the DNS branch of `flowio/flowjson`, whose other branches it does not touch.
- **DNS layer and mode servers** lands the reverse-mode bootstrap first (the table file `reverse_schemes.go`, the one edit of `modes.go` that replaces the switch by a table lookup, the `modeserver` admission of `dns`, `quic` and `http3` and the per-scheme listener factory, with `--mode reverse:dns://` executable and `reverse:quic://`/`reverse:http3://` admitted but answered by a frozen `not implemented yet` start error until their units land their entries), then writes `internal/proxy/modeserver/**` for the DNS listeners and the dual-stack UDP listeners of UDP-based modes. The package transfers to the P6 track's packet-source modes and gates unit only by the lead's `TRANSFER modeserver` ruling after the last P4 commit touching it has landed; after the ruling this unit never edits it again. It writes the DNS branch of `addons/nextlayer`; the QUIC branch of the same file belongs to the QUIC unit, and the two units land their `nextlayer` commits serially: DNS first, and only the QUIC unit rebases, onto the DNS unit's landed commit.
- **QUIC sniff and raw layer** writes `tlsparse/**` for the QUIC Initial parser only; the TLS and DTLS parsers on main stay as they are. It is the exclusive owner of the `addons/tlsconfig` QUIC handler additions and their tests: `quic_start_client/server` port the pinned upstream's configuration (leaf certificate, chain and key, ALPN, preservation of settings an earlier addon supplied, upstream verification and trusted-CA settings) and the layer converts the frozen `hookdata.QUICTLSSettings` to quic-go TLS configuration outside dispatch from dispatch-owned snapshots; the existing TLS and DTLS tlsconfig tests are preservation rows. No new exported hook type is needed. **HTTP/3 engine** writes `internal/h3/**`. **HTTP/3 integration** is the sole writer of `internal/proxy/layers/httplayer/**` in this track apart from the two files the hardening unit names (`stream.go`, `driver.go`), which the two units touch serially (hardening first, because its change is small and its row must be red on `d915d8f`).
- **Proxy core hardening** writes the files its row enumerates and nothing else: not `modeserver`, not `layer`, not a packettransport production file, nothing in `dns/`, `tlsparse/`, `internal/h3/` or the P6 packages. Its `udplayer` change (return `ctx.Err()` when the context is already cancelled instead of the reader's deadline error) is one commit with a regression row that cancels while a reader is blocked and asserts the relay's error.
- **Presentation and gates** is the one writer of `docs/compat.md` and `docs/dependencies.md` in this track and of the two existing workflow files; the dependency rows of the units' first commits are the one exception (those commits land before any other commit of the unit, so no concurrent edit exists).

Deliverables
1. `go:dns/` codec glue over miekg v2 for the P0 state shape, HTTPS/SVCB record helpers (`py:mitmproxy/net/dns/https_records.py`; unblocks the `flowjson` DNS branch from P2), `dns` contentview (interactive; owned here, not by P7); DNS codec fuzz target. (DNS codec and resolver)
2. `go:internal/proxy/layers/dnslayer` (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/dns.py`), `dns` mode servers (UDP and TCP :53), `reverse:dns://`, the UDP listener side of `reverse:https`, `dns_request/response/error` hooks (DNS layer and mode servers); `go:addons/dnsresolver` (`dns_name_servers`, `dns_use_hosts_file`; system servers from `/etc/resolv.conf` and the Windows IP helper API; hosts file parsing; A/AAAA interleave; `gaierror`-style errors); `go:addons/stripdnshttpsrecords` exactly as `py:mitmproxy/addons/strip_dns_https_records.py`: a `dns_response` hook that, when `strip_ech` (its own option, default true) is set, clears the `ech` SvcParam of every HTTPS answer, and, when `http3` is off, removes `h3` and `h3-*` ALPN ids (the ALPN list becomes absent when it ends up empty); it never removes records (DNS codec and resolver).
3. `go:tlsparse` QUIC ClientHello extraction from Initial datagrams (port of `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/quic/_client_hello_parser.py`: Initial secret derivation, header protection removal, CRYPTO frame reassembly across datagrams) for sniffing before deciding passthrough; QUIC Initial fuzz target. (QUIC sniff and raw layer)
4. `go:internal/proxy/layers/quic`: `RawQuicLayer`/`QuicStreamLayer` for `reverse:quic://` (stream-level relay with `tcp_*`-style hooks as upstream), `quic_start_client/server` hooks and their default handlers in `addons/tlsconfig` (`py:mitmproxy/addons/tlsconfig.py:379-464`), which land before the interception handshake. (QUIC sniff and raw layer)
5. `go:internal/h3` + layer: **own HTTP/3 framing** over `quic.Stream` with a frame parser ported from `qgo:http3/frames.go`: HEADERS/DATA/SETTINGS/GOAWAY, control stream, QPACK encoder/decoder streams (dynamic table capacity 0), unknown and GREASE stream and frame types ignored, h2-reserved frame types 0x2/0x6/0x8/0x9 treated as `H3_FRAME_UNEXPECTED` connection errors (`qgo:http3/frames.go:118-127`), closing a critical stream as `H3_CLOSED_CRITICAL_STREAM`, server push refused (`MAX_PUSH_ID` never raised, `CANCEL_PUSH` on receipt), H3 error codes mapped to stream resets both ways; `qpack` ordered header lists (HTTP/3 engine); client-facing HTTP/3 server and upstream HTTP/3 client; `http3` option; `reverse:http3://`; `tls_version` `QUICv1` in connection state; the HTTP/3 listener that P2's `update_alt_svc` points Alt-Svc at in reverse mode (upstream has no HTTP/3 in regular mode: `RegularMode.transport_protocol = TCP`, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_specs.py:186-191`) (HTTP/3 integration). The adapter reuses the HTTP/2 adapter's shape: one connection Receive owner per QUIC connection demultiplexing by stream id to one flow owner per exchange, `layer.EndpointDescriptor` publication under dispatch, original-byte receipts for DATA, bounded per-stream queues, and the same cancellation semantics; stream flow control is quic-go's, so the HTTP/2 budget text does not apply, and the bound the adapter owns is its per-stream receive queue (frozen at contract review, tested, never an unbounded channel).
6. mitmdump listeners for UDP-based modes (dual-stack). (DNS layer and mode servers)
7. Proxy core hardening: the carried items listed in the round preamble, one commit each with its regression row; the client-facing 502 body policy for GOAWAY debug data is decided by the lead at this unit's first report (the unit proposes; the default is a fixed client message `upstream closed the HTTP/2 connection` with the bounded, escaped debug text kept in the log line and the flow error) and recorded in `docs/compat.md` by presentation and gates. (Proxy core hardening)

Acceptance criteria
- DNS wire: round-trip of every RR type exercised in `py:test/mitmproxy/test_dns.py` and `py:test/mitmproxy/net/dns/test_*.py`; `strip_dns_https_records`: with `strip_ech=true` the `ech` parameter is cleared and all other SvcParams and records are kept; with `http3=false` an answer with ALPN `h2,h3,h3-29` keeps only `h2` and an answer with ALPN `h3` ends with no ALPN parameter; with `http3=true` ALPN is untouched. (DNS codec and resolver)
- `mitmdump --mode dns@5353` answers an `example.test A` query from an in-process miekg client, both over UDP and over TCP, using the resolver addon against an in-process miekg authoritative server (no external `dig` binary); a second query for the same name within the TTL is answered from the same upstream answer; `reverse:dns://<server>` forwards to that server; a pinned malformed vector whose header declares one question but whose question bytes are missing is sent over UDP and over TCP framing: parsing fails, the upstream invalid-message diagnostic is logged, only the offending connection or tuple is closed or evicted, no DNS flow is created and no `dns_error` hook fires (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/dns.py:153-160`, `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/layers/test_dns.py:50-67`), while other active UDP tuples stay up; separately, a valid request with no resolver answer and no upstream address fires `dns_error` exactly once and is answered `SERVFAIL` (`dns.py:103-110`, `test_regular_mode_no_hook`); a `FORMERR` answer is not implemented (it would be a compatibility deviation needing its own decision and `docs/compat.md` row). (DNS layer and mode servers)
- QUIC sniff: `tls_clienthello` fires with the correct SNI for a quic-go client's two-datagram Initial and every buffered datagram is replayed in order; `ignore_hosts` passthrough works for QUIC (origin completes its handshake directly); the Initial parser rejects a truncated CRYPTO frame and a wrong version without allocating beyond the datagram size. (QUIC sniff and raw layer)
- HTTP/3: with a frame-level qpack test client and a frame-level test origin, a request carrying duplicate lowercase names `x-foo: 1`, `x-bar: 2`, `x-foo: 3` arrives in the same order; with a GREASE frame and a GREASE unidirectional stream injected by the test client the connection stays open; `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/test_http3.py`-derived scenarios (headers, trailers, stream reset, 1xx) pass; `reverse:https://origin` listens on TCP and UDP and rewrites `Alt-Svc` unless `keep_alt_svc_header`; a 64 MiB download through `reverse:http3://` completes within 30 s on the isolated Linux job, and an intercepted `requestheaders` on one stream leaves an unrelated stream's response complete within 2 s (the same two rows as HTTP/2, so the adapter proves the same isolation). (HTTP/3 integration; the two timed rows run on the isolated Linux job of `network.yaml`)
- Option table test: `dns_resolver`, `strip_dns_https_records` (`strip_ech`) and `http3` options equal upstream; `dnsresolver` and `stripdnshttpsrecords` are registered in upstream's `default_addons()` position and `TestAddonOrder` pins it. (Presentation and gates)
- Hardening rows: each carried item has a test that is red on `d915d8f` with the parent failure line recorded in the lane note (the paused `ErrorHook` row, the `udplayer` cancellation row, the dispatch-admission cancellation row, the `WithoutCancel` rows, the negative-cache bound row, the `privfile` ACL rows on Windows, the packet-transport admission row that distinguishes delayed admission from datagram loss, the 502-body row); the packet-transport repair is proved by two fresh full-tree `go test -race -count=1 ./...` runs in the gate plus `go test -race -count=200 -cpu=1,2,8` on the named packettransport and UDP rows, with the exact commands recorded in the lane note (isolated successes are not full-tree-load evidence); the parent-red evidence for that row demonstrates the identified admission cause, or the lane note states that the historical load-only failure is retained as nondeterministic evidence rather than claiming a deterministic parent failure; the optional `GetFreeTCPPort` adoption and the removal of the two redundant terms are preservation work and are labelled as such. (Proxy core hardening)

#### Executable gate recipe and testability

Deterministic protocol/clock/ownership tests run under `-race` on ubuntu-26.04, xcode-27 and windows-2025. The per-commit gate is the P3 gate (format, lint with and without the difftest tag, build, vet on four targets, `go test -race -count=1 ./...`, the track's packages at `-count=5`, `go test -tags integration ./internal/proxy/...`), and before a unit's final push its own stress rows: the DNS layer at `-count=200 -cpu=1,2,8`, the QUIC hand-off and HTTP/3 scenario rows at `-count=50 -cpu=1,2,8`, the three fuzz targets for 30 s each. The two timed HTTP/3 rows run in the isolated Linux job of `network.yaml`, added by presentation and gates beside the HTTP/2 rows, with the same archive of raw values. Every new regression row is red on `d915d8f` (or on the unit's parent commit) in a detached scratch overlay and green on the unit's tip; preservation rows may be green on both and are named as such; the packet-transport admission row follows the evidence rule of the hardening acceptance (a demonstrated cause, or the historical load-only failure retained and labelled as nondeterministic evidence).

| Criterion | Status against main | Required change / evidence | Owner / execution |
|---|---|---|---|
| DNS RR round-trips, HTTPS/SVCB, strip rules | State shape only, no codec | Codec over miekg with every upstream RR case; strip cases; fuzz target | DNS codec and resolver; all three OSes |
| `dns` view and flowjson DNS | Absent / rejected | View and JSON against upstream fixtures | DNS codec and resolver; all three OSes |
| Reverse-mode bootstrap, DNS mode servers, reverse:dns, TCP framing, resolver | modeserver admits three modes; `modes.go` rejects `dns`/`quic`/`http3` | Bootstrap table and factory rows; `--mode reverse:dns://` executable; in-process miekg client/server rows over UDP and TCP; malformed-vector close/log/no-hook row; SERVFAIL-with-one-error-hook row | DNS layer and mode servers; all three OSes |
| QUIC Initial sniff, ordered replay, passthrough, QUIC TLS settings | No parser, no hand-off, tlsconfig marks QUIC not applicable | Spike row, parser vectors, two-datagram replay, `ignore_hosts`, tlsconfig certificate/ALPN/CA/`ssl_insecure` rows | QUIC sniff and raw layer; all three OSes |
| HTTP/3 framing, QPACK, reserved/critical/push rules | Absent | Frame-level peers, exact error codes, GREASE tolerance, fuzz target | HTTP/3 engine; all three OSes (fuzz on the existing Linux job) |
| HTTP/3 scenarios, Alt-Svc, 64 MiB / 2 s | Absent | Scenario rows on all OSes; timed rows on the isolated Linux job | HTTP/3 integration; all three OSes, timed rows Linux (contributor: presentation and gates runs the job) |
| Carried hardening rows | Open items of P3/P3.5 | One red-on-parent row each (or an honestly labelled preservation row); two fresh full-tree race runs plus `-count=200` on the named packet and UDP rows, commands recorded | Proxy core hardening; all three OSes (Windows ACL rows on the Windows job) |
| Options, registration order, dumper | Needs ownership wording | Compared once; `TestAddonOrder`; upstream DNS/HTTP/3 dumper output | Presentation and gates; all three OSes |

### P5 — transparent and socks5 modes

Deliverables
1. `go:internal/platform/`: linux `SO_ORIGINAL_DST` (v4 and v6, IPv4-mapped handling, `py:mitmproxy/platform/linux.py`); darwin/freebsd native `DIOCNATLOOK` ioctl on `/dev/pf` with a generated `pfioc_natlook` layout per OS (from the xnu / FreeBSD `pfvar.h` sources, checked in with a comment naming the header and version), a one-time runtime self-check comparing the ioctl answer with `pfctl -s state`, and the `pfctl` fallback (`py:mitmproxy/platform/pf.py` with `testdata/mitmproxy/pf01`, `pf02`); openbsd `getsockname`; windows unsupported (error text as upstream).
2. `transparent` mode server (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_servers.py`) on top of the `TransparentProxy` top layer that the P4/P6 round builds in `go:internal/proxy/layers/modes` (P6 deliverable 5, because that round runs before this phase; the layer is shared by transparent, WireGuard, TUN and local).
3. `go:internal/proxy/layers/socks5` (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/modes.py:133` `Socks5Proxy`): RFC 1928 greeting/auth/connect, RFC 1929 user/pass via `socks5_auth` hook and `proxyauth`, IPv4/IPv6/domain ATYP, error replies; BIND/UDP ASSOCIATE rejected as upstream does; SOCKS5 fuzz target.
4. `go:cmd` wiring, `docs` note on `sudo` requirement.

Acceptance criteria
- Linux CI job: `iptables -t nat` REDIRECT of a local client to `mitmdump --mode transparent` yields a flow whose `server_conn.address` equals the original destination (v4 and v6).
- macOS job: `pf01`/`pf02` parse to the expected tuples; the ioctl path is exercised in a `-tags integration` test that skips without root and is run manually with `sudo` on the plan author's machine (result recorded in the PR).
- socks5: `curl --socks5-hostname 127.0.0.1:1080` (and `--socks5` with IPs) works for HTTP and HTTPS; `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/layers/test_modes.py` SOCKS cases ported; the SOCKS5 fuzz target runs 30 s without panics.

### P6 — netstack, WireGuard mode, TUN mode (Linux), local mode (macOS/Windows/Linux)

**Pre-tasks (3 days total)**: (a) download the `mitmproxy_macos`, `mitmproxy_windows` and `mitmproxy_linux` 0.12.11 wheels listed in §1.4, verify the macOS `.app.tar` is non-empty by extracting and listing it (unsigned CI builds ship an empty placeholder, `rs:.github/scripts/build-macos-redirector.sh:55-58`) and record versions + full SHA-256 in `go:internal/local/artifacts.go`; (b) capture IPC frames between the pinned Python mitmproxy and each redirector (control connection, `NewFlow`, `PacketWithMeta`, `UdpPacket`, Linux datagrams) into `testdata/local-ipc/` for the compatibility tests.


#### Execution: units and ownership

This section is built against main `d915d8f`. What exists: `internal/proxy/modespec` already parses `wireguard[:PATH]@port`, `tun[:NAME]`, `local[:SPEC]`, `transparent` and `socks5` (parsing accepts every mode; the server decides what it supports), so no spec grammar is written here; `internal/proxy/modeserver` admits regular, reverse and upstream and, after the P4 track's bootstrap and DNS commits land, the DNS modes and the three P4 reverse schemes; `internal/proxy/layers/modes` registers the regular, reverse and upstream top layers and has no `TransparentProxy`; `internal/proxy/layer` exposes `PacketTransport` (a virtual `net.PacketConn` per fixed tuple with context, deadlines and bounded queues) and `internal/proxy/packettransport` the UDP demultiplexer behind it, which the netstack UDP forwarder reuses as its per-tuple surface; `internal/proxy` dials origins through the pool and records byte streams; `golang.org/x/sys` is already a dependency, `gvisor.dev/gvisor`, `golang.zx2c4.com/wireguard`, `github.com/Microsoft/go-winio` and `google.golang.org/protobuf` are not; the repository has no `.proto` file and no generated IPC bindings (the generated tables under `command/` and `internal/emoji/` are unrelated). Each missing integration has one owner below; a netstack unit test over raw packet builders does not close an executable mode.

| Unit | Exclusive files / integration ownership | Tests and acceptance owned | Frozen output / waits | Landing order |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| netstack core | `internal/netstack/**` (gVisor stack, forwarders, `Stream`, the semantics table, the §4 adapter file `internal/netstack/gvisor.go`), the gVisor dependency commit | Ports of `rs:src/network/tests.rs`, one contract test per semantics-table row, ICMP echo, UDP expiry with an injected clock, backpressure and close ordering | The `netstack.Stack` API (`New`, `Inject`/`Outbound` packet channel, `TCPConns()`/`UDPConns()` accept surfaces, `Stream`) frozen by its first report; no proxy import beyond `layer.PacketTransport` | First in the track; API commit, then the forwarders |
| WireGuard mode | `internal/wireguard/**` (the wireguard-go dependency commit with `internal/wireguard/device.go` as its importing adapter), `testdata/wg-test-client/**` | Config file round-trip, printed client config text, peer routing by learned source IP with first-peer fallback, the end-to-end `HELLO`/`HELLO WORLD!` row | Frozen netstack API; `TransparentProxy` from the packet-source modes unit | After netstack's API commit; end-to-end row after the modes unit's server lands |
| TUN mode (Linux) | `internal/tun/**` | Descriptor hand-over, created/fallback paths, `rp_filter`/`route_localnet`/`accept_local` end state, persistent-interface re-attach, non-Linux refusal text | Frozen netstack API; `TransparentProxy` | After netstack's API commit; the sudo rows in `packet-modes.yaml` |
| local IPC, artifacts and macOS stream mode | `internal/local/**` except the files the two packet-mode units name, the generated `mitmproxy_ipc.pb.go` and its checked-in `.proto` (the protobuf dependency commit: the generated file imports it; the `go:generate` line records the `protoc` invocation and the `--go_opt=M<proto>=github.com/zchee/mitmproxy-go/internal/local` package mapping, because the upstream proto has no `go_package` option), `testdata/local-ipc/**` | IPC decode over captured frames, artifact download/SHA-256/untar/mtime/`MITMPROXY_KEEP_REDIRECTOR`, intercept-spec grammar, `unavailable_reason` texts, macOS stream protocol over the Unix socket, the process-wide singleton | The IPC types and the `Redirector` interface (launch, intercept-spec push, packet in/out) frozen by its first report; nothing from netstack for the macOS stream path | Pre-tasks (a) and (b) first; IPC commit before either packet-mode unit |
| Windows packet mode | `internal/local/windows*.go` and `winio_windows.go`/`winio_other.go` (the go-winio dependency commit, build-constrained so every OS compiles) | Pipe lifecycle (message mode, first instance, reject remote, one client then listener closed), `PacketWithMeta` through netstack, checksum fill, `runas` launch kept alive across stops, the Windows manual runbook | Frozen IPC types and netstack API | After both; the manual runbook before `DONE` |
| Linux packet mode | `internal/local/linux*.go` | `sudo echo -n` probe inheriting the terminal, `sudo --non-interactive --preserve-env` launch, stderr level relay, socket path within 5 s, `UnixDatagram` packet in/out, no pid attribution, the stub-`sudo` failure text, the runner-user interception row | Frozen IPC types and netstack API | After both; the sudo rows in `packet-modes.yaml` |
| Packet-source modes and gates | `internal/proxy/layers/modes/**` (`TransparentProxy` with `remote_endpoint`, the `wireguard`/`tun`/`local` top layers), `internal/proxy/modeserver/**` after the lead's explicit `TRANSFER modeserver` ruling, `.github/workflows/packet-modes.yaml` (new), `docs/compat.md` and `docs/dependencies.md` rows of this track, `internal/tools/dump/**` only if a default addon is added (none is planned) | `TransparentProxy` layer tests against the P1 layer contract, mode-server admission/start/stop/`unavailable_reason` rows, the host-executable FreeBSD refusal row with an injected platform value, the privileged Linux workflow (TUN, Linux local, WireGuard end-to-end) with readiness probes, teardown traps and artifact upload | The modeserver transfer; row text from the other units | `TransparentProxy` early (P5's own transparent mode server reuses it later); the workflow after the first Linux row exists |

#### File ownership and serial transfers

- **netstack core** is the only writer of `internal/netstack/**`. Its API is frozen by its first report and approved by the lead before WireGuard, TUN and the packet-mode units build on it; a later change is announced before it is made. The stack consumes packets through a channel endpoint and hands TCP connections and UDP tuples to the proxy as `net.Conn` streams and `layer.PacketTransport` tuples, so the P1 layer stack (`TransparentProxy` → nextlayer → TLS/HTTP/TCP/UDP) runs unchanged on top of it.
- **local IPC, artifacts and macOS stream mode** owns the vendored `rs:src/ipc/mitmproxy_ipc.proto` copy, the generated Go (checked in, regenerated by a `go:generate` line pinned to the §4 `protoc-gen-go` version) and `testdata/local-ipc/**`; the Windows packet-mode unit owns only its `windows*.go`, `winio_windows.go` and `winio_other.go` files and the Linux packet-mode unit only its `linux*.go` files inside `internal/local`; both import the IPC types. The three units never edit each other's files; a shared helper goes into the IPC unit's files by a `BLOCKED` to the lead.
- **Packet-source modes and gates** writes `internal/proxy/layers/modes/**` except the P4 reverse-mode bootstrap files (`reverse_schemes*.go` and the P4 track's one edit of `modes.go`; its own top layers live in their own files) and, after the lead's `TRANSFER modeserver` ruling, `internal/proxy/modeserver/**`; until that ruling its mode servers are written in the unit's worktree in new files only (`modeserver/wireguard.go`, `modeserver/tun.go`, `modeserver/local.go` and their tests, an explicit new-file carve-out that touches no P4-owned `modeserver` file) against the frozen factory extension point, and are not pushed before the ruling. It is the one writer of `docs/compat.md` and `docs/dependencies.md` in this track (dependency rows of first commits excepted, as in P4) and of `packet-modes.yaml`; it never edits `ci.yaml` or `network.yaml`.
- No unit of this track writes `internal/h2`, `internal/h3`, `httplayer`, `dns`, `tlsparse`, `addons/**` or `cmd/**` except the mode wiring the modes unit needs in `cmd/mitmdump` for `--mode wireguard|tun|local`, which it lands in one commit after the three mode servers exist.


Deliverables
1. `go:internal/netstack/`: gVisor `stack.Stack` with IPv4/IPv6, promiscuous + spoofing NIC, default routes, `tcp.NewForwarder`/`udp.NewForwarder`, MTU 1420 (`rs:src/network/virtual_device.rs:67`), TCP 64 KiB buffers, 60 s idle timeout, 28 s keepalive (`rs:src/network/tcp.rs`), UDP 4-tuple LRU with 60 s expiry (`rs:src/network/udp.rs`), fake ICMP echo replies (`rs:src/network/icmp.rs`), a `Stream` type with the upstream `get_extra_info` keys. **Semantics table** (contract tests): close = flush then FIN, never RST; half-close requests are treated as close (as Rust ignores `half_close`); peer RST surfaces as a read error after buffered data; backpressure = `Write` never blocks, `Drain` waits for the send buffer; UDP expiry 60 s; ICMP echo answered locally. (netstack core)
2. `go:internal/wireguard/`: `device.NewDevice` over a `tun.Device` bridged to the netstack `channel` endpoint, peer config from `<confdir>/wireguard.conf` JSON `{server_key, client_key}` (base64 X25519), printed client config (`Address 10.0.0.1/32`, `DNS 10.0.0.53`, `AllowedIPs 0.0.0.0/0`, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_servers.py:336-415`), `wireguard[:PATH]@port` spec (default 51820), peer routing by learned source IP with first-peer fallback (`rs:src/packet_sources/wireguard.rs`). (WireGuard mode)
3. `go:internal/local/`: protobuf IPC from `rs:src/ipc/mitmproxy_ipc.proto` (generated with `protoc-gen-go`, checked in) with a decode test over `testdata/local-ipc/`; artifact acquisition into `<confdir>/redirector/<version>/` on first use (or `--set local_redirector_path=`), macOS untar of `Mitmproxy Redirector.app` into `/Applications` (mtime check, `MITMPROXY_KEEP_REDIRECTOR` honoured); macOS stream mode: the proxy listens on the Unix socket `/tmp/mitmproxy-<pid>` (`rs:src/packet_sources/macos.rs:85-86`), length-delimited `InterceptConf`, per-flow `NewFlow` handshake (u32 BE prefix), raw TCP bytes, length-delimited `UdpPacket`; Windows packet mode: go-winio pipe `\\.\pipe\mitmproxy-transparent-proxy-<pid>` (message mode, first instance, reject remote, one accepted client then the listener is closed), `PacketWithMeta` in/out through netstack, IP checksum fill on reinjection, `ShellExecuteW "runas"` launch kept alive across stops; intercept spec grammar (`rs:src/intercept_conf.rs`: comma list, int = PID, substring otherwise, `!` excludes, own PID always excluded), `describe_spec` validation for `local:SPEC`; `unavailable_reason()` equivalents (on an OS without a redirector, e.g. FreeBSD, the text is upstream's `Local redirect mode is not supported on <GOOS>`, `rs:mitmproxy-rs/src/server/local_redirector.rs:79-83`, where Rust's `std::env::consts::OS` and Go's `GOOS` both name FreeBSD `freebsd`; upstream also fails the mode start with that text, `:158-161`; on Linux it is upstream's effective-uid check, `mitmproxy is not running as root.` when the effective uid is not 0 and none otherwise, `rs:mitmproxy-rs/src/server/local_redirector.rs:72-77`; upstream reads it only for the mitmweb `/state` field, `py:mitmproxy/tools/web/app.py:839`, and starting local mode never consults it); `local` mode is a process-wide singleton. **Linux packet mode** (added to scope by the user): launch `mitmproxy-linux-redirector` from the `mitmproxy_linux` wheel exactly the way `rs:src/packet_sources/linux.rs:29-111` does: an interactive privilege probe `sudo echo -n` (`:30-41`; the `-n` is an argument of `echo`, the probe inherits stdin, stdout and stderr so sudo can prompt for a password, and a non-zero exit fails the start with `Failed to elevate privileges`, `:39-40`), then `sudo --non-interactive --preserve-env <exe> <tmpdir>` (`:44-53`), with the redirector's stderr lines relayed to the log at the level their `[LEVEL` prefix names (`:58-83`) and its socket path read from the first stdout line within 5 s (`:103-111`); `sudo` is resolved through `PATH` (`exec.LookPath`, as `Command::new("sudo")` does); a start failure is logged as an error by `proxyserver` (`py:mitmproxy/addons/proxyserver.py:93-96`), so mitmdump exits 1 through `errorcheck` (`py:mitmproxy/addons/errorcheck.py:28-46`); the redirector chmods its sockets 0777; speak `PacketWithMeta` over a `UnixDatagram` socket pair (one message per datagram) into the netstack and reinject the same way; the intercept spec is pushed as `InterceptConf`; no `pid`/`process_name` attribution on Linux (the redirector sends `tunnel_info: None`, `rs:mitmproxy-linux/src/main2.rs`); requires root via sudo and cgroup v2 at `/sys/fs/cgroup`. (local IPC, artifacts and macOS stream mode for the IPC, artifacts, spec grammar, `unavailable_reason` and the macOS stream path; Windows packet mode for the pipe path; Linux packet mode for the redirector launch and datagram path)
4. **TUN mode (Linux)** (added to scope by the user): `tun[:NAME]` spec (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_specs.py:307-325`; macOS accepts only `utunN` names but the mode is Linux-only at runtime, as upstream), a `golang.zx2c4.com/wireguard/tun` device bridged to the netstack `channel` endpoint, with the device setup of `rs:src/packet_sources/tun.rs:49-107,172-213` ported as follows. The proxy opens `/dev/net/tun` itself with `O_RDWR|O_CLOEXEC`, as `CreateTUN` does (`tun/tun_linux.go:552`), issues `TUNSETIFF` with `IFF_TUN | IFF_NO_PI | IFF_VNET_HDR` (the flags `CreateTUN` uses, `golang.zx2c4.com/wireguard@v0.0.0-20260522210424-ecfc5a8d5446` `tun/tun_linux.go:566`) and, once the device is configured, hands a duplicate of the descriptor to `tun.CreateUnmonitoredTUNFromFD` (`:637-660`): the proxy takes the duplicate with `unix.FcntlInt(fd, unix.F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC, 0)` (a plain `unix.Dup` has no close-on-exec flag, and the same process spawns children: the Linux redirector through `sudo`, the `browser` addon, the console's external editor), sets it non-blocking itself with `unix.SetNonblock` (closing the duplicate if that fails, because the function returns before wrapping it at `:638-641`), passes the duplicate, and always closes its original descriptor afterwards; from the call on, the duplicate belongs to the `os.File` the function creates (`:642`), which closes it on `Close` (`:482-499`) or, on the error returns at `:651-658`, through its finalizer, so the proxy never closes a descriptor number twice; the device stays attached because the attachment belongs to the open file description, which the duplicate shares. The unmonitored device starts no netlink or hack listener, so it never emits `EventUp` (only those listeners send it, `:88,181`), and the netstack bridge must not wait on `Events()`. `tun.CreateTUN` and `CreateTUNFromFile` are not used, because `CreateTUN` leaks the descriptor when `TUNSETIFF` fails (`:567-569`) and `CreateTUNFromFile` always calls `setMTU`, which needs `CAP_NET_ADMIN`, and on that error closes only the netlink socket (`:626-629`). *Created path*: MTU 65535 (`SIOCSIFMTU`), address `169.254.0.1` (`SIOCSIFADDR` with `unix.Ifreq.SetInet4Addr`) and link up (`SIOCSIFFLAGS` with `IFF_UP`) through `golang.org/x/sys/unix` ioctls (`tun.rs:50-56`) issued on an `AF_INET` datagram socket opened for the purpose, not on the TUN descriptor (the `SIOCSIF*` ioctls name the interface in the `Ifreq`; wireguard-go's own `setMTU` opens such a socket, `tun/tun_linux.go:231-245`), then `configure_device` (`:89-107`): `disable_rp_filter` ported in full (`:174-213`: write 0 to the interface's `rp_filter`; if `conf/all/rp_filter` is not 0, raise every other interface's `rp_filter` to max(all, interface) and then write 0 to `conf/all/rp_filter`; values are compared after trimming the trailing newline, whereas upstream compares the raw file contents with `"0"` at `:185`, so its early return never fires and only the end state, which is the same, is reproduced), `route_localnet=1` (`:93-98`) and `accept_local=1` (`:99-106`, which upstream sets so that injected packets with a local source address such as 127.0.0.1 are accepted), each failure logged as upstream does. *Fallback path*: when `TUNSETIFF` or a configuration ioctl fails with `EPERM` and `NAME` is set, the proxy closes its descriptor and attaches to the existing persistent interface with `TUNSETIFF` alone, leaving MTU, address and `/proc` values untouched, as upstream retries with a default configuration (`:67-83`); without `NAME` the `EPERM` is returned. The fallback happens before the hand-over, so the descriptor the proxy closes there has never been duplicated, and no descriptor leaks on the fallback; on other OSes the mode start fails with upstream's text `TUN proxy mode is only supported on Linux` (`rs:mitmproxy-rs/src/server/tun.rs:46-47,80-83`). (TUN mode)
5. `TransparentProxy` in `go:internal/proxy/layers/modes` (moved here from P5 deliverable 2, because this round runs before P5): the top layer that takes the original destination from `remote_endpoint` and hands the connection to nextlayer exactly as the reverse layer does with its configured target; mode servers for `wireguard`, `tun` and `local` reusing it; `cmd/mitmdump` wiring for the three modes. (Packet-source modes and gates)

Time-boxes: netstack 5 days, WireGuard 4 days, TUN 2 days, Linux packet mode 3 days, macOS stream mode 4 days, Windows packet mode 5 days; WireGuard and TUN may land alone if any local-mode sub-task overruns by more than 3 days (S3), but the round remains incomplete until every required P6 criterion is met or the user explicitly changes the scope.

Acceptance criteria
- netstack: ports of `rs:src/network/tests.rs` cases (`ipv4_udp`, `ipv6_udp`, `tcp_ipv4_connection`, `tcp_ipv6_connection`, `receive_icmp4_echo`, `receive_icmp6_echo`) pass against the Go stack with raw packet builders; the semantics table has one contract test per row; the UDP 60 s expiry and the 28 s keepalive are proved with the injected clock, never by sleeping; a TCP stream whose peer stops reading stalls only itself (`Write` returns at once, `Drain` waits) while another stream on the same stack completes. (netstack core; all three OSes)
- WireGuard e2e: `testdata/wg-test-client/<platform>` run with `test.conf` against `mitmdump --mode wireguard` plus an upper-casing TCP/UDP addon receives `HELLO` and `HELLO WORLD!` (mirrors `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/test_mode_servers.py`); run on linux-x86_64 (`packet-modes.yaml`) and macos-aarch64 (`ci.yaml`'s existing macOS test job can run it without privileges, because the WireGuard listener is a plain UDP socket) in CI, windows-x86_64 manually. (WireGuard mode)
- local (macOS): manual runbook on macOS 12+ — artifact download verified by SHA-256, extension approval, `local:curl` intercepts `curl https://example.test` from a terminal, `pid`/`process_name` present in `client_conn` extra info; result recorded as a dated note in the PR (no CI). (local IPC, artifacts and macOS stream mode)
- local (Windows): manual runbook on windows-2025 runner or a local VM — UAC prompt once, intercept of a `curl.exe` process, packets round-trip through netstack; checksum correctness checked with a packet capture; IPC decode test over the captured frames passes in CI on all OSes. (Windows packet mode; the IPC decode row belongs to the IPC unit)
- local (Linux): on the ubuntu-26.04 runner (passwordless sudo, cgroup v2), the test setup writes an `/etc/hosts` entry mapping `example.test` to the runner's primary non-loopback IPv4 address, where the test origin listens (the redirector's handling of loopback destinations is not verified, so the test does not depend on it); `mitmdump --mode local:curl` run as the runner user plus `curl http://example.test:<port>/` from a shell produces an HTTP flow whose `server_conn.address` is that address and port and whose client extra info has no `pid`; a `wget` in the same session is not intercepted; with a stub `sudo` that exits 1 placed first on `PATH`, `mitmdump --mode local` exits 1 and the log contains `Failed to elevate privileges`; the password-prompt path (a sudoer with a password typing it at the probe) is manual-only, run once and recorded in the PR. (Linux packet mode; the rows run in `packet-modes.yaml`)
- TUN (Linux): on the runner, the test runs under sudo: `mitmdump --mode tun` creates an interface; with an `httptest` origin on `127.0.0.1:<port>` and **no route added**, `curl --interface <tun-name> http://127.0.0.1:<port>/` (curl binds to the device, as upstream's test binds its socket with `SO_BINDTODEVICE`, `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/test_mode_servers.py:445-447`, and upstream's docs use `curl --interface tun0`, `py:docs/src/content/concepts/modes.md:392`) yields a flow with `server_conn.address` `127.0.0.1:<port>` and curl receives the origin's response; no route is added because a route for the destination in the main table would also carry mitmdump's own outbound dial (eager by default) into the TUN, so each connection would spawn another; the interface's `rp_filter` and `conf/all/rp_filter` read back as `0`, `route_localnet` and `accept_local` as `1`; with a persistent `tun0` pre-created by `ip tuntap add dev tun0 mode tun user <runner user>` and configured by the test, `mitmdump --mode tun:tun0` run without sudo attaches to it, writes no `/proc` value, and a second start after the first one stopped attaches again; on macOS `mitmdump --mode tun` exits 1 and the log contains `TUN proxy mode is only supported on Linux`. (TUN mode; the sudo rows run in `packet-modes.yaml`)
- A host-executable unit test of the mode server with an injected platform value `freebsd` asserts the start refusal, the exact log text `Local redirect mode is not supported on freebsd` (`rs:mitmproxy-rs/src/server/local_redirector.rs:79-83,158-161`) and the exit classification (exit 1 through `errorcheck`); a `GOOS=freebsd go build ./...` cross-compile is separate evidence that the tree builds there, not execution of that binary; the IPC unit owns only the `unavailable_reason` function's own test; the mitmweb `/state` check lives in P8. (Packet-source modes and gates)

#### Executable gate recipe and testability

Deterministic netstack, IPC and mode-server tests run under `-race` on ubuntu-26.04, xcode-27 and windows-2025 through `ci.yaml` unchanged. The privileged rows (TUN under sudo, Linux local mode as the runner user with passwordless sudo, the WireGuard end-to-end row on Linux) run in the new required job of `packet-modes.yaml` on ubuntu-26.04: it records kernel, `ip`, `sudo` and cgroup versions, pre-creates the persistent `tun0` for the re-attach row, writes the `/etc/hosts` entry for `example.test`, installs EXIT/INT/TERM traps that delete the interfaces, hosts entry and `/proc` values it changed, and uploads logs and the redirector's stderr on success and failure; a missing privilege, kernel feature or artifact is a failed required job, not a skip. The macOS and Windows local-mode runbooks are manual, dated and recorded in the pull request before the unit's `DONE`; their IPC frames feed the decode test that does run in CI. The per-commit gate is the P3 gate; before a unit's final push, `internal/netstack` at `-race -count=50 -cpu=1,2,8`, the IPC decode and mode-server rows at `-count=200`, and the netstack packet-builder fuzz target (the IPv4/IPv6/ICMP parsers of the injected path) for 30 s. Every new regression row is red on its parent commit in a detached scratch overlay and green on the unit's tip.

### The follow-up round after P4+P6 (revision 11)

**What the round is.** Nine units, each funded by one of the user's closeout decisions of 2026-10-09 (ledger `.omc/artifacts/p46-session/rulings-p46.md`, "USER CLOSEOUT DECISIONS batch 1" 22:02 JST, "batch 2" 22:27 JST, "batch 3" 22:29 JST, re-appended 2026-10-10 00:17 JST after the `.omc` recovery): fixture hardening (batch 2, item 1); HTTP/3 abort-table and QUIC close parity as one unit (batch 2, items 2 and 3); deterministic real-HTTP/3 acceptance (batch 2, item 4); WireGuard multi-peer routing (batch 2, item 5); the HTTP/2 malformed-present framer path (batch 3, item 9); DNS transaction-ID eviction (batch 3, item 11); CI skip visibility (batch 3, item 14); the macOS and Windows local-mode runbooks (batch 1, item 6); the upstream apply-ACK proposal (batch 1, item 13). It is built against main `6161318`. P5 follows this round. The round's mechanics are those of the P4+P6 round (one lead, one `flock` gate lock, one unlanded pushed commit per lane, rebase before review, exact-hash evidence, draft pull requests re-opened after every landing, dependency commits first and alone, the lead never rewriting a pushed candidate), with the additions under "Round rules" below.

**Truth paragraph against `6161318`.** What exists and what is open, per unit:

- *Carried flaky rows (LI-2 family, CI-31, CI-38).* The one-rerun policy is in force: a carried row that fails in a full-tree gate is rerun once in isolation, the original log retained. The rows: `go:internal/proxy/packettransport/transport_test.go` `TestTuplePackets/preserves_boundaries` and `/empty_is_not_EOF` (`:135`, `:140`, "first datagram socket delivery did not complete", 30 s); `go:internal/proxy/modeserver/dns_authoritative_test.go:67` `TestReverseDNSAuthoritativeTransports/UDP` ("DNS exchange: read udp ... i/o timeout; request hooks=1 response hooks=1 error hooks=0 origin replies=1"); `go:internal/proxy/modeserver/local_flow_test.go:111` `TestLocalIPCOrigins/UDP_real_origin` ("read udp4 i/o timeout"); `go:internal/proxy/proxytest/streaming_test.go:228` `TestLargeBodyStreaming/success:_upload` ("waiting for test event hung", 30 s, CI-31, the proxytest event-collector family); `go:internal/netutil/freeport/freeport_test.go:27` `TestGetFreePort/success:_TCP_and_UDP` on `windows-2025` (CI-38: "bind: An attempt was made to access a socket in a way forbidden by its access permissions", a Windows excluded port range; the helper tries `attempts = 64` candidates, `go:internal/netutil/freeport/freeport.go:17`). These are six subtest rows in five families (two `TestTuplePackets` subtests, the DNS, local, streaming and freeport rows). The verifier saw two of them fail under full-tree load and pass alone (NB-3); `integ.sh` needed its single rerun on four consecutive landings. Every one of them is a test-fixture or helper row; no production defect has been attributed to them.
- *HTTP/3 abort table and QUIC close parity.* `go:internal/proxy/layers/httplayer/quicgo_abort_test.go:33-44` holds the twelve-row consumer abort table (`request`/`response` × `buffered`/`streamed` × `reset`/`close`/`reset then close`). LG-2 (`request streamed close`, a Linux CI ordering failure) was fixed test-only in `28c6d0d`, which the verifier could not discriminate on its parent (NB-9). LG-3 (`response streamed close`: a missing error hook observed once in an expanded race run, `.omc/handoffs/lane-p46-h3-integration.md:272`) is open with no reproduction and an unanswered cause-preservation question (which close cause reaches the consumer when the remote application close and the local cleanup race). In `go:internal/proxy/layers/quic`, `TestQUICConsumerBorrowing/protocol abort` (`consumer_test.go:71`) failed under cold full-tree load as an idle timeout instead of application code `0x102`; the keepalive fixture of `57e23bd` (hypothesis B, idle expiry under load) landed test-only and the original finding stays open. Transport-level `CONNECTION_CLOSE` parity (`rs:` has none; upstream `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/quic/_raw_layers.py:290-302` forwards code, frame type and reason) is unrepaired: quic-go v0.63.0 exposes only application `CloseWithError` for caller-originated closes, and the `docs/compat.md` row under `internal/proxy/layers/quic` says so; `close_forward_test.go:95` ("remote transport close") tests only the tracer's diagnostic formatter with a synthetic `qlog.ConnectionClosed` event, so no real transport-close forwarding row exists; normal cleanup sends application code 0 with an empty reason (`go:internal/proxy/layers/quic/quicgo.go:54-56`) and forwarding maps only a remote `ApplicationError` (`:523-528`), never a transport code. LG-1 (`TestQUICWireCloseBeforeContextPublication` readiness timeouts under load) is closed as "cause unproven, fixture hardened" (`0d82e26`) and is not reopened by this round.
- *Real-HTTP/3 acceptance.* `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/test_http3.py` (at `3368a0a`) defines eighteen test functions: `test_ignore_push`, `test_fail_without_header`, `test_invalid_header`, `test_simple`, `test_response_trailers`, `test_request_trailers`, `test_upstream_error`, `test_http3_client_aborts` (parametrised), `test_rst_then_close`, `test_cancel_then_server_disconnect`, `test_cancel_during_response_hook`, `test_stream_concurrency`, `test_stream_concurrent_get_connection`, `test_kill_stream`, `test_receive_stop_sending` (parametrised), `TestClient.test_no_data_on_closed_stream`, `TestClient.test_ignore_wrong_order`, `test_early_server_data`. The engine-level map `.omc/handoffs/p46-h3-upstream-map.md` (eighteen Python rows, `:56-73`) marks the hook-dispatch and flow-level sequences as integration-owned and untested (`:65-66`, `:72`). The closeout counted six full-case sequences covered, one covered with a documented divergence and eleven uncovered (closeout item 4); the case-reconciliation archive that listed them was lost in the `.omc` deletion of 2026-10-09 22:31 JST, so the unit re-derives the list before it writes a test.
- *WireGuard.* `go:internal/wireguard/server.go:33`: `New` configures exactly one client peer (R46-16); the mode server in `go:internal/proxy/modeserver` (`wireguardSource`) prints that peer's client configuration. Upstream's Rust engine routes by learned source IP with a first-peer fallback (`rs:src/packet_sources/wireguard.rs`), and upstream's Python mode server writes one `{server_key, client_key}` pair (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_servers.py:336-415`), so the routing algorithm follows the Rust engine, and supplying more than one peer additionally requires the Go-only `client_keys` configuration extension that unit 4 defines; the legacy two-key format and the default generated file stay unchanged.
- *HTTP/2 malformed-present framer path.* `go:internal/h2/owner.go:219-224`: a framer `http2.StreamError` for a stream the owner knows is handled by `o.cancel(...)` with the framer's code, which takes the stream-cancel path; a present identity whose frame the framer rejects therefore follows the same path as a peer reset. The P3.5 review named this a retained, unaddressed boundary (round preamble above); no regression row exists.
- *DNS transaction-ID retention.* `go:internal/proxy/layers/dnslayer/dns.go:49` keeps `flows map[int]*flow.DNSFlow`; `:123-128` inserts a flow per message id and nothing deletes an entry until the connection ends, exactly like upstream's `self.flows` (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/dns.py:61,67,164-169,180`). Up to 65,536 entries per connection, each message bounded at 1 MiB, no aggregate budget (security review, inherited debt, closeout item 11). The user kept parity for the P4+P6 round and funded eviction now.
- *CI skip visibility.* `go:.github/workflows/ci.yaml:64` runs `go test -race -count=1 ./...` without `-v`, so a `t.Skip` prints nothing and the job stays green whether the row ran or skipped. Four capability skips fire when IPv6 loopback is absent: `TestBothListenerDualStack` (`go:internal/proxy/modeserver/both_failure_test.go:56,61`), `TestStandaloneDNSWireTransports/IPv6_*` (`dns_standalone_test.go:39`), `TestBoundPacketFactoryRollback` (`factory_binding_test.go:21`); they PASS with 0 SKIP on macOS and on the remote Debian host with `-v`, and the GitHub runner behaviour is unobserved (NB-4, R46-147). The other eleven added skips are `GOOS` guards, one env opt-in (`MITMPROXY_TEST_REDIRECTOR_DOWNLOAD=1`) and one Windows helper guard (NB-5). `hack/` holds `network-gate.sh`/`network-gate_test.sh` and `packet-modes-gate.sh`/`packet-modes-gate_test.sh`, the pattern a new gate script follows.
- *macOS and Windows local mode.* `docs/compat.md` under `internal/local` says that installed-app and system-extension capture on macOS and UAC/WinDivert process attribution on Windows "remain manual acceptance boundaries"; no runbook document exists under `docs/`, and the P6 criteria that asked for dated manual runs recorded in a pull request were not executed (closeout item 6). The user chose a manual runbook over dedicated runners.
- *Readiness contract.* §3.4 item 8: the barrier works without an ABI change; the user chose to keep it and to propose an explicit apply-ACK upstream (closeout item 13).

**Lane composition and ownership.** The nine units run on five lanes so that the two `httplayer` units and the two small protocol units do not contend; the lead may split a lane when a unit's time-box is at risk, never merge two units' files into one commit.

**File ownership and serial transfers**

- Frozen for the round: `go:internal/proxy/packettransport/*.go` production files, `go:internal/proxy/layer/**`, `go:addon/hookdata/**`, `go:internal/h3/**` (the engine is complete; a needed change is a `BLOCKED` to the lead), `go:internal/netstack/**`, `go:internal/local/**`, `go:internal/tun/**`, `go:dns/**`, `go:tlsparse/**`. Lane A's grant on `transport_test.go` is the one test-file exception inside a frozen package, and it is written here so that NB-10 does not recur.
- `go:internal/proxy/layers/httplayer` is written by lane B (production: `quicgo.go`; tests: the abort-table files) and lane C (new test files only, plus the `h3test` helper package). Lane B lands first; lane C rebases onto lane B's landed commits and never the reverse. Neither touches `http1.go`, `driver.go`, `stream.go` or the HTTP/2 adapter.
- `go:internal/proxy/modeserver` is written by lane A (two test files and their helpers) and lane D (`wireguard*` files, by grant); the two sets do not overlap, and each lane rebases onto the other's landed commit when its own rebase conflicts in a shared helper.
- `go:internal/proxy/proxytest` is written by lane A (`streaming_test.go`, the event collector helpers) and lane C (new `http3_*_test.go` files only); lane A lands first.
- `docs/compat.md` and `docs/dependencies.md` have one writer, lane E; lanes B and D supply their row text in their `DONE` reports, and the lead relays it. `docs/runbooks/**` and `docs/proposals/**` are new directories owned by lane E.
- No unit edits `cmd/**`, `addons/**`, `internal/proxy/handler*.go`, `pool.go`, `poolconn.go` or `runner.go`; the P4+P6 hardening unit's files are closed.

**Deliverables and acceptance criteria**

1. *Fixture hardening (lane A).* Deliverables: (a) a recorder watchdog for the packet-transport and UDP fixtures: the fixture peer reports positive admission and delivery signals (first-tuple admitted, first datagram delivered) on channels the test selects on, and a hang detector with a goroutine dump replaces the silent 30 s timeouts at `transport_test.go:135,140`, `dns_authoritative_test.go:67` and `local_flow_test.go:111`; (b) a request-hook barrier in proxytest's event collector so that `TestLargeBodyStreaming` waits for the request hook to be observed before it asserts streaming, and the collector's "waiting for test event hung" reports which event it waited for and which events arrived; (c) `freeport` skips Windows excluded port ranges: on Windows the helper reads the excluded ranges once (`netsh int ipv4 show excludedportrange protocol=udp` and `protocol=tcp`, parsed; a parse failure falls back to the current behaviour with a logged note) and never proposes a candidate inside them, keeping `attempts = 64` and the retained-candidate rule of R46-57. Acceptance: each of the six subtest rows (both `TestTuplePackets` subtests named individually in the stress manifest) runs `-race -count=200 -cpu=1,2,8` clean in isolation and two fresh full-tree `go test -race -count=1 ./...` runs under the lock pass without a rerun, on macOS (lead gate) and on the remote Debian host (`-count=20` on the named rows), with the exact commands and logs in the lane note; the freeport row passes on the exact-head `windows-2025` job twice (two pushes or one rerun requested explicitly as evidence, labelled as such); a parent-red row exists for the request-hook barrier (the collector's old timeout path, reproduced with an injected delay) and for the excluded-range skip (a fixture excluded range that the old helper proposes); the recorder-watchdog rows are preservation rows and are labelled so. When all six subtest rows have landed, the lead retires the one-rerun policy for them by name in the ledger; a later failure of one of them is a blocking finding, not a carried row.
2. *HTTP/3 abort cause and QUIC close parity (lane B).* Deliverables: (a) LG-3: a deterministic reproduction of the missing error hook on `response streamed close` using the tracer-gated close-cause publication window already used by `close_forward_test.go` (quic-go `Config.Tracer`, no library patch), then a repair that preserves the first observed close cause (the remote application error observed by the datagram reader wins over the local cleanup close, as `go:internal/proxy/layers/quic/quicgo.go:523-528` already does for raw QUIC; `0d82e26` hardened only a readiness fixture) so the consumer's error hook fires exactly once with that cause; (b) the `protocol abort` row: a cold-load reproduction attempt with the fixture-hardening load profile (two fresh full-tree `-race` runs after lane A's first landing) and the native-event capture of the P4+P6 investigation; if the row still fails only as an idle timeout under load, the unit may convert the assertion to "application code `0x102`, or the idle timeout when the abort packet is provably undelivered" only under an evidence contract stated in the test: the proxy-side qlog shows the `CONNECTION_CLOSE` frame sent, the client-side packet capture (the native-preserving `UDPConn` wrapper of the P4+P6 investigation, `ReadBatch` path) shows the datagram not received, and the client's reader goroutine is proved runnable (a goroutine dump at the timeout shows it blocked in the socket read, not stalled behind the race scheduler); endpoint qlog non-receipt alone does not distinguish packet loss from a stalled reader and is not sufficient; the lane note labels the outcome as a fixture decision with that evidence attached; (c) transport-close parity: a decision, not necessarily a repair. The unit checks the quic-go v0.63.0 public API for a way to emit a transport-level `CONNECTION_CLOSE` with a caller-chosen code and frame type (`quic.Conn.CloseWithError` is application-level; `closeWithTransportError` is unexported, `connection.go:2189`); if none exists, the compat row stays and the unit adds a real peer-induced transport-error certification row (a frame-level peer that provokes a transport error, not a synthetic tracer event) asserting the current behaviour: the opposite peer receives the cleanup application close with code 0 and an empty reason (`quicgo.go:54-56`) and the original typed transport diagnostic is preserved in the returned error; any mapping of a transport code onto an application code is a separate behavioural change and is not what this row pins. Acceptance: the LG-3 row is red on `6161318` (or on the unit's parent) through the tracer gate and green on the unit's tip, at `-race -count=200 -cpu=1,2,8`; the abort table (`quicgo_abort_test.go`) passes `-count=200` and the two full-tree runs without a rerun; the `protocol abort` row passes the same profile or carries the labelled fixture decision with the qlog evidence; the compat row under `internal/proxy/layers/quic` is either rewritten (repair) or confirmed by the new pinning row (decision), and the lane note says which. The time-box is strict: a fourth day without a deterministic LG-3 reproduction ends the unit with "unproven, pinning rows landed" as the disposition, reported to the user.
3. *Real-HTTP/3 acceptance (lane C).* Deliverables: (a) `docs/compat.md` gains no row; the unit first re-derives the case map: for each of the eighteen upstream functions, one row stating covered (which Go test), covered with divergence (which `docs/compat.md` row explains it) or uncovered, recorded in the lane note and in a comment block at the head of the new test file; (b) one deterministic Go test per uncovered sequence in `http3_upstream_cases_test.go`, driven by frame-level peers in `h3test` (a client that sends HTTP/3 frames over a real quic-go connection to the proxy's HTTP/3 listener, and an origin that answers at the frame level; modelled on the existing in-package helpers `newHTTP3RoutingListener` and `startHTTP3RoutingPeer` in `go:internal/proxy/layers/httplayer/quicgo_routing_test.go:45,70` and the `internal/h3` plus `qpack` peers of `quicgo_abort_test.go`, which lane B owns and lane C does not edit; `proxytest` has no HTTP/3 row today), with the addon hook sequence asserted through `addon/addontest` and the flow state through `flow`; each test names the upstream function and line range it maps. Acceptance: eleven new rows (or the re-derived count, with the difference explained in the lane note), each green on all three OSes at `-race -count=50 -cpu=1,2,8`; for a behavioural regression the lane overlays only the new test and helper files onto the parent's unmodified production code and records the actual failure, while a sequence that is already correct is labelled a certification row (allowed: these rows exist to pin behaviour, not to find defects); a deliberate production mutation is separate mutation-sensitivity evidence and is never reported as red on parent; the two timed HTTP/3 rows of `network.yaml` stay green on the landed head; the Windows `-count=50 -cpu=1,2,8` evidence comes from lane E's stress matrix job (the baseline `count=1` jobs are not stress evidence); `test_http3_client_aborts` maps onto the existing abort table and is not duplicated.
4. *WireGuard multi-peer routing (lane D).* Deliverables: (a) a contract commit that extends `go:internal/wireguard` `Config` with an ordered peer list (the existing `{server_key, client_key}` file stays valid and yields one peer; a second form with `client_keys: [...]` is a Go-only extension recorded in `docs/compat.md`, never written by default; precedence is defined: a file carrying both `client_key` and `client_keys` is rejected with a named error, `client_keys` must be non-empty and free of duplicates, and the first entry is the fallback peer), and `New` with the engine's routing: outbound packets are routed to the peer whose learned source address last sent from that address, with the first configured peer as the fallback for addresses no peer has used yet (`rs:src/packet_sources/wireguard.rs`); (b) the mode server prints one client configuration per peer, each with a distinct `Address` (the first peer keeps `10.0.0.1/32`, the n-th gets `10.0.0.n/32`; the DNS and `AllowedIPs` lines are unchanged); (c) `testdata/wg-test-client` runs two clients with distinct keys and addresses, driven by the extended `TestWireGuardExecutable`. Acceptance: the routing table is a unit row with an injected packet sequence (learn, route, fallback, re-learn after a client changes address); the end-to-end row runs two clients concurrently against `mitmdump --mode wireguard` through the DumpMaster harness and each receives its own upper-cased reply (`HELLO` and `HELLO WORLD!` per client, no cross-delivery), on the Linux privileged job and the macOS job; the single-peer configuration file produces byte-identical behaviour to `6161318` (preservation row); the `docs/compat.md` row under `internal/wireguard` is rewritten by lane E from lane D's `DONE` text: the single-peer deviation paragraph is replaced by the routing description and the Go-only multi-key configuration form.
5. *HTTP/2 malformed-present framer path (lane D).* Deliverable: in `go:internal/h2/owner.go:219-224`, a framer `StreamError` for a present stream is classified: when the framer rejected a frame of a stream the owner has an identity for, the stream fails with a `ProtocolError`-derived stream error that names the framer's reason (not the peer-reset path), the owner queues a `RST_STREAM` with the framer's code, and the flow's error records "malformed frame" rather than "reset by peer"; the idle-stream and unknown-stream branches are unchanged. Acceptance: a frame-level peer sends `WINDOW_UPDATE` with increment zero on an already-open non-zero stream, the one stream-scoped framer rejection `x/net/http2` v0.60.0 produces (`frame.go:1015-1020`; a HEADERS pad length larger than its payload is the other, `:1106`); the flow's error text names the malformed frame, the peer receives `RST_STREAM` with `PROTOCOL_ERROR`, a sibling exchange completes, and the new test overlaid onto the unmodified parent fails on `6161318` (the parent records the reset-by-peer text) and passes on the tip; a DATA frame with a bad padding length is a connection error in the framer (`frame.go:669-675`) and a CONTINUATION-order violation is disabled by `AllowIllegalReads` (`go:internal/h2/endpoint.go:293`), so those two stay connection-error preservation rows and are not this stream-error row; the P3 budget, intercept and stream-stall rows pass as preservation rows; the reset-source labels of the P3.5 round stay distinct (a peer reset still says so).
6. *DNS transaction-ID eviction (lane E).* Deliverable: `dnslayer` evicts a transaction-ID entry when its exchange completes (the response has been forwarded and the `dns_response` hook has run, or `dns_error` has fired) and keeps it while the request is outstanding, so a retransmitted request with the same id before the answer reuses the flow (as upstream), and a response for an evicted id follows the path upstream and main already take for a never-seen id: a new flow is created, `dns_response` fires with no request on the flow, and the response is forwarded to the client (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/dns.py:96-101,164-169`; `go:internal/proxy/layers/dnslayer/dns.go:123-128,271-283`), so eviction changes nothing a hook can observe for a well-behaved exchange; because the map holds the owner's only reference to a flow, eviction first finalises the flow the way shutdown does today for the remaining entries (`Resume`, `Live = false`, `dns.go:88-96`) and only then deletes the entry, so an intercepted or paused flow is never dropped while live; an aggregate bound of 1,024 outstanding (unanswered) entries per connection closes the connection with the invalid-message diagnostic when exceeded (UDP: the tuple is evicted). Acceptance: rows for complete-then-evict (the entry is gone after the response hook; asserted through the owner's map size in a package test), retransmit-before-answer (one flow; `dns_request` fires once per received request, so twice, and the retransmission is forwarded upstream again, exactly as upstream's `handle_request` and main's `owner.request` do, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/dns.py:71-93`, `go:internal/proxy/layers/dnslayer/dns.go:204-205`; one `dns_response`), late response after eviction (a second flow with no request, one `dns_response`, the response forwarded, identical to the never-seen-id row on main), the 1,024 bound on TCP and on a UDP tuple; the existing `test_dns.py`-derived rows pass unchanged; `-count=200 -cpu=1,2,8` and the fuzz target 30 s clean; the `docs/compat.md` row under `internal/proxy/layers/dnslayer` records the deviation (upstream retains every id until shutdown; the Go layer evicts completed exchanges and bounds outstanding ones, retransmissions preserved).
7. *CI skip visibility (lane E).* Deliverable: the `ci.yaml` test step runs `go test -race -count=1 -json ./... | hack/test-skips.sh`, where the script reads the `go test -json` event stream in shell without `jq` (no new dependency), correlating each `skip` action that carries a non-empty `Test` field with the `output` events of the same package and test (the skip message arrives as escaped `Output` lines interleaved with other tests' output, so the reader keys on `Package` and `Test`, not on an `Action` regex alone), prints every per-test skip with its package, test name and message, classifies it (`GOOS` guard, env opt-in, privilege guard, capability, unknown) from the message text, writes the counts to the job summary, reports package-level `no test files` skips separately, preserves the `go test` pipeline exit status (`set -o pipefail`), and exits non-zero for every non-GOOS-guarded per-test skip, unknown messages included, as the user's decision words it (ledger `:1202`: "failing on non-GOOS-guarded skips"); the env opt-in row (`TestAcquirePinnedArtifacts`, `MITMPROXY_TEST_REDIRECTOR_DOWNLOAD=1`) and the privilege-guard rows (`internal/privfile` foreign-owner rows, `privfile_unix_test.go:30-35`, which skip when the test cannot chown) fire on every runner today, so the unit lands only after the user rules on an explicit allowlist for them (§10 item 18); until that ruling the script is landed in report-only mode behind a `TEST_SKIPS_ENFORCE` workflow variable defaulting to off, and the enforcing switch is the unit's last commit. `hack/test-skips_test.sh` feeds recorded `go test -json` samples for the unknown, privilege, capability, environment and `GOOS` cases plus a `no test files` package and asserts the counts and the exit status. Acceptance: an exact-head CI run on the unit's commit prints the counter for all three OSes with zero capability skips and zero unknown skips; `shellcheck` and `actionlint` clean; the shell test runs in the lint job; the four NB-4 rows are listed by name in the script's capability class; the enforcing run fails a synthetic unknown skip (a temporary test in a scratch branch, evidence only).
8. *macOS and Windows local-mode runbooks (lane E).* Deliverable: two runbooks that a person follows on a macOS 12+ machine and on a Windows 11 or `windows-2025` VM: prerequisites (the pinned 0.12.11 artifacts and their SHA-256, extension approval on macOS, UAC on Windows), the exact `mitmdump --mode local:curl` invocation, the `curl` command, what to observe (a captured flow whose `server_conn.address` is the origin; `pid` and `process_name` are native transport `GetExtraInfo` values on the local stream, `go:internal/proxy/modeserver/local.go:292,305`, and not fields of the stock mitmdump client flow, because `connection.Client` carries no extra-info field and the handler builds the client from addresses only, reading `remote_endpoint` alone, `go:internal/proxy/handler_source.go:27-41`; the runbook therefore supplies an exact approved diagnostic procedure for reading them, such as a debug log line the proxy emits at the local stream's admission, and if no such procedure exists on main the runbook marks process-attribution verification BLOCKED, keeps that native evidence absent and never claims the CLI exposes those fields), how to capture packets for the Windows checksum check, teardown, and a results block (date from `date`, OS build, artifact digest, pass/fail per step) that the person fills and commits. Acceptance: the two files exist, pass the docs gate, and name every step the P6 criteria asked for (macOS: artifact SHA-256, extension approval, `local:curl` intercept, pid/process_name; Windows: one UAC prompt, `curl.exe` interception, netstack round-trip, checksum check by capture); the manual execution is a user action outside the round's completion criterion and is recorded, when it happens, by committing the filled results block.
9. *Upstream apply-ACK proposal (lane E).* Deliverable: a proposal document that states the problem (the redirector publishes its endpoint before rules are applied; `rs:src/packet_sources/linux.rs` and `rs:mitmproxy-linux/src/main2.rs` at `51fe2b7`, with line references verified against the clone), the Go-side barrier that works around it (§3.4 item 8), the proposed IPC change (an `InterceptConfApplied` message, or a sequence number echoed back, in `rs:src/ipc/mitmproxy_ipc.proto`), its backward compatibility (a Go frontend that does not receive the ACK within the barrier window keeps the barrier), and the Go adoption plan (one `internal/local` unit after the upstream release that carries it). Acceptance: the document exists under `docs/proposals/`, passes the docs gate, and ends with the exact text of an upstream issue the user may file; nothing is sent to upstream by this round.

**Executable gate recipe and testability**

The per-commit gate is the P3 gate (format, lint with and without the difftest tag, build, vet on four targets, `go test -race -count=1 ./...`, the unit's packages at `-count=5`, `go test -tags integration ./internal/proxy/...`) under the lock, plus the unit's stress rows named in the table. Two additions: a commit that changes `go.mod` runs `go test -race -tags integration ./addons/proxyauth` against the OpenLDAP container locally (L2) and `govulncheck ./...`, and records both in the lane note; and the landing script refuses a body line over 72 characters (NB-2). Every new regression row is red on `6161318` (or on the unit's parent) in a detached scratch overlay and green on the unit's tip; certification and preservation rows are labelled as such in the lane note and in the test's comment. Rows that need the GitHub runner (the freeport Windows row, the CI skip counter, the privileged WireGuard row) take their evidence from the exact-head jobs. Stress counts on Windows (`-count=50`/`-count=200 -cpu=1,2,8`) have no execution path in the baseline jobs (`ci.yaml:55-64` runs `-count=1` under a 30-minute job timeout; the P4+P6 verifier measured the QUIC stress rows at 2,030 s with `-timeout=60m`, `verify-p46.md:78`), so lane E's dedicated stress matrix job runs the named rows of lanes B, C and D at the table's exact settings with an explicit `go test -timeout` and a job timeout above it, uploads the exact-head logs, and lands first; a lane's candidate is accepted only with that job green on its hash. The remote Debian host (`ssh debian-13-trixie.gaudiy-platform`, linux/amd64, go toolchain under `/tmp`) is the second load profile for unit 1 and unit 2; the lane records `date` and the kernel version with each run.

| Criterion | Status against `6161318` | Required change / evidence | Owner / execution |

| LG-3 cause preserved; `protocol abort` disposed; transport-close decision | Open, open, unrepaired | Tracer-gated red/green; cold-load profile; API check and pinning row | Unit 2; all three OSes |
