**Round rules** (continuations of the P4+P6 round, restated so the charters can quote them)

- Workers are spawned as unprefixed agent types (`executor`, `test-engineer`, `writer`, `critic`, `verifier`, `grok-security-reviewer`) with no model override; the security review runs on `grok-security-reviewer` with the tool allowlist (Bash, Read, Glob, Grep, SendMessage); the verifier pass is the one lane the user approved on sonnet. Instructions to workers are English; reports to the user are Japanese.
- Every time a worker or the lead writes comes from `date` in the same command; a worker never lists its environment (`printenv`, `env`, `set`) in any transcript.
- A stray `.omc` directory is deleted only after its resolved absolute path is compared with the resolved state root and found different; the P4+P6 closeout deleted the state root through a trailing-slash mismatch, and the recovery is `.omc/artifacts/p46-session/closeout/RECOVERY-README.md`.
- One commit per landing, fast-forward only, the gate under the lock, exact-head CI on the hash that lands, draft pull requests re-opened after every landing, rulings and landing cues sent over both the inbox and the lane's socket. A lead push or re-stack on a lane's behalf follows §3.4 item 11.
- The one-rerun policy continues for the six carried subtest rows (both `TestTuplePackets` subtests, the DNS, local, streaming and freeport rows) until unit 1 retires each of them by name in the ledger; no other row may be rerun.

**Round pre-mortem (five scenarios)**: (1) the fixture rows pass in isolation and keep failing under load because the cause is a lost datagram, not admission timing: unit 1's positive delivery signals distinguish the two, and a proven loss is reported as a product finding to the lead, not fixed in the fixture; (2) LG-3 never reproduces: the strict four-day box ends the unit with pinning rows and the honest label; (3) the uncovered-sequence count changes on re-derivation: the lane note explains the difference and the user decides whether the count matters; (4) multi-peer routing changes single-peer behaviour: the preservation row on the existing configuration file catches it; (5) the skip counter fails a job for a skip nobody expected: that is the intended behaviour (unknown per-test skips fail closed and need classification before landing), and the report-only mode behind `TEST_SKIPS_ENFORCE` lets the lead see every class on all three runners before the enforcing switch lands.

**Verification at the end of the round**: one scoped security re-confirmation on `grok-security-reviewer` over `internal/proxy/layers/dnslayer` (eviction changes retention semantics under attacker-chosen ids), `internal/wireguard` (routing keyed by untrusted source addresses), `internal/h2/owner.go` (the new error path) and the `httplayer` abort changes; then one verifier pass on sonnet with the same criteria as the P4+P6 round (every regression row red on its parent and green on main, certification and preservation rows labelled, the stress rows at the counts above, main CI and both evidence workflows green on the landed head). The round is complete only on the final immutable head.

### P7 — Rust-derived contentviews, highlighting, process_info

Deliverables
1. `go:contentviews/`: `msgpack` (to YAML, reencode from YAML), `protobuf` (schema-less decode with field-type guessing and YAML tags `!varint !sint !fixed32 !fixed64 !binary`, `protobuf_definitions` `.proto` file/folder loading via protocompile + dynamicpb, best match by `/pkg.Service/Method`, reencode), `grpc` (5-byte frames, `grpc-encoding` gzip/deflate/identity, frames joined by `\n---\n\n`) — semantics from `rs:mitmproxy-contentviews/src/msgpack.rs` and `src/protobuf/` (`view_protobuf.rs`, `view_grpc.rs`); plus the remaining Python views `graphql`, `mqtt`, `socketio`, `wbxml` (port of `py:mitmproxy/contrib/wbxml`), `zip`, `http3` (the `dns` view is owned by P4).
2. `go:contentviews/highlight`: chroma lexers for css/javascript/xml/yaml mapped onto the seven upstream tags; `languages()`/`tags()` equivalents; used by dumper (P1 hook point) and console.
3. `go:internal/processinfo/`: `ActiveExecutables()` grouped by executable path with `display_name`, `is_visible` (macOS window list via purego/CoreGraphics; Windows `EnumWindows` + DWM cloak check), `is_system`; `ExecutableIcon(path)` 32×32 PNG on macOS (`NSRunningApplication`/`NSWorkspace` via purego objc) and Windows (`ExtractAssociatedIconW` + `GetDIBits`), cached by hash; Linux returns no icons (as upstream).

Acceptance criteria: `rs:mitmproxy-contentviews/testdata/protobuf/*.proto` and the `test_roundtrip!` vectors ported (decode→reencode→decode stable); msgpack vectors ported; `py:test/mitmproxy/contentviews/test_*.py` ported per view; highlight: the inputs are the `#[test]` input strings in `rs:mitmproxy-highlight/src/{css,javascript,xml,yaml}.rs` (3, 3, 3 and 2 tests) plus one 200-line real-world sample per language checked into `testdata/highlight/inputs/`; golden outputs are generated once from `mitmproxy_rs==0.12.11` (`uv run --with mitmproxy_rs==0.12.11`; the highlight crate's grammar pins are identical between the 0.12.11 tag and the 51fe2b7 pin, which the P7 pre-step verifies by diffing the resolved `tree-sitter*` package entries (name, version, checksum) in `Cargo.lock` between the two, because `mitmproxy-highlight/Cargo.toml` declares caret ranges such as `tree-sitter-css = "0.25.0"`, `rs:mitmproxy-highlight/Cargo.toml:16-21`), frozen in `testdata/highlight/`, and the Go output must match them token-for-token except on lines listed in `testdata/highlight/EXEMPTIONS` (each with a reason), with at most 5% of lines exempted per language and no exemption on a line whose golden tag is `String` or `Name`; processinfo returns the current test binary on all three OSes, icon bytes decode as PNG on macOS/Windows.

### P8 — mitmweb

Deliverables
1. `go:internal/tools/web/static/` = copy of `py:mitmproxy/tools/web/index.html` + `static/*` (10 files, 2.5 MB, same commit) with the MIT notice; served via `embed` + `http.FileServerFS`; `templates/login.html`.
2. `go:internal/tools/web/`: all 24 routes of `py:mitmproxy/tools/web/app.py` with identical paths, methods, JSON shapes (`flowjson` from P2 with `contentLength`/`contentHash`, options/state/commands/events/filter-help/processes/executable-icon), cookie `mitmproxy-auth-<web_port>`, `?token=`/`Authorization: Bearer`, `web_password` argon2, XSRF cookie `_mitmproxy_xsrf` + `X-XSRFToken`, `Sec-Fetch-Site` 403 rule, gzip; `/updates` WebSocket via gows `Upgrader.UpgradeHTTP` with the server→client messages `flows/add|update|remove|reset|filterUpdate`, `events/add|reset`, `options/update`, `state/update`, client→server `flows/updateFilter`, close 1011 on bad input or queue overflow (§3.1 rule 7); `webaddons` (`web_open_browser`, `web_debug`, `web_port` 8081, `web_host` 127.0.0.1, `web_columns`, `web_theme`); `static_viewer` (`web_static_viewer`); all mutations through `master.Do`.
3. `go:cmd/mitmweb`.

Acceptance criteria: differential contract test — the same `testdata/mitmproxy/flows/*.mitm` loaded into the pinned Python mitmweb and into the Go mitmweb produce `go-cmp`-equal JSON for `/flows`, `/flows/<id>`, `/flows/<id>/{request,response}/content.data`, `/filter-help`, `/commands` and `/options` (after removing the four Go-only option keys and normalising timestamps and ids); `/state` is compared on `contentViews` and `servers` only, with both processes started with the same fixed `--mode regular@127.0.0.1:18080` so `servers[*].listen_addrs` match, one after the other (both bind 127.0.0.1:18080, so the Python process is stopped before the Go process starts) (`version`, `platform` and `localModeUnavailable` are host-specific, `py:mitmproxy/tools/web/app.py:829-840`); on a Linux runner, `/state` `localModeUnavailable` equals `mitmproxy is not running as root.` when mitmweb runs as the non-root runner user (passwordless sudo does not change it) and is `null` when mitmweb runs as root (`rs:mitmproxy-rs/src/server/local_redirector.rs:72-77`); `/processes`, `/executable-icon` and highlighted `content/<view>` are excluded with the reason recorded in the test; the WebSocket message sequence for `flows/add` matches; every object returned by `/flows` decodes with `encoding/json/v2` `RejectUnknownMembers(true)` into Go structs transcribed from `py:web/src/js/flow.ts`, and a test asserts that no required field declared in those structs is missing from the JSON (the 27 fields declared optional with `?:` in `flow.ts` are exempt from the presence check); the embedded React UI driven by chromedp on ubuntu-26.04 lists flows produced by an `httptest` run, opens a flow, edits and replays it, with a screenshot artifact uploaded; XSRF-less POST returns 403; after a flow is added, the `/updates` client receives `flows/add` before the test's 5 s deadline (event-driven wait, no fixed sleep); a hook storm of 10,000 flows while one WebSocket client never reads completes within 10 s and that client is closed with 1011 while others keep receiving; the 8 web options equal upstream's names and defaults.

### P9 — console TUI

Deliverables: `go:internal/tools/console/` on bubbletea v2: window/layout (`console_layout` single/vertical/horizontal, `console_layout_headers`), flow list (virtualised, `console_flowlist_layout`, `console_focus_follow`), flow view with tabs (request/response/detail/websocket messages) and contentview switching, event log (`console_eventlog_verbosity`), options editor, grid editors (headers, query, cookies, form, path, URL-encoded, raw bytes), commander/command palette with completion over `command.Manager`, keymap + `keys.yaml` loading (`console_palette`, `console_palette_transparent`, `console_mouse`, `console_strip_trailing_newlines`, `console_quickhelp_visible`), status bar, help, the 48 `py:mitmproxy/tools/console/consoleaddons.py` commands, external editor/viewer, `console_default_contentview`; notifications via the coalescing dirty flag and `tea.Cmd` goroutines, all flow mutations through `master.Do` (§3.1 rule 7).

Acceptance criteria: teatest golden-frame tests for flow list, flow view and the commander at 80×24 and 200×50 (goldens reviewed against upstream screenshots in `py:docs/src/content/` at creation and frozen); all 48 commands registered (diff-checked list); key bindings from `py:mitmproxy/tools/console/defaultkeys.py` reproduced (table test); the 11 console options equal upstream's names/defaults; a hook storm of 10,000 flows while the `Update` loop is blocked for 2 s does not deadlock and the list shows all flows afterwards; manual QA checklist (navigation, intercept/resume, edit+replay, filter, save) run on macOS Terminal and Linux tmux, recorded in the PR.

### P10 — Starlark scripting

Deliverables: `go:script/` loading each `-s` path as a Starlark module (`scripts` option) via `Program.Init` with unfrozen globals, hook dispatch to top-level functions named after hooks (`request(flow)`, `response(flow)`, `on_load(loader)` (rename of `load`, a Starlark keyword), `configure(updated)`, `running()`, `done()` …) always inside the dispatch domain; `flow`/`request`/`response`/`headers`/`client_conn`/`server_conn`/`websocket`/`tcp`/`udp`/`dns` bindings as `starlark.HasAttrs`/`HasSetField` wrappers with `ctx.log`, `ctx.options`, `ctx.master.commands.call`, `flow.intercept/resume/kill`, `Headers` mapping semantics; the predeclared `concurrent` builtin per §1.3.1 (written `request = concurrent(_request)`, `GlobalReassign` off, hook name and eligibility taken from the global binding name after `Program.Init`; a direct Starlark call of a wrapper fails with the rule 1 text; a second module instance per script from a second `Program.Init`, frozen after init, with its own predeclared object graph whose `Freeze` is a no-op, in direct mode while its top-level code runs under the dispatch lock and in worker mode afterwards; one goroutine and `starlark.Thread` per invocation, bounded per script; deep-copied flow; release and reacquire through `addon.Concurrent`, the flow not intercepted; on the worker, `ctx.options` reads from the latest published immutable options snapshot (an `atomic.Pointer` set by an options subscriber) and `ctx.options` writes, `ctx.master.commands.call` and `ctx.log` through `master.Do`; hot reload and `remove` retire the frozen instance without waiting for in-flight workers (§1.3.1 rule 4); field-level merge-back with index-merged message lists and replayed `intercept`/`resume`/`kill` actions; eligibility limited to the 23 flow hooks) and blocking builtins (`ctx.sleep`, `ctx.http_get`) that park the Starlark thread and release the dispatch lock only at dispatch depth 1 when called from ordinary hooks, and never touch the lock when called on a `concurrent` worker; 1 s mtime hot-reload with `remove`→`load`→`configure`→`running` order; `script.run` command as a synchronous invocation of the script module only (§1.3.1 rule 7: `configure`, `running`, then the `eventsequence` events of each flow; `concurrent` handlers rejected whatever the depth; the run stops at the first error); `script_max_steps` option (`Thread.SetMaxExecutionSteps`); `py:examples/addons/*.py` ported to Starlark as `examples/addons/*.star` where the API allows (the rest listed in `docs/compat.md`).

Acceptance criteria: at least 10 upstream examples (`anatomy.py`, `http-add-header.py`, `http-modify-form.py`, `http-redirect-requests.py`, `http-reply-from-proxy.py`, `websocket-simple.py`, `tcp-simple.py`, `dns-simple.py`, `options-simple.py` (using `on_load`), `commands-simple.py`) run as Starlark through mitmdump with asserted effects; a script keeping a module-level dict counter increments across hooks; `ctx.sleep(1)` inside a hook does not block hooks of other flows (second flow completes within 200 ms); a `request = concurrent(_request)` handler that sleeps 1 s and sets the request header `x-concurrent: 1` runs on a worker goroutine while a second flow's ordinary hook completes within 200 ms, and the origin receives the header (merge-back); five flows through that handler started together all complete within 2 s (the handlers run in parallel); a write to a module-level dict inside the handler raises `cannot insert into frozen hash table` (logged, flow resumes); a module-level `ctx.log("init")` appears twice per `-s` load in a script that binds a `concurrent` wrapper (the second time from the frozen instance's init in direct mode, without waiting on `master.Do`) and once in a script that does not, and once when either script is run through `script.run`; a counter incremented by an ordinary `requestheaders` hook is visible to the next ordinary hook and is seen at its post-init value inside the `concurrent` handler; in a script that binds a `concurrent` wrapper and has a module-level `o = ctx.options`, the handler sets one option through `ctx.options` and another through `o`, both changes reach the options through `master.Do` (the frozen instance's predeclared objects are not made read-only by `Freeze`), and an ordinary hook still sets an option through its own instance's `o`; inside the handler, `ctx.log`, a `ctx.options` read and a `ctx.master.commands.call` complete, the log line reaches `eventstore`, an option read made after a `master.Do` change of that option, which the test makes while the handler runs, returns the old or the new value, and the `-race` run reports nothing; an ordinary `requestheaders` hook that calls `request(flow)` directly, where `request = concurrent(_request)`, fails with `concurrent(_request) cannot be called directly; it runs only when invoked as a hook` (logged, flow resumes) and `_request` does not run; a reload of the script while a `concurrent` handler that sleeps 1 s, sets the request header `x-concurrent: 1` and calls `ctx.log` is in flight returns within 200 ms while the handler is still sleeping, then the handler's log line still reaches `eventstore`, and the origin receives the header (the merge-back still applies while the flow's hook chain waits); while the handler runs, a `master.Do` edit of `flow.comment` survives the merge-back when the handler does not touch `comment`; a WebSocket message from the other direction appended during a `concurrent` `websocket_message` handler is still in the list after merge-back; a write to `flow.server_conn` inside the handler raises an error (logged, flow resumes) and the P10 tests pass under `-race`; `flow.kill()` inside the handler leaves the live flow with `error.msg` `Connection killed.` (`py:mitmproxy/flow.py:35,240`) and the connection closed; `on_load = concurrent(_on_load)` and `configure = concurrent(_configure)` fail the load with `Concurrent decorator not supported for 'on_load' method.` and `... 'configure' method.` (the binding name, not `_on_load`), `tls_clienthello = concurrent(_tls)` and `helper = concurrent(_helper)` with the same text naming `tls_clienthello` and `helper`; `def request(flow): ...` followed by `request = concurrent(request)` fails the load with the resolver's error `cannot reassign global request declared at ...` (`resolve/resolve.go:337-340`); `script.run` over two flows with a script whose `requestheaders` is ordinary and whose `request` is a `concurrent` wrapper, invoked both from a `master.Do` callback (dispatch depth 1) and from inside a hook (depth 2): the handler is not run in either case, the log contains `Async handler request (` and `cannot be called from sync context` (upstream logs it through `safecall` with the prefix `Addon error: `, `py:mitmproxy/addonmanager.py:56-59,279-282`), the first flow's `requestheaders` ran and nothing after the failing `request` event ran (no later event of the first flow, no event of the second flow, `py:mitmproxy/addons/script.py:180-188`), and no other addon's hook was invoked by the run; `script.run` with a script that binds `on_load = concurrent(_on_load)` logs `error in script <path>` with the rule 6 text and invokes no event (`py:mitmproxy/addons/script.py:51,54,60-71,178-179`); `nonblocking.py` ported with its `@concurrent` branch written as `request = concurrent(_request)` (the `async def` branch has no Starlark equivalent) runs with the same observable timing as upstream's thread branch: two requests sent together both log `start  request` about 5 s after their `handle request`, not 10 s; reload test (edit file → new behaviour within 2 s); a script raising an error logs once and does not crash the proxy; a script exceeding `script_max_steps` is aborted with a logged error; `script.run` replays a dump through a script; `scripts` and `script_max_steps` options are registered with the documented type and default.

### P11 — Observability, fuzzing, benchmarks, release hardening

Deliverables: OTel tracer with one span per flow (attributes: mode, method, host, status, sizes, timings from flow timestamps, `http_fidelity`) and connection/flow gauges, OTLP exporter behind `otel_exporter_endpoint`; `pprof_addr` enables `net/http/pprof` on a loopback address; `runtime/metrics` snapshot in `/debug`; nightly 10-minute fuzz job over every target; benchmarks with `b.Loop()` for the parsers and a proxy throughput harness reporting requests/s and MiB/s at 1, 16 and 256 concurrent connections for 1 KiB and 1 MiB bodies over h1, h2, h3 and WS (same scenarios as `py:test/bench/benchmark.py`); `go:.github/workflows/release.yaml` building the three binaries for linux/darwin/windows on tags; `README.md`, `docs/compat.md` (compatibility matrix vs upstream, accepted differences, option precedence note), `docs/dependencies.md` kept current.

Acceptance criteria: 30 s fuzz smoke per target in CI with zero crashes; benchmark baselines recorded under `docs/bench/` for darwin/arm64 and the linux/amd64 host (`ssh debian-13-trixie.gaudiy-platform`), never run in parallel; span per flow visible in an in-memory exporter test with the attribute set above; release workflow produces artifacts on a test tag; `go-licenses` gate green; **final option check**: the set of option names registered after loading all addons of all three binaries equals `testdata/options-upstream.txt` ∪ `testdata/options-go-only.txt`, with each upstream option's type and default equal to the upstream list.

## 6. Pre-mortem (three failure scenarios, with the mitigations attached to phases)

### S1 — A toolchain or dependency update breaks the build mid-port

*What happened*: Go 1.28 ships; `gvisor.dev/gvisor@go` moves to a layout the `go` branch generator mishandles (already observed for `@latest` on 1.27.1), `golang.org/x/net/http2` diverges further from std, `quic-go` 0.64 changes `quic.Stream`, `codeberg.org/miekg/dns` v0.7 changes the `RR` interface, or `go.yaml.in/yaml/v4` rc.7 changes decoding of merge keys. Three phases stall on unrelated compile errors.
*Early signals*: dependabot PRs failing; the `tip` CI job red; adapter packages growing `//nolint` comments.
*Mitigations (P0 deliverable 1 and §4)*: exact pins in `go.mod`; the five adapter packages with contract tests; CI on `stable` (blocking) and `tip` (non-blocking); `oldstable` is impossible because `go 1.27`, `encoding/json/v2` and miekg v2 (`go 1.27.0`) all require 1.27; grouped weekly dependabot; `docs/dependencies.md`.

### S2 — HTTP/1 fidelity regressions surface only in production traffic

*What happened*: the own parser mishandles a corner the upstream tests do not cover (duplicate `Content-Length` with different values, chunk extensions, trailers without `TE`, `HTTP/1.0` + `Connection: keep-alive`, `Expect: 100-continue` with a rejected request, a 204 with a body, obs-fold inside a `Set-Cookie`). Clients see hangs or truncated bodies that upstream mitmproxy handles.
*Early signals*: differential job disagreements ignored as "flaky"; bug reports mentioning specific origins; `http_fidelity` > 0 in traffic that should need no normalisation.
*Mitigations (P1 deliverables 4, 10, 19 and §7)*: `test_read.py`/`test_assemble.py`/`test_http1.py`/`test_http.py` ported as P1 acceptance criteria (each upstream test function mapped to a Go case or listed as not applicable with a reason), with the S2 corners as fixed differential rows; the differential harness `go:internal/difftest` on every PR touching `internal/http1`; fuzz targets seeded by hand from the vectors (upstream's fuzz tests are hypothesis strategies with no corpus, so seeds are written here); the `http_fidelity` counter from P1 (exported through OTel in P11).

### S3 — local mode on macOS/Windows/Linux stalls on artifacts and platform gates

*What happened*: the `mitmproxy_macos` wheel's `.app.tar` is an empty placeholder for unsigned builds (`rs:.github/scripts/build-macos-redirector.sh:55-58`), the chosen wheel's redirector refuses to pair with a non-mitmproxy parent, Gatekeeper/System Extension approval differs between macOS releases, the WinDivert driver (shipped signed by upstream) still needs admin and a reboot-free load, the 0.12.11 Linux redirector's eBPF program does not load on the `ubuntu-26.04` runner kernel, or the IPC schema changes in mitmproxy-rs 0.13. P6 consumes weeks without a CI-verifiable result.
*Early signals*: manual runbook attempts failing twice in a row; IPC parse errors in logs; wheel SHA-256 mismatch.
*Mitigations (P6 pre-tasks and time-boxes)*: pin a **released** 0.12.x wheel whose `.app.tar` is verified non-empty in the P6 pre-task; vendor `mitmproxy_ipc.proto` and test it against frames captured in the pre-task; keep `local` behind an explicit mode spec with a clear `unavailable_reason`; time-boxes of 3 + 4 + 5 days for the three local-mode sub-tasks (Linux packet mode, macOS stream mode, Windows packet mode) with WireGuard and TUN allowed to land alone; otherwise document local mode as "experimental, manually verified" rather than blocking the release.

---

## 8. Risks and mitigations

| # | Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Scope (≈48k Python lines + Rust surface) exceeds available effort | High | High | Phase gates; P1 alone is a usable mitmdump; P8–P10 are independently droppable without breaking earlier phases |
| R2 | Reviewed gows frame API/pin delayed or contract incompatible with relay | Medium | High | In-session parallel gows unit freezes the nine-point relay contract, reviews/pushes a pull request for the user; WebSocket waits and pins that exact branch/main pseudo-version. No interim relay, replace or alternative dependency; failed conformance blocks adapter landing. |
| R3 | `codeberg.org/miekg/dns` pre-1.0 breaking changes | Medium | Medium | adapter in `dns/`; pin v0.6.118; `go.mod` `exclude` on known-bad versions |
| R4 | `go.yaml.in/yaml/v4` still RC | Medium | Low | options codec isolated; v3 fallback is a one-file change |
| R5 | chroma vs tree-sitter tag differences break console/dumper colour parity | High | Low | goldens generated from the pinned Rust highlighter; any non-exempted diff fails CI; exemptions carry reasons (presentation only, not flow data) |
| R6 | macOS `pfioc_natlook` layout differs across OS versions | Medium | Medium | per-OS generated layout + one-time runtime self-check against `pfctl -s state` + pfctl fallback |
| R7 | Binary size (gVisor +3 MB, quic-go, chroma lexers, bubbletea) | Certain | Low | `-s -w` release builds; `mitmdump` excludes console/web packages by package boundaries |
| R8 | A slow path that stays local upstream stalls every hook here, because hooks share one dispatch lock: regexp2 backtracking in filters and host patterns, decoding a compression bomb in `~b*`, contentviews or the dumper, and re-scanning a growing sniff buffer in `next_layer` | Medium | Medium | `MatchTimeout` 100 ms + log + treat as non-match for filters; host patterns accepted, with an abandoned match counted as a match for `ignore_hosts` and as no match for `allow_hosts`, `tcp_hosts` and `udp_hosts` (P1 deliverable 9); decoded bodies bounded by the Go-only option `content_decode_limit`, default 256 MiB (P1 deliverable 18); no streaming zstd decoder yet, so a zstd decode at a small limit still allocates up to about 48 MiB; sniffing capped at 64 KiB (P1 deliverable 9); RSA signing of new leaves under the lock is measured in P11, as upstream also signs on its loop |
| R9 | `--set` and option-precedence semantics mis-ported | Medium | Medium | P0 tests for accumulation, bare flag, `toggle`, precedence order incl. an unpassed dedicated flag; option help text diffed against upstream at the pinned commit; P11 final set equality |
| R10 | Licence contamination | Low | High | `go-licenses` denylist in CI; upstream static assets and fixtures carry MIT notices; redirector binaries are downloaded, never vendored |
| R11 | HTTP/2 flow-control bugs (stalls, window accounting, memory) | Medium | High | pinned SETTINGS, consumption defined per message kind (downstream write when streamed, with send-buffer bytes charged to Σ; body-buffer append when buffered), per-stream growth by doubling after half the window is consumed, up to 16 MiB drawn from a 28 MiB headroom, full chunks passed by ownership from receive to send buffer and a partly filled tail moved only when the send buffer is empty and the client can take data (checked on append and on drain or window reopen, so small streamed bodies are not held back), the `Σ granted stream windows ≤ 128 MiB` invariant asserted on its own counter, receive buffers chunked at `MAX_FRAME_SIZE`, the 99-stall-plus-1 test with a client that returns connection-level window, pins a 1 MiB stream window and stalls each stream after 8 MiB, and the 160 MiB heap bound, rerun with 1 KiB origin frames, a prompt-delivery check for a small streamed body, and the relative netem throughput floor with a pinned client window (ramp concern in §10.10); no RST watchdog (not upstream behaviour) |
| R12 | Starlark cannot express some Python addon idioms (classes with instance state, asyncio, `load` as a function name, decorators); `concurrent` handlers run from a second, frozen module instance, so top-level code runs twice and module state is not shared with them; the field-level merge-back can diverge from upstream's shared-object semantics in a corner the tests miss | Medium | Low | unfrozen module globals via `Program.Init` for state in ordinary hooks; `on_load` rename; `concurrent` per §1.3.1 (the `request = concurrent(_request)` spelling with the hook name taken from the binding, frozen second instance with its own predeclared object graph (direct mode during its init, worker mode afterwards, no-op `Freeze`), release through `addon.Concurrent` without intercepting the flow, worker reads from the latest published options snapshot and worker writes, commands and log lines through `master.Do`, a direct call of a wrapper rejected with a stated text, reload and `remove` that retire the instance without waiting for in-flight workers, field-level merge-back, flow hooks only, `script.run` as a sync run of the script that rejects `concurrent` handlers and stops at the first error) with one P10 criterion per rule; blocking builtins with await-point semantics; the deviations listed in `docs/compat.md`; Go addons remain first-class |
| R13 | Legacy TLS suites (FFDHE, SSLv3, RC4, 3DES, export) cannot be intercepted with std `crypto/tls` | Certain | Medium for IoT/legacy users | Documented in §1.4 and `docs/compat.md`; `ssl_insecure` still disables verification; a utls/OpenSSL-backed TLS layer is a possible follow-up, not in this plan |
| R14 | ECH-capable clients fail against the proxy when HTTPS records still advertise ECH | Medium | Medium | `strip_ech` (default true) in P4; documented behaviour of Go's ECH ordering in §1.4 |
| R15 | Dispatch-lock discipline violated by future code (stale frame use, blocking under the lock) | Medium | High | epoch-checked frames panic in test builds; rule 7 storm tests in P8/P9; layers fire hooks only through the per-connection hook runner, checked by a grep in the P1 integration script; a `go vet`-style custom analyser (follow-up) |
| R16 | Parallel work on one phase changes shared contracts under their consumers; in the foundation phase a fix introduced a new defect twice (an overlapping regex rewrite, a command deadlock) | Medium | High | P1 lands a serial spine whose first commit freezes the contracts with conformance tests before other units code against them; each P0 file edited in P1 has one owner; a contract change is agreed with its consumers before it lands; adversarial tests (negative watchdog case, `HookFunc` atomicity, two-direction TCP race) are part of the criteria |
| R17 | Two tracks (P4 and P6) land on one main through one lead: landing, pre-verification and rulings become the bottleneck; a shared package edited by both tracks produces rebase conflicts the landing script refuses; CI queues double | High | Medium | Exclusive package ownership per track with serial transfers (`modeserver`), dependency commits landed first and alone, an `flock`-based gate lock, two evidence workflows (`ci.yaml`/`network.yaml` for P4, `packet-modes.yaml` for P6), per-commit landing in `PUSHED` order with one unlanded commit per lane and rebase before review, one round-end security and verifier pass instead of two; at most four implementation lanes are live per track (eight combined) behind one serialised landing queue and one gate lock |

---

## 9. ADR — Go port of mitmproxy with a hybrid core and full upstream compatibility

- **Decision**: build `mitmproxy-go` as a hybrid (sans-I/O parsers + goroutine/`net.Conn` orchestration with a single hook dispatch domain using epoch-checked re-entrancy frames and non-blocking frontend edges) port with full wire fidelity (own HTTP/1 parser, independent h2 endpoints on x/net Framer + hpack with bounded windows, own HTTP/3 framing on quic-go streams + qpack, frame-level WebSocket relay on an extended gows) and full upstream compatibility (flow format v18–v21 read / v21 write, CA layout, filter grammar, options with upstream precedence, hooks, mode specs, mitmweb API with the embedded upstream React build), covering HTTP/1–3, WebSocket, TCP, UDP, DNS, DTLS, modes regular/reverse/upstream/transparent/socks5/dns/wireguard/tun(Linux)/local(macOS, Windows and Linux via upstream redirectors), three binaries (mitmdump, mitmproxy TUI on bubbletea v2, mitmweb), Starlark scripting, Rust-derived contentviews, highlighting, process info and OpenTelemetry.
- **Drivers**: D1 asset compatibility, D2 maintainability on Go 1.27+, D3 delivery-risk control.
- **Alternatives considered**: O2 std `net/http` proxy with data-level compatibility; O3 faithful sans-I/O port; O4 fork of go-mitmproxy; an actor-goroutine dispatch variant of O1 (§2).
- **Why chosen**: O1 is the only option that satisfies the user's explicit fidelity and compatibility choices while using Go's native TLS/QUIC I/O; it reuses upstream vectors where they are cheap to port and avoids adapters where they are not.
- **Consequences**: largest code volume of the options (estimated 60–80k Go lines including tests); the h2 and h3 state machines are written here (x/net and quic-go contribute framing, HPACK/QPACK and the QUIC transport only); two coding styles that the dispatch domain and package layout keep apart; a lock discipline enforced by tests rather than types (R15); dependency on the user's gows extension; pre-1.0 dependencies (quic-go, qpack, miekg v2, yaml v4 RC) isolated behind adapters; local mode depends on upstream artifacts and manual verification; legacy TLS suites and ECH termination are not supported (R13, R14); the h2 window policy and TLS half-close (close_notify + FIN) deviate from upstream and are documented; Starlark `concurrent` is a predeclared builtin written `request = concurrent(_request)` because Starlark has no decorator syntax, runs handlers from a second, frozen module instance (top-level code runs twice per load, module-level mutable state is not shared between a concurrent handler and the main-thread hooks), gives handlers a read-only snapshot of the connection objects, is rejected on the 21 non-flow hooks other than `load`/`configure`, fails a direct Starlark call of a wrapper with a stated error where upstream returns an unawaited coroutine, and prints the script path where upstream's sync-context error prints the module repr (§1.3.1); DTLS accepts only `tls_version_*` windows that contain `TLS1_2` (P3 deliverable 4); all of these are listed in `docs/compat.md`.
- **Follow-ups** (not in this plan): `py:mitmproxy/io/compat.py` migrations for flow formats < 18; DTLS 1.0/1.3 (pion speaks 1.2 only); QUIC datagrams; Windows transparent mode (`py:mitmproxy/platform/windows.py` pydivert path); tree-sitter parity for highlighting; `mitmproxy_rs.certs` (macOS keychain install); an OpenSSL/utls-backed TLS layer for legacy suites; a static analyser for the dispatch-lock rules; kubernetes/transparent-vm how-tos.

---

## 10. Open items and assumptions to confirm at approval

Decided by the user during planning (recorded here so they are not re-asked): flow formats v18–v21 migrated and < 18 rejected; option precedence reproduces upstream; h2 bounded windows (100 streams, 1 MiB initial, 16 MiB max, 128 MiB granted-window budget); TLS half-close as `close_notify` + FIN; Starlark `concurrent` provided with a deep-copied flow, built per §1.3.1 (lead decision on the rev 4 review: a second frozen module instance per script, release through `addon.Concurrent`, flow hooks only; lead decisions on the rev 5 review: a predeclared `concurrent` builtin written `request = concurrent(_request)`, `GlobalReassign` off, the hook name taken from the global binding, and `script.run` a synchronous run of the script module only that rejects `concurrent` handlers whatever the depth and stops at the first error); h2 consumption defined as the downstream write for streamed messages (send-buffer bytes charged to Σ) and the body-buffer append for buffered ones, window growth by doubling after half the window is consumed, and a budget-test client that returns connection-level window (lead decisions on the rev 5 review); DTLS, TUN mode (Linux) and Linux local mode added to scope; Linux local mode follows upstream exactly (interactive `sudo echo -n` probe, `unavailable_reason` `mitmproxy is not running as root.`); TUN e2e binds curl to the device with a loopback origin and no route; h2 budget criterion uses 99 stalled streams plus one. Lead decisions on the rev 6 review: the frozen `concurrent` instance gets its own predeclared object graph, in direct mode while its top-level code runs under the dispatch lock and in worker mode afterwards, where `ctx.options` reads come from the latest atomically published immutable snapshot and writes, `ctx.master.commands.call` and `ctx.log` go through `master.Do`; h2 full chunks move to the send buffer by ownership and a partly filled tail moves only when the send buffer is empty and the client can take it, with a 1 KiB-frame rerun of the budget test; the Python `$` claim stays and P0 gains the vectors and differential rows that check it; a direct call of a `concurrent` wrapper is an error; hot reload and `remove` retire the frozen instance without waiting for in-flight workers; the sync-context error prints the script path.

Decided by the user for P1 (2026-10-05 12:15 JST): the raw TCP layer package is `internal/proxy/layers/tcplayer` (later `udplayer`, `dnslayer`); the cert-only `mitmproxy-ca-cert.p12` was to be written by a hand-written encoder (replaced at 13:00 JST, below); htpasswd accepts exactly upstream's bcrypt and `{SHA}` entries, ported onto `x/crypto/bcrypt` and `crypto/sha1`, and `go-htpasswd` left the manifest; the image contentview ports upstream's four parsers; LDAP `proxyauth` is tested against an OpenLDAP service container in the ubuntu CI job; the in-process app host is `addons/apphost`; onboarding static assets are copied byte-exact; the css and javascript contentviews use regexp2 with the 100 ms limit. Decided by the user for P1 (2026-10-05 12:22 JST): handlers of `next_layer` choose layers through a public descriptor in `addon/hookdata` that the proxy resolves through an internal registry (refined by the lead to an ordered stack, `hookdata.LayerStack []LayerSpec`, because upstream's `next_layer` returns pre-built stacks); `httpmsg.Message` gains `Stream` and `StreamFunc`, the function called under the dispatch lock for each chunk; decoded bodies are bounded on every `Content`/`Text` path by the Go-only option `content_decode_limit` (default 256 MiB); host patterns that need regexp2 are accepted and an abandoned match counts as a match for `ignore_hosts` and as no match for `allow_hosts`, `tcp_hosts` and `udp_hosts`, logged; the command lexer, argument types, `Execute` and the `core` commands stay in P1, with flow-spec arguments failing until the `view` addon exists; `ciphers_client`/`ciphers_server` accept only colon-separated exact OpenSSL suite names, with names mapped both ways and OpenSSL names recorded; `--version` prints `Mitmproxy-go`, `Go` and `Platform` lines; the `browser` addon is tested with a recording executable first on `PATH` on all three CI operating systems. Re-decided by the user for P1 (2026-10-05 13:00 JST), after the decision above turned out to rest on a false premise (`cryptography` writes a MAC on both `.p12` files, and the upstream confdir fixture carries one too): both `.p12` files are written with go-pkcs12, their structural differences from `cryptography`'s files are listed in `docs/compat.md`, and the certificate criterion requires only that the pinned Python's `pkcs12.load_pkcs12` reads both files and recovers the same certificate and key. Lead rulings for P1: the layer contract lives in `internal/proxy/layer`; CONNECT and upstream CONNECT belong to the HTTP layer as upstream; no tunnel package; `OnDispatchStart`/`OnDispatchEnd` stay as process-level callbacks with their godoc fixed; the `http_fidelity` counter is per proxy instance; no streaming zstd decoder in P1; command lines need no new path, because every command call already runs through the manager's runner under the dispatch lock.

Decided for P3 after its phase-entry check: gows is an in-session parallel frame-API unit with exact branch/main pseudo-version handoff and no fallback; default `disableh2c` addon remains loaded, positive h2c available only to embedders omitting it; hookdata preserves TLS Config and adds typed DTLSConfig under IsDTLS; WebSocket layer is `internal/proxy/layers/websocket`, Fragmentizer compares total plaintext length (same-length edits preserve lengths), with compressed FIN-only decode/single-frame behavior documented as a deviation. Contracts land serially before consumers; shared file writers and ALPN/factories atomic promotion are fixed in P3. DTLS v3.1.10/1.2 and the 36-pair rule remain settled. Bounded h2 windows are per receive direction and charged by original input including padding, with receipt/Send consumption and invariant rules in P3. Privfile ACL/privileged filesystem CI follow-ups move to P4.

Decided by the user for the P4/P6 round (2026-10-07 15:35 JST): the whole of P6 is brought forward and runs in parallel with P4 after the P3.5 follow-up round; P5 follows both; `TransparentProxy` moves from P5 to the P6 track; the round's carried items from the P3 and P3.5 reviews are assigned to the P4 track's proxy core hardening unit; the round ends with one scoped security re-confirmation and one verifier pass over both tracks.

Decided by the user at the closeout of the P4+P6 round (2026-10-09 22:02 to 22:29 JST, three `AskUserQuestion` batches, ledger "USER CLOSEOUT DECISIONS batch 1..3"): every deviation of the round is ratified (§3.4); the CI `ubuntu-26.04` runner is the Linux reference host for native local-mode evidence, macOS and Windows local mode get a manual runbook, and the remote Debian BTF incompatibility is a host note; the netrc PAT was removed and rotated; the Go readiness barrier stays and an explicit apply-ACK is proposed upstream as a follow-up; a fixture-hardening unit is funded and the one-rerun policy continues until it lands; the HTTP/3 abort table (LG-3, NB-9), QUIC `protocol abort` and transport-close parity are tracked as one unit, with LG-1 closed as "cause unproven, fixture hardened"; the deterministic real-HTTP/3 acceptance follow-up, WireGuard multi-peer routing, the HTTP/2 malformed-present framer path, DNS transaction-ID eviction (parity kept until then, `docs/compat.md` deviation when it lands) and a CI skip counter are funded; the three dirty lane worktrees were to be snapshotted and all round worktrees removed (executed; the snapshots were lost with the `.omc` deletion, the worktrees' last pushed state survives on the `p46/*` branches and in `closeout/p46-unmerged-branches.bundle`). The round section "The follow-up round after P4+P6" in §5 carries these as nine units.

Still open:
1. **Legacy TLS suites and ECH** (§1.4, R13, R14): acknowledge the loss or request the OpenSSL/utls follow-up now.
2. **Redirector wheel version**: 0.12.11 digests are recorded in §1.4; the P6 pre-task still extracts the macOS `.app.tar` and confirms the IPC schema matches the vendored 51fe2b7 proto by decoding captured frames.
3. **gows extension — settled**: an in-session parallel unit in `github.com/zchee/gows` reviews/pushes the frame API and opens a pull request for the user to merge. P3 pins its exact branch pseudo-version first and main version after merge; no interim relay, replace or replacement library.
4. **DTLS versions — settled**: pion/dtls v3.1.10 speaks DTLS 1.2 only; P3 deliverable 4 retains the TLS option values and accepts exactly windows containing TLS1_2 (36 ordered pairs per side, 15 accepted / 21 rejected). Rejection fires the failed hook with the fixed log and starts no handshake; no further confirmation required.
5. **Highlight goldens** come from the 0.12.11 wheel while the Rust pin is 51fe2b7; the P7 pre-step diffs the resolved `tree-sitter*` entries in `Cargo.lock` between the two and the plan assumes they match.
6. **Linux redirector on the runner kernel — settled (revision 11)**: the 0.12.11 redirector's eBPF program loads on the `ubuntu-26.04` runner (CI-34: `TestLinuxLocalExecutable/success:_curl_process_intercepted` PASS on the privileged packet-modes job, 2026-10-09) once the Go side waits for the readiness barrier (§3.4 item 8); it does not load on the remote Debian 13 kernel 6.12 host (`BPF_BTF_LOAD Invalid argument`, R46-140), which stays a host note. The runner is the Linux reference host (user decision, closeout item 6).
7. **Loopback destinations under Linux local mode**: still not verified; the P6 criterion uses a non-loopback origin address so it does not depend on the answer, and the revision 11 round adds no row for it.
8. **Re-attaching a persistent TUN** (Critic, unscored, asked whether a leaked descriptor makes a second attach fail with `EBUSY`): the P6 design hands wireguard-go a close-on-exec duplicate and always closes its original, and the fallback runs before any hand-over, so no descriptor leaks on the fallback; the P6 TUN criterion starts the fallback path twice to show it. If `CreateUnmonitoredTUNFromFD` fails after wrapping the duplicate, the mode start fails and the duplicate stays attached until the `os.File` finalizer closes it, so an immediate restart could still see `EBUSY`; this error path is not expected in practice and is not covered by a criterion.
9. **`IFF_VNET_HDR` on an existing persistent TUN** (Critic on rev 5, MEDIUM confidence, from memory of `drivers/net/tun.c` and not checked against source: `tun_set_iff` rewrites the feature flags on every attach, so the attach is likely accepted; the P6 lane confirms): the P6 fallback attaches with `IFF_TUN | IFF_NO_PI | IFF_VNET_HDR` (the `CreateTUN` flags, `tun/tun_linux.go:566`), while the P6 criterion pre-creates `tun0` with `ip tuntap add dev tun0 mode tun user <runner user>`, which does not set `IFF_VNET_HDR`. Whether the kernel accepts that flag mismatch when attaching to an existing device is not verified. The P6 lane either creates the test device with matching flags (`ip tuntap add ... vnet_hdr`) or confirms that the attach succeeds, and records which one in the PR.
10. **netem throughput ramp**: the initial 1 MiB window grows toward 16 MiB, so the whole-transfer 80% floor is a measured requirement, not an assumed result. The dedicated isolated Linux gate keeps identical 16 MiB client windows/direct settings and archives bytes/durations. A missed floor blocks landing; neither excluding the ramp nor raising initial windows is authorized by this item. Any changed policy requires a new explicit decision before implementation, not automatic relaxation after failure.
11. **Protocol-entry follow-ups — assigned in P3**: HTTP integration owns HTTP head/h2 preface/HEADERS deadlines and replay loaders bounded by flowio/har MaxEntries; DTLS owns ClientHello/head deadline; gates/cleanup owns the three filter/regex minors (anchor-free capture-table check, typed capacity errors, builder concatenation). These remain separate from idle expiry and h2 replay gate removal.
12. **Filesystem follow-ups — carried to P4**: Windows privfile ACL/check-use hardening and privileged foreign-owner privfile CI stay outside P3's network/protocol scope. Netem privilege setup does not close either filesystem item.
13. **502 body from origin GOAWAY debug data** (P3.5 security re-confirmation, APPROVE with a qualification): the client-facing 502 for a GOAWAY-derived stream failure carries the origin's debug data (at most 131,064 bytes, HTML-escaped to at most about 787 KB, control bytes kept, origin secrets not redacted). The hardening unit proposes; the default is a fixed client message with the bounded text kept in the log and the flow error; the choice is recorded in `docs/compat.md`.
14. **Packet-transport loopback flake — carried into the revision 11 round as unit 1**: `TestTuplePackets/preserves_boundaries` fails only under a full-tree `-race` run (2 of 3 gates on macOS in the P3.5 round) and never in isolation at `-count=500` on macOS or Linux; the recorded failure is the first-tuple admission wait in `acceptTestTuple` (the 10 s context at `transport_test.go:43-47`, reported at `:64`), not the later `ReadFrom` deadline (`:70`); the P4+P6 hardening unit landed the admission-versus-delivery fixture (`d68edb6`, NB-10) and the row still fails under load as "first datagram socket delivery did not complete" (`transport_test.go:135,140`), together with four sibling rows (the LI-2 family, CI-31, CI-38). Unit 1 of the follow-up round owns the six subtest rows in five families with positive delivery signals, a hang detector with a goroutine dump, the proxytest request-hook barrier and the Windows excluded-range skip in `freeport`, proved under two fresh full-tree `-race` runs, `-count=200` on each row and the remote Debian profile.
15. **Items the closeout left with the user, outside the plan's scope**: rotation of the credentials a stopped worker printed into transcript `608dc733` (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_CLAUDE_MANAGED_AGENTS_API_KEY`, `ALIBABA_CLOUD_MODELSTUDIO_API_KEY`, `GROK_API_KEY`, `GROK_DEPLOYMENT_KEY`, `NGROK_API_TOKEN`, `NGROK_TOKEN`, `CLAUDE_CODE_MESSAGING_TOKEN`) and of `CC_LANGSMITH_API_KEY` (embedded in the lead's shell snapshot), and whether to delete that transcript; the verifier's 112 evidence files under `reviews/verify-p46-evidence` are unrecoverable (the report survives in full, the checksums are left mismatching on purpose), so a later audit of the P4+P6 round reads the report, the CI job ids and the surviving lane bundles, not those files.
16. **Transport-close parity on quic-go v0.63.0** (unit 2 of the follow-up round): the public API offers `CloseWithError` (application close) only; whether a caller can emit a transport-level `CONNECTION_CLOSE` with a chosen code and frame type is decided by the unit's API check, and "unrepaired, pinned by a row" is an allowed outcome. A quic-go upgrade that adds such an API reopens the item.
17. **Manual runbook execution** (unit 8): the macOS and Windows runs are user actions outside the round's completion criterion; until a filled results block is committed, the native macOS and Windows local-mode evidence is absent, and `docs/compat.md` says so. Process attribution (`pid`, `process_name`) is not a field of the stock mitmdump client flow (it lives on the local stream's `GetExtraInfo`, `go:internal/proxy/modeserver/local.go:292,305`), so the runbook needs an approved diagnostic procedure to read it or marks that step BLOCKED.
18. **Skip-counter allowlist (unit 7, needs a user ruling before the enforcing switch lands)**: the user's decision fails every non-GOOS-guarded skip (ledger `:1202`), and two families fire on every runner today: the env opt-in `TestAcquirePinnedArtifacts` (`MITMPROXY_TEST_REDIRECTOR_DOWNLOAD=1`) and the `internal/privfile` foreign-owner privilege guards (`privfile_unix_test.go:30-35`). Options: (a) allowlist them by exact package, test name and message in the script, reviewed with every change; (b) convert them so they do not skip (the artifact row becomes a `-tags` row run by a dedicated job, the privfile rows run in the privileged job); (c) keep the literal rule and accept a permanently red test job. The lead recommends (a) now and (b) as a follow-up; until the ruling the script runs in report-only mode.
19. **Pinned `mitmproxy-rs` source for unit 9**: no local clone of `mitmproxy-rs@51fe2b7` exists on the planning machine (`testdata/mitmproxy-rs` holds fixtures only), so the proposal's `rs:` line references are written only after the lane clones the pinned commit and verifies them; the plan's own `rs:` citations in this revision are carried from revision 10 and are marked "not checkable here" by the critic.

## 11. Confidence scores (0.0–1.0)

| Dimension | Score | Reasoning |
|---|---|---|
| Performance | 0.75 | Goroutine-per-connection with native `crypto/tls`, own h2/h3 endpoints with pinned 1–16 MiB windows and gows's SIMD paths should match or beat upstream's asyncio; hook CPU is serialised by the dispatch domain exactly as upstream; gVisor adds overhead only for packet modes |
| Scalability | 0.75 | Connection-count scaling follows Go's runtime; I/O, TLS and parsing stay parallel; only addon code is serialised; per-connection memory is bounded by the h2 budget |
| Reliability | 0.70 | HTTP/1 vectors + differential + fuzz tests, bounded h2 windows, the epoch-checked dispatch domain and non-blocking frontend edges remove the main correctness hazards; the scope is still large and three pre-1.0 deps are in the critical path |
| Cost effectiveness | 0.50 | The full-compat, full-fidelity choice is the most expensive option; value is high only if upstream asset compatibility and mitmweb reuse are actually used |

- 2026-10-05 rev 3: applied the second Architect review (APPROVE WITH CHANGES) and the second Critic review (REVISE, 9 items), both verified against the upstream clones, h2 4.4.1 and bubbletea v2 sources:

  - §5: P0 trimmed to data model + codecs + registries (certs and command manager → P1, HAR + `flowjson` → P2); raw TCP layer moved into P1 (removes the P1→P3 inversion); P3 h2 SETTINGS numbers, growth and budget, h2 4.4.1 normalisation steps, revised intercept/throughput criteria, autobahn via `reverse:http://`; P4 `strip_dns_https_records` described as implemented (ECH param cleared, h3 ALPN removed, no records removed), h3 frame-parser scope (GREASE, reserved frames, critical streams, push), QUIC Initial fuzz target, qpack-level header-order criterion; P6 one-client pipe listener and the exact Linux unavailable text; P7 goldens generated from the pinned Rust highlighter with an exemption file; P8 route equality narrowed with reasons, storm test; P9 storm test; P10 blocking builtins instead of `concurrent`; P11 final option-set equality; per-addon option tests in P1, P3, P4, P8, P10.
  - §1.2/§P0: state keys taken from the `get_state` sources and the v21 fixture (the connection `state` key is gone since format 20; research §7 corrected); tnetstring reverse dict order; `>21` error text and tuple rendering; precedence test with an unpassed dedicated flag.
  - Citations fixed: `rs:mitmproxy-contentviews/src/hex_dump.rs`, `hex_stream.rs`, `msgpack.rs`, `src/protobuf/view_grpc.rs`; `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/tls.py:32-71`; `modes.py:133`; `optmanager.py:310-347`; `compat.py:391-433`; `actions/setup-go@v7`.
  - §2: principles renamed Pr1–Pr5; actor-model variant recorded; R15 added; §10 items 8–10 added for the three documented deviations.
  - Amended before the verification pass (advisor): the command registration core stays in P0 (`Loader.AddCommand` needs it) and the lexer/types/`core` commands move to P1; the h2 4.4.1 `CONNECTION_HEADERS` set is enumerated; the ALPN rule names `client_conn.alpn`.
- 2026-10-05 rev 4: applied the Critic verification pass on rev 3 (all nine rev-2 items confirmed landed; citations hold; 126/133 criteria testable; verdict REVISE on three contradictions and six placement/wording items, all applied) and the user's answers to the deviation and scope questions:
  - P3: the h2 budget now bounds the **sum of granted stream windows** (100 MiB reserved + 28 MiB growth headroom = 128 MiB), with the 100-stream grow-then-stall criterion; the h2 header criterion is split into the three option cases (`validate_inbound_headers` on → GOAWAY `PROTOCOL_ERROR`; off → lowercased with the upstream log line; never silent); "CONNECT over h2" replaced by the secure-web-proxy `http/1.1` negotiation; the netem floor is relative (≥ 80% of the direct transfer on `lo` with 64 MiB TCP buffers).
  - §3.1/P1: ALPN rule gains the secure-web-proxy override (`tlsconfig.py:254-262`) and the server-side mirror rule (`:293-302`); P1 restricts both sides to HTTP/1 ALPNs until P3, with criteria.
  - P0: precedence test goes through `options.Load` with a passed-flag map (no CLI in P0); the command type identities join P0; the fixture list adds `corrupted_har/`, `compressed.zhar`, the `.pem` files, `htpasswd`, `test_config.yml`, `image.*`; the byte-identity reference is what the pinned Python writes; the `> 21` error text places the period after the suffix.
  - P2: the `flowjson` DNS branch is gated on P4 and stubbed until then; `update_alt_svc` ownership stays in P2.
  - P6 → P8: the mitmweb `/state` `localModeUnavailable` clause moved to P8 with pinned listen addresses; P8 decodes `/flows` strictly against `flow.ts` structs.
  - P7: highlight inputs defined (the `#[test]` strings plus one real sample per language), exemptions capped at 5% per language and none on `String`/`Name`; the `dns` view is owned by P4.
  - Citation drifts fixed: `tnetstring.py:70-148`, `tls.py:606-617`, `http/__init__.py:1178-1188`.
  - User decisions: Starlark `@concurrent` provided (§1.3, P10, R12); DTLS via `pion/dtls/v3` v3.1.10 (§1.1, §1.4, §4, P3); Linux local mode and TUN mode added to P6 with criteria; §10 rewritten to list the decisions and the five remaining open items; 0.12.11 wheel digests recorded in §1.4.
- 2026-10-05 rev 5 (plan date from `date`: 2026-10-05 05:49 JST): applied the Critic verification pass on rev 4 (verdict REVISE; four MAJOR and several MINOR findings in Part C, four citation drifts in Part B, six required changes) and the lead's decisions on the open design points. Every Critic claim was checked against the clones (mitmproxy@3368a0a, mitmproxy-rs@51fe2b7) and the module cache (go.starlark.net@v0.0.0-20260930220527-d7438c5a85ac, pion/dtls/v3@v3.1.10, wireguard-go@v0.0.0-20260522210424-ecfc5a8d5446, x/sys@v0.48.0); **no claim was rejected**. Two cited ranges were adjusted: the no-ALPN branch is `tlsconfig.py:304-310` (the Critic wrote `:304-312`), and the sudo probe is `linux.rs:30-41` (the Critic wrote `:31-40`).
  - §1.3, new §1.3.1, P10, R12, §9 (Critic C1, required change 2): `@concurrent` redesigned. A second `Program.Init` instance per script, frozen after init, because a live-globals snapshot cannot be built (`interp.go:640-641`, `value.go:729-734`, `eval.go:177-181`); top-level code runs twice per load and module state is not shared with concurrent handlers (documented deviation). Release and reacquire go through `addon.Concurrent` and the flow is no longer intercepted (`concurrent.py:18-30`). One goroutine per invocation, bounded at min(32, GOMAXPROCS + 4) per script (CPython's default executor size). This refines the lead's "its worker thread": a single worker could not give the P10 `nonblocking.py` criterion upstream's parallel timing, and frozen modules may be called from many threads (`doc/spec.md:1390-1393`). The merge-back covers every flow state key, uses a three-way field merge, merges message lists by index, and replays `intercept`/`resume`/`kill` as actions; the connection objects are a read-only snapshot in the copy (deviation, they are goroutine-owned). `on_load`/`configure` are rejected with upstream's text (`concurrent.py:13-16`), and the other 21 non-flow hooks are rejected too (deviation). At depth > 1 the handler is not run and the call fails with upstream's error (`addonmanager.py:279-282`), matching §3.1 rule 4 (lead decision after the rev 5 report; an earlier draft of rev 5 ran it inline as a deviation). Blocking builtins on a worker never touch the dispatch lock. P10 gains one criterion per rule.
  - P3 (C4, required change 1): the budget criterion now runs 99 stalled streams and a 100th that completes on its reserved 1 MiB, with the Σ-granted counter asserted directly (reaching 127 MiB, and ≤ 128 MiB at every sample). Heap is sampled as `/gc/heap/live:bytes` after `runtime.GC()`. The 16 MiB margin is explained by a new deliverable rule: receive buffers are chunked at `MAX_FRAME_SIZE`, so at most one partial 128 KiB chunk per stream (12.5 MiB), plus about 3.5 MiB for the Framers, HPACK and stream state. The netem client's stream and connection windows are pinned at 16 MiB. R11 updated.
  - §1.4, P3 deliverable 4, P3 criteria, §10.4 (C2, required change 5): pion calls `GetCertificate` when `Certificates` is empty or SNI is present (`certificate.go:129-135`), so `Certificates` stays empty; a no-SNI pion client criterion was added (`flight1handler.go:129-131`, `tlsconfig.py:615-623`). There is now one DTLS version rule: accept a window that contains `TLS1_2` and reject min `TLS1_3` or max `SSL3`/`TLS1`/`TLS1_1`, checked per connection with one logged text. §10.4 points to it, and a 36-pair table test was added. The rule does not state upstream's outcome, because libssl's handling of TLS version values on a DTLS context cannot be verified from the clone.
  - P6 deliverable 3, P6 and P8 criteria (C3, required change 4): Linux local mode follows upstream exactly. The probe is the interactive `sudo echo -n` (`linux.rs:30-41`), `sudo` is found through `PATH`, and a failure is logged as `Failed to elevate privileges` and exits 1 through `errorcheck`. `unavailable_reason` is upstream's euid check, `mitmproxy is not running as root.` (`local_redirector.rs:72-77`). That function is read only by `/state` (`app.py:839`), so P8 asserts the text for a non-root mitmweb and `null` for root, and the "sudo disabled" setup was removed from P8. P6 tests a stub `sudo` that exits 1 on `PATH`, keeps the passwordless path on `ubuntu-26.04`, leaves the password prompt manual-only, and adds an `/etc/hosts` entry for `example.test` that points at a non-loopback origin.
  - P6 deliverable 4 and TUN criterion (C3, required change 3): curl binds to the device (`--interface <tun-name>`), no route is added, the origin is on `127.0.0.1`, and the test runs under sudo (`test_mode_servers.py:445-447`, `modes.md:392`). The device is set up with a hand-written `TUNSETIFF` and `CreateUnmonitoredTUNFromFD`, not `CreateTUN`/`CreateTUNFromFile` (`tun_linux.go:567-569,626-629`). MTU, address and link-up are set with `x/sys/unix` ioctls, `disable_rp_filter` is ported in full with a `conf/all/rp_filter` read-back, and the fallback path stays unconfigured as upstream's does (`tun.rs:67-83`). The fallback is started twice to answer the Critic's `EBUSY` question.
  - Part B citations: `tun.rs:89-107`/`:174-213`/`:67-83`, `linux.rs:29-111` (probe `:30-41`, stdout read `:103-111`), all 14 `CommandTypes` entries in P0 deliverable 7 (`types.py:482-497`, adding `CutSpec`, `StrSeq`, `Bytes`; the only P0 change), and "the log contains" for the `_http2.py:319` message.
  - C5: §1.2 netstack is used by Windows and Linux packet mode and TUN. §3.2 `internal/local` cites the Linux sources, and a new `internal/tun/` package was added. S3 now has three local sub-tasks, lets WireGuard and TUN land alone, and covers the Linux eBPF risk. §7 rows gain TUN, Linux local, DTLS and the DTLS ClientHello fuzz target. P8 runs the Python and Go `/state` processes one after the other and exempts the 27 optional `flow.ts` fields from the presence check. P7 diffs the `tree-sitter*` entries in `Cargo.lock`, and §10.5 says the same. The §3.1 ALPN rule covers the forced `http/1.1` preset (`:97-101`, `:257-260`) and the no-ALPN upstream offer (`:304-310`).
  - P6 deliverable 3 and the FreeBSD criterion (lead decision after the rev 5 report, full compatibility): platforms without local mode use upstream's text `Local redirect mode is not supported on <GOOS>` (`local_redirector.rs:79-83,158-161`) instead of the Go-specific text; TUN mode on other platforms likewise uses upstream's `TUN proxy mode is only supported on Linux` (`rs:mitmproxy-rs/src/server/tun.rs:46-47,80-83`).
  - §10: decisions updated; open items 6–9 added (runner kernel eBPF, loopback under Linux local mode, persistent-TUN re-attach, `IFF_VNET_HDR` on an existing persistent TUN).
  - §11 unchanged: the changes make criteria precise and correct the designs, and none of them changes the expected performance, scalability, reliability or cost of the chosen architecture.
- 2026-10-05 rev 6 (plan date from `date`: 2026-10-05 06:44 JST): applied the Critic verification pass on rev 5 (verdict REVISE; M1, M2, M3 major, m1 to m5 minor) with the lead's decisions on each. Every claim was checked against the clones and the module cache (go.starlark.net@v0.0.0-20260930220527-d7438c5a85ac, wireguard-go@v0.0.0-20260522210424-ecfc5a8d5446); **no claim was rejected**. One citation was corrected: the `GlobalReassign` field is `syntax/options.go:28`; the Critic's `:45-47` is `LegacyFileOptions`. P0 changes only in the wording of deliverable 5 (filter parser, below); its acceptance criteria are unchanged.
  - §1.3, §1.3.1 rules 1 and 6, P10, §7, R12, §9, §10 (M1): Starlark has no decorator syntax (`syntax/scan.go` has no `@` token), so `concurrent` is a predeclared builtin written `request = concurrent(_request)` at module top level, with `GlobalReassign` off (standard Starlark; `def request` followed by `request = concurrent(request)` fails with `cannot reassign global ...`, `resolve/resolve.go:337-340`). Eligibility and the error text use the global binding name read after `Program.Init`, because `Function.Name()` returns the `def` name (`starlark/value.go:759`); a wrapper bound to a non-hook name fails the load as upstream's check does (`concurrent.py:13`). The syntax difference is a documented deviation (§1.3.1, §9, `docs/compat.md`), and `nonblocking.py` is ported with its thread branch in the new spelling.
  - §1.3.1 rule 7 (replaces the rev 5 "Depth > 1" rule), P10 (M2): `script.run` is a synchronous invocation of the script module only (`py:mitmproxy/addons/script.py:180-188`): `concurrent` handlers are rejected with upstream's error whatever the dispatch depth, the run stops at the first error because upstream wraps it in one `safecall`, and other addons' hooks are not involved. The "chain continues with the next addon" clause and the "other addons' `request` hooks still run" criterion were removed. No other synchronous flow-hook path exists upstream (the other sync sites fire `update`, `configure`, `load`, `running`, `done`), so the general depth > 1 clause was deleted rather than kept; `addon.Concurrent`'s depth rule for Go addons (§3.1 rule 4, P0) is unchanged. `script.run` builds no frozen second instance.
  - P3 deliverable 1 and the budget criterion, R11, §10 (M3): (a) the budget-test client keeps reading, returns connection-level window and advertises at least 132 MiB, and stalls streams only by withholding stream-level `WINDOW_UPDATE`s; (b) "consumed" means written to the downstream peer's socket for a streamed message, with bytes in the opposite endpoint's send buffer charged to Σ, and appended to the flow's body buffer for a buffered message (bounded by `stream_large_bodies`/`body_size_limit`, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/__init__.py:568-650`). This refines the lead's wording: counting only the downstream write would deadlock every buffered body larger than its stream window, including the existing 64 MiB upload/download criterion under default options; the budget test therefore sets `stream_large_bodies=1m` with explicit `content-length`s. (c) Growth: a stream's window doubles when half of it has been consumed, up to 16 MiB, from headroom only, taking the remainder when less than the increment is left; growth returns to the headroom when the stream closes. The heap explanation was rechecked against (b): with chunks passed from receive to send buffer by ownership, a stream holds up to two partly used 128 KiB chunks, not one, so the bound is 128 + 25 + 3.5 = 156.5 MiB and the criterion now says 160 MiB (was 144 MiB); the in-process test peers use fixed buffers so they do not count against it.
  - m1: `py:mitmproxy/websocket.py:161-175` (was `:164-175`); the stray `,;` in §1.3 removed.
  - m2, §1.3.1 rule 2: predeclared bindings implement `Freeze` as a no-op (shared Go objects, synchronised in Go), because `StringDict.Freeze` freezes every global value (`starlark/eval.go:177-181`); P10 criterion added.
  - m3, §1.3.1 rule 4: on a worker, `ctx.options` reads come from a copy-time snapshot, and `ctx.options` writes, `ctx.master.commands.call` and `ctx.log` go through `master.Do`; P10 criterion added under `-race`.
  - m4, P6 deliverable 4, §10.8: the TUN descriptor is handed over as a duplicate taken with `F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC` (a refinement of the lead's `unix.Dup`, which would leak the descriptor into child processes such as the `sudo`-launched redirector; `/dev/net/tun` is opened with `O_CLOEXEC` as `tun_linux.go:552` does) that the proxy sets non-blocking itself (the function returns before wrapping it on that error, `tun/tun_linux.go:638-641`), the original is always closed, and the `os.File` owns the duplicate (`:642`, `:482-499`); the bridge does not wait on `Events()` because only the netlink and hack listeners send `EventUp` (`:88,181`); `SIOCSIF*` run on an `AF_INET` datagram socket, as wireguard-go's `setMTU` does (`:231-245`).
  - m5, P3 DTLS criterion: the no-SNI case pins the listener to `127.0.0.1` with the mode spec's `@` listen suffix (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_specs.py:103-135`) and asserts `127.0.0.1` in the SANs (`tlsconfig.py:619`).
  - P0 deliverable 5 and §1.3 Filter regex row (lead instruction, the one allowed P0 change): the filter parser is described as an operator-precedence (shunting-yard) parser that mirrors pyparsing's iterative `infix_notation` (`py:mitmproxy/flowfilter.py:770-781`), verified by a golden file recorded from the pinned upstream for 418 expressions (`filter/testdata/parse_golden.json`, 418 records), replacing "recursive-descent", because upstream accepts and rejects inputs differently from a backtracking grammar (`a |`, `(a b)`, `(~q)` and a bare `!` are errors; `a&b` is one bare word); Python's `$`, which also matches before a final newline, is reproduced by a `regexp/syntax` rewrite in tail position and by regexp2 elsewhere.
  - §10: decisions updated; item 9 carries the Critic's MEDIUM-confidence note; item 10 added (netem ramp, mitigation: measure in the first P3 week, then compare after the ramp or raise the initial window).
  - §11 unchanged: the changes define behaviour that rev 5 left open and raise one test bound; none changes the expected performance, scalability, reliability or cost of the chosen architecture.
- 2026-10-05 rev 7 (plan date from `date`: 2026-10-05 07:09 JST): applied the Critic verification pass on rev 6 (verdict REVISE; N1, N2, N3 major, m-a, m-b, m-c minor, the "What's missing" list and two open questions) with the lead's decisions on each. Every claim was checked against the clone (mitmproxy@3368a0a), the module cache (go.starlark.net@v0.0.0-20260930220527-d7438c5a85ac) and the repository on main (`go:options/manager.go`, `go:master/master.go`, `go:filter/regex/regex.go`); **no claim was rejected**. Two Critic citations were adjusted: upstream's load-error path is `py:mitmproxy/addons/script.py:23-57` with the catches at `:51,54` and `script_error_handler` at `:60-71` (the Critic wrote `:38,53-55`), and `_iter_hooks` is `py:mitmproxy/addonmanager.py:243-262` with `traverse` at `:110-117` (the Critic's `:278` is the call site in `invoke_addon_sync`). Later-phase sections (P3 onward) get a focused Critic check of their own section at phase entry, because six whole-plan passes have each found defects only in newly written later-phase text.
  - §1.3.1 rules 2 and 4, P10 deliverables and criteria, R12, §10 (N1): `HasAttrs.Attr` and `HasSetField.SetField` receive no `*Thread` (`starlark/value.go:367,385`) and predeclared names are read from the function's own module (`starlark/interp.go:649-651`), so the frozen instance now gets its own predeclared object graph through the second `Program.Init` call (`starlark/eval.go:436-444`). The graph is in direct mode while that instance's top-level code runs on the loading goroutine under the dispatch lock, and is switched to worker mode under the lock after `StringDict.Freeze` and before the instance is handed to the dispatcher. In worker mode a `ctx.options` read loads the latest immutable snapshot from an `atomic.Pointer`, published at load and by an `options.Manager.Subscribe` subscriber after every change and again after a rollback (`go:options/manager.go:176-181` on main); writes, `ctx.master.commands.call` and `ctx.log` go through `master.Do` (`go:master/master.go:90` on main). Attribute access therefore needs to know neither the goroutine nor the worker. Rule 2 keeps the no-op `Freeze` with the reason restated: the objects hold no Starlark state of their own, and the Go state behind them is internally synchronised. The P10 criterion now reads "returns the old or the new value, and the `-race` run reports nothing"; the `o = ctx.options` criterion, which no longer tested `Freeze` because the main instance is never frozen, now writes through the frozen instance's `ctx.options` and its own `o`.
  - P3 deliverable 1, budget criterion, §7, R11 (N2, m-a, m-b): full chunks move to the send buffer by ownership; a partly filled tail moves only when the send buffer is empty and the client-facing stream and connection windows have credit, otherwise the receiver keeps appending to it. The check runs when DATA is appended and when the send buffer drains or a downstream window reopens, which is why small streamed bodies (server-sent events) are still forwarded as soon as they can be sent; a P3 criterion checks a 100-byte frame every 500 ms arriving within 100 ms. The lead's alternative (the send buffer copies the tail into its own last chunk) is allowed only when that chunk is also the send buffer's head, because otherwise a stream could hold three partly used chunks and the 25 MiB term would not hold; chunks are released when emptied. The budget test runs a second time with 1 KiB origin DATA frames under the same 160 MiB bound. m-a: the 128 MiB sentence now applies to streamed messages, and buffered bodies are bounded only by `body_size_limit`, unset by default (`py:mitmproxy/addons/proxyserver.py:176-178`). m-b: the client pins a 1 MiB stream `INITIAL_WINDOW_SIZE` and stalls each stream after at least 8 MiB; the criterion shows the arithmetic (7.5 MiB consumed reaches 16 MiB; 99 fully grown streams would need 1,485 MiB of headroom, so the 28 MiB is used up and Σ reaches 127 MiB).
  - P0 filter acceptance criteria (N3): the `$` claim in §1.3 and P0 deliverable 5 stays and is now tested: `~b "done$"` against `done\n` (matches) and `done\n\n` (does not), one `~u` case with a path set directly to `/done\n` through the `TReq` builder (and the two-newline negative), and one differential row per affected token, the ten regex tokens without MULTILINE whose subject can end with a newline (`py:mitmproxy/flowfilter.py:219`); `~src`/`~dst` are excluded (their subject is `host:port`, `:536,550`) and the five MULTILINE tokens are excluded (`:333,350,365,593,617`). `go:filter/regex/regex.go:29-31` on main still lists `$` as untranslated; the filter lane is implementing the `regexp/syntax` tail rewrite and the regexp2 route in the evaluation commit now. **This is the one P0 criterion added in rev 7, and it is already assigned to that lane.** P0 is otherwise unchanged.
  - §1.3.1 rule 7, P10 criteria (m-c): the eligibility walk runs on the module init of a script run by `script.run`, and a failure takes upstream's load-error path (`error in script <path>`, no event invoked, `script.py:51,54,60-71,178-179`); "other addons are not involved" now says that only the script module and the nested `addons` it defines are visited (`addonmanager.py:110-117,243-262,278`).
  - §1.3.1 rules 1, 4 and 7, P10, §9 (missing items and open questions): a direct Starlark call of a `concurrent` wrapper fails with `concurrent(<fn>) cannot be called directly; it runs only when invoked as a hook`, because the wrapper implements `starlark.Callable` (`starlark/value.go:182-186`; otherwise `invalid call of non-function (concurrent)`, `starlark/eval.go:1201`); upstream returns an unawaited coroutine (`concurrent.py:18-30`), recorded as a documented deviation. Hot reload and `remove` mark the frozen instance retired and fire `done` without waiting for in-flight workers, as upstream's `remove` waits for nothing (`addonmanager.py:206-220`); a finished worker's merge-back applies while its flow's hook chain still waits, as upstream keeps awaiting `run_in_executor` (`concurrent.py:30`), and is dropped only when the connection closed and the wait ended. The sync-context error prints the script path where upstream prints the module repr (`addonmanager.py:281`), recorded as a documented deviation. P10 gains criteria for the direct call, the reload during an in-flight handler, and the `script.run` load error.
  - §11 unchanged: the changes make the worker bindings buildable, close a peer-memory gap in the chunk hand-off and add tests; none changes the expected performance, scalability, reliability or cost of the chosen architecture.
